#### F006

**Template applicationId com.example.motocam cannot ship on Google Play, and the manifest hard-codes the INTERNAL_BROADCAST permission name that code derives from packageName, so a rename breaks the Stop relay and leaves its receiver behind an undeclared, claimable permission**

Severity: **high** · Category: android-platform · Location: `android/app/build.gradle.kts:75` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **owner decision**

Also affects: `android/app/build.gradle.kts:61`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:4`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:22`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:30`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:33`, `ios/Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:375`, `lib/features/map/map_source_config.dart:12`, `README.md:218` …

- **What is wrong:** applicationId and namespace are the template placeholder com.example.motocam (lines 61 and 75). The iOS bundle id (ios/Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:375) and the OSM user agent (lib/features/map/map_source_config.dart:12) use it too, while README.md:218 plans Play distribution. Play Console rejects 'com.example' package names; this is community-reported, and no official Google page was found. The custom signature permission is also declared with the literal name com.example.motocam.permission.INTERNAL_BROADCAST (AndroidManifest.xml:4, :22), but the code derives the name from packageName (MainActivity.kt:30-31, BackgroundRecordingService.kt:33-34).
- **Failure scenario:** Play Console refuses the first AAB upload. If only applicationId is then renamed, sendBroadcast and registerReceiver use '<newId>.permission.INTERNAL_BROADCAST', which no longer exists in the manifest, so the Stop relay never delivers. The new id is also a different app: existing installs must be uninstalled first, which deletes every recording in Android/data/com.example.motocam.
- **Evidence:** `applicationId = "com.example.motocam"` (build.gradle.kts:75); `<permission android:name="com.example.motocam.permission.INTERNAL_BROADCAST" .../>` (AndroidManifest.xml:3-5) vs `"$packageName.permission.INTERNAL_BROADCAST"` (MainActivity.kt:30-31); README.md:218 'For Play Store distribution in future...'. The restriction is community-reported, with the Play Console error saying the package name must not use 'com.example' because it is restricted (e.g. forum.ionicframework.com/t/error-deploying-app-to-google-play-store/90882, community.kodular.io/t/invalid-package-name-issue/81714). developer.android.com and docs.flutter.dev only say to replace the placeholder.
- **Verifier corrections:** The failure scenario says existing installs 'must be uninstalled first, which deletes every recording'. That is wrong: an app with a new applicationId installs alongside the old one. Old recordings in Android/data/com.example.motocam survive until the user uninstalls the old app, but the new app cannot see them. Also, the precheck (release-security-precheck.ps1:53) hard-codes the literal name and would fail a correct rename, so it has to change in the same commit. The method-channel names (com.example.motocam/pip and others) are only strings and need not change. | A new applicationId does not force an uninstall. It installs side by side as a separate app. The old app's recordings in Android…
- **Recommendation:** Now, without an owner decision: in AndroidManifest.xml, declare the permission and uses-permission as `${applicationId}.permission.INTERNAL_BROADCAST`, which Gradle substitutes as a manifest placeholder. Relax precheck.ps1:53 to match that placeholder. Later, once the owner picks the final reverse-DNS id, change applicationId in build.gradle.kts, the iOS PRODUCT_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER and userAgentPackageName together.
- **Owner decision:** Changing the applicationId and bundle id fixes the app's public identity and turns it into a different app for existing installs, whose recordings stay with the old package. Only the owner can choose the id and decide how to migrate existing riders. | Choosing the final applicationId/bundle id is an identity decision. Changing it makes a different app for existing installs, which orphans their re…
- **Fix note:** applicationId stays com.example.motocam (owner decision). Docs sync: docs/release.md:163-179 (Application ID) says Google Play rejects the com.example prefix and gives 7 migration steps (applicationId/namespace, the INTERNAL_BROADCAST literal, the precheck rule, the iOS bundle id, the OSM user agent, docs). It also states that the owner keeps the id for now.

#### F008

**Unused Mappls SDK credential blobs (app1770452729873i2092687013.a.conf/.a.olf and assets/mappls-conf.txt) are tracked in the public repo and packaged into every APK**

Severity: **high** · Category: security · Location: `android/app/src/main/assets/mappls-conf.txt:1` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/app1770452729873i2092687013.a.conf:1`, `android/app/app1770452729873i2092687013.a.olf:1`, `scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:45`, `android/app/src/main/assets/mappls-conf.txt:1`, `android/build/reports/problems/problems-report.html:1`

- **What is wrong:** The Mappls (MapmyIndia) SDK configuration files that Mappls issues per app to authenticate the SDK are committed to the public GitHub repo. They are android/app/app1770452729873i2092687013.a.conf (2032 B) and .a.olf (256 B), added in f0c1f08 on 2026-02-22, plus a byte-identical copy of the .a.conf at android/app/src/main/assets/mappls-conf.txt, added in 712d087. All three are still at the tips of origin/main, origin/Sarthak and origin/samaksh, and in every pushed tag from V0.1.1 to v0.1.13. The copy sits in the Android asset root, so every build packages it as assets/mappls-conf.txt. That includes the only APK users can download today (the v0.1.12 release asset) and current HEAD builds. The SDK and its only user (map_view.dart) were removed in 712d087, and nothing in the tree references the files, so they are pure liability. Severity is high rather than critical: the blobs are opaque and Mappls binds them to the package name and signing-certificate SHA-256, which raises the bar for reuse. They are still live authentication material for a paid third-party API, published to everyone. Deleting them from the working tree does not revoke them, because history, tags and released APKs ke…
- **Failure scenario:** Anyone can obtain the developer's Mappls credential configuration by cloning github.com/Sarthak195/MotoCam, or by downloading app-release.apk from the v0.1.12 release and running `unzip -p app-release.apk assets/mappls-conf.txt`. A repackaged app with package name com.example.motocam (or a patched SDK, if the package/cert binding is only enforced client-side) could then authenticate as the developer's Mappls project and use its quota or billing until the keys are revoked. Every future build also keeps shipping the dead file.
- **Evidence:** `git ls-files` lists android/app/app1770452729873i2092687013.a.conf, ...a.olf and android/app/src/main/assets/mappls-conf.txt (`file`: data, high entropy). The sha256 of the asset copy and of the .a.conf both begin c790e87f. `unzip -l` of the published v0.1.12 asset (sha256 539f2bb9..., downloadCount 3 per `gh release view v0.1.12`) shows: "2032 1981-01-01 01:01 assets/mappls-conf.txt". The HEAD debug APK build/app/outputs/flutter-apk/app-debug.apk has the same entry, and `unzip -p ... | sha256sum` gives c790e87f.... `git ls-tree -r` of origin/main, origin/Sarthak and origin/samaksh matches all 3 files in each. `git tag --contains f0c1f08` returns V0.1.1 ... v0.1.13, and all of them are on origin per `git ls-remote`. Mappls docs (pub.dev mappls_gl): "Download Configuration files for your app (associated with Package Name and Signing Certificate SHA-256)" and "To initialize and authentic…
- **Recommendation:** First, revoke or regenerate the credentials in the Mappls console. Then `git rm` the three files, add `*.a.conf`, `*.a.olf` and `android/app/src/main/assets/mappls-conf.txt` to .gitignore, and add an Assert to scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1 that fails if any *.a.conf/*.a.olf or mappls* file is tracked. A history purge (filter-repo and force-push of all branches and tags) and replacing the v0.1.12 release asset are optional follow-ups for the owner.
- **Owner decision:** Revoking the Mappls credentials, rewriting public git history and tags, and deleting or replacing published release assets are destructive account and release actions only the owner can take. | Only the owner can revoke the keys in the Mappls console. Purging them from history means rewriting history and force-pushing all branches and tags V0.1.1-v0.1.13 of a public repo, and replacing or deletin…
- **Fix note:** Mappls credential files git rm'd, with ignore and precheck rules; history not rewritten; the owner has rotated the keys (confirmed 2026-10-21)

#### F009

**Task removal mid-recording is unhandled (no onTaskRemoved/stopWithTask, START_STICKY for every intent): the FGS, 12 h wake lock and stale 'recording' notification outlive the engine while the current segment and the ride's telemetry are lost**

Severity: **high** · Category: android-platform · Location: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:66` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:59`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:362`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:58`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:28`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:66`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:59-63`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1864-1876`

- **What is wrong:** Nothing handles task removal or activity destruction during a recording: - BackgroundRecordingService has no onTaskRemoved and returns START_STICKY for every command (line 66). - The service element has no stopWithTask (AndroidManifest.xml:59-63). - MainActivity has no onDestroy handling. - The ride's telemetry JSON is written only by stopRideSessionAndPersist at a normal stop (telemetry_provider.dart:362). When the task is removed, the FlutterActivity and its engine are destroyed while CameraX is still streaming. In the reproduction the process then died from an upstream Flutter-engine crash (FlutterRenderer$ImageReaderSurfaceProducer.onImage after the engine detached). That crash is not in the app's code. The app's exposure is that nothing finalizes the recording or stops the service before the engine goes away, and START_STICKY brings the service back in a fresh process with its notification.
- **Failure scenario:** The rider swipes MotoCam's card away in Recents, or uses 'Clear all', while recording (the card is there when PiP is disabled or unsupported). The in-progress MP4 has no moov atom and will not play. Completed segments have no .telemetry.json, so the ride never appears in Ride History (rides_list_screen.dart:58-61 lists only telemetry files). A non-dismissible 'MotoCam recording in progress - Elapsed 00:00:49' notification stays up although nothing is recording.
- **Evidence:** Emulator (API 36), recording with the FGS and wake lock active. At 20:50:49 ran `am stack remove 9`; ActivityTaskManagerService.removeRootTask calls removeTask(killProcess=true, REMOVE_FROM_RECENTS), the same path Recents uses. Logcat then showed, in order: - `Recorder: Video source has transitioned to state: INACTIVE` and `CameraService: disconnect ... PID 8320`. - `20:50:54.074 FATAL EXCEPTION: main ... RuntimeException: Cannot execute operation because FlutterJNI is not attached to native. at ... FlutterRenderer$ImageReaderSurfaceProducer.onImage(FlutterRenderer.java:633)`. - `ActivityManager: Scheduling restart of crashed service com.example.motocam/.BackgroundRecordingService in 1000ms`, then `Start proc 8887 ... for service`. Afterwards: - Notification 4001 had `flags=ONGOING_EVENT|ONLY_ALERT_ONCE|NO_CLEAR|FOREGROUND_SERVICE` and the text 'Elapsed: 00:00:49 - screen can be off'. -…
- **Verifier corrections:** The 12 h wake lock did not outlive anything in the reproduced scenario: process death releases it, and the null-intent restart never re-acquires it. What survives is the sticky service record and the restored ongoing notification with a frozen elapsed time. A wake-lock leak is possible only in the variant where the activity or engine is destroyed but the process stays alive. The process crash itself is upstream Flutter engine behaviour, not app code. The finding already says this. | The title's claim that the 12 h wake lock outlives the engine is overstated for the reproduced path. The process crashed, and Android releases a process's wake locks when it dies. The sticky restart arrives with…
- **Recommendation:** In BackgroundRecordingService, override onTaskRemoved to release the wake lock, call stopForeground(STOP_FOREGROUND_REMOVE) and stopSelf. Return START_NOT_STICKY, and treat a null intent as a stop. In TelemetryProvider, write the .telemetry.json sidecar periodically with sessionComplete=false, at every segment roll and/or every N seconds, so an aborted ride still appears in history. Optionally recover orphaned cache segments on the next launch. Keeping the engine alive across task removal (FlutterEngineCache) is a larger follow-up.
- **Fix note:** START_NOT_STICKY, onTaskRemoved teardown, stop on destroy. Still open: swiping the app away from Recents ends the recording, because the activity and the Flutter engine are destroyed, and the segment in progress is not finalised (F004's lifecycle hold does not cover task removal). README.md, features.md, recording-pipeline.md and platform-integration.md say so.

#### F010

**Notification Stop is broadcast to a receiver registered only between onStart and onStop while the service unconditionally drops the FGS and wake lock, so Stop from the lock screen or screen-off is lost and Dart keeps 'recording'**

Severity: **high** · Category: lifecycle · Location: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:352` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:55-63`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:36-42`, `lib/core/services/background_recording_service.dart:40-47`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:341-360`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:352`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:330`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:55`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:45` …

- **What is wrong:** backgroundStopReceiver is registered in onStart and unregistered in onStop (330-358). BackgroundRecordingService handles the notification action by sending ACTION_STOP_REQUESTED and then, unconditionally, releasing the wake lock, calling stopForeground(REMOVE) and stopSelf (BackgroundRecordingService.kt:55-63). When the activity is stopped, nobody receives the broadcast. That covers screen off or lock screen, the app in the background without PiP (always the case on API 24-25), and PiP dismissed. Dart never gets onStopRequested: the camera session keeps 'recording', nothing is finalized or persisted, and isRecordingActive stays true, but the FGS and wake lock are gone. Every second, updateForegroundRecording then calls startService from the background (MainActivity.kt:76-84). Android 8+ rejects that with IllegalStateException, which surfaces as an unhandled PlatformException from the fire-and-forget call (recording_screen.dart:418-420). Once the app is visible again, an update re-creates the service as a plain started service that re-posts the ongoing notification (BackgroundRecordingService.kt:45-49).
- **Failure scenario:** MotoCam is not visible, e.g. the phone is on the lock screen after the display slept. The rider pulls down the shade and taps Stop. The notification disappears, so the rider believes the ride was stopped and saved. In fact nothing was stopped or persisted. The next time the app is opened it still shows REC, and a fresh 'recording in progress' notification appears. If the app is killed before the rider presses STOP in-app, the ride never gets a sidecar.
- **Evidence:** MainActivity.kt:330-350 `override fun onStart() { super.onStart(); ... registerReceiver(backgroundStopReceiver, filter, internalBroadcastPermission(), null, RECEIVER_NOT_EXPORTED) ...` and 352-357 `override fun onStop() { super.onStop(); try { unregisterReceiver(backgroundStopReceiver) } catch (_: IllegalArgumentException) {} }`; BackgroundRecordingService.kt:55-62 `ACTION_STOP_FROM_NOTIFICATION -> { sendBroadcast(Intent(ACTION_STOP_REQUESTED).setPackage(packageName), internalBroadcastPermission()); releaseWakeLockIfHeld(); stopForeground(STOP_FOREGROUND_REMOVE); stopSelf() }`; MainActivity.kt:82 `startService(serviceIntent)` for ACTION_UPDATE; recording_screen.dart:418-420 `camera.timerStream.listen((elapsed) { _backgroundRecordingService.updateForegroundRecording(elapsed: elapsed); });`.
- **Verifier corrections:** (1) Android 8+ throws IllegalStateException from background startService only after the app leaves its background-start allowance window, roughly a minute after it stops being foreground. Right after the Stop tap, the per-second startService calls will likely succeed and immediately re-post the ongoing notification. So 'every second ... rejects' overstates the timing. (2) When the Kotlin handler throws, Flutter's MethodChannel returns it as an error reply. The fire-and-forget Dart listener turns that into a logged unhandled async error, not a crash. (3) 'The camera session keeps recording' is uncertain. Once the camera-type FGS is gone and the activity is stopped, Android can cut off backgr…
- **Recommendation:** In MainActivity, register backgroundStopReceiver in onCreate (or configureFlutterEngine) and unregister it in onDestroy, so the stop request reaches Dart whenever the engine is alive. In BackgroundRecordingService's ACTION_STOP_FROM_NOTIFICATION branch, only send the broadcast and keep the FGS and wake lock; Dart's existing stopForegroundRecording at the end of _stopRecordingFlow then tears the service down.
- **Fix note:** receiver in onCreate/onDestroy, stop-relay keeps FGS until Dart stops it; Dart stops FGS after save

#### F011

**Kotlin Gradle plugin pinned at 2.1.0 is below current Flutter stable's (3.47) KGP error floor of 2.2.20, so the Android build fails on current stable**

Severity: **high** · Category: build-ci · Location: `android/settings.gradle:22` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/settings.gradle:21`, `android/gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.properties:5`, `README.md:39`, `README.md:120`, `.metadata:7`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:16`

- **What is wrong:** android/settings.gradle:22 pins `id "org.jetbrains.kotlin.android" version "2.1.0"`. Only the Flutter version the project was last migrated with accepts it silently: 3.41.2 (.metadata:7), whose warnKGPVersion is 2.1.0 with a strict `<`. Flutter 3.44.x warns (warnKGP 2.2.20, errorKGP 2.0.0); its warnAGP 8.11.1 and warnGradle 8.14.0 are also strict `<`, so that KGP warning is the only message there. Flutter 3.47.0 (2026-08-12) raised errorKGPVersion to 2.2.20, and FlutterPlugin.kt rethrows that check, so applying dev.flutter.flutter-gradle-plugin fails. The current stable is 3.47.7 (2026-10-08). AGP 8.11.1 (settings.gradle:21) now sits exactly on 3.47's errorAGPVersion (8.11.1), and Gradle 8.14.4 (gradle-wrapper.properties:5) sits just above errorGradleVersion (8.14.0). Both already print 'will soon be dropped' warnings, so the next floor bump breaks them too. Nothing pins the Flutter version for contributors: README.md:39 says 'Flutter 3.0+', pubspec has no `flutter:` bound, and CI uses unpinned stable without any Android build. So the breakage is invisible to the maintainers.
- **Failure scenario:** A contributor installs today's stable Flutter (3.47.7), which README.md:39/120 permits, clones the public repo and runs `flutter run` or `flutter build apk`. Gradle stops while applying the Flutter plugin with 'Your project's Kotlin version (2.1.0) is lower than Flutter's minimum supported version of 2.2.20', so no Android build is possible without editing settings.gradle or passing --android-skip-build-dependency-validation. The maintainer hits the same wall on the first Flutter upgrade past 3.44, for example one done to pick up the camera_android_camerax bitrate fix.
- **Evidence:** Reproduced on a `git archive HEAD` copy with Flutter 3.47.7 / Dart 3.13.5 (log: C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/repro/r_buildapk.log): "Build file '...\android\app\build.gradle.kts' line: 3 * What went wrong: An exception occurred applying plugin request [id: 'dev.flutter.flutter-gradle-plugin'] > Failed to apply plugin 'dev.flutter.flutter-gradle-plugin'. > Error: Your project's Kotlin version (2.1.0) is lower than Flutter's minimum supported version of 2.2.20. ... BUILD FAILED in 5m 49s". The same run warned: "Flutter support for your project's Gradle version (8.14.4) will soon be dropped ... at least 9.1.0" and "... Android Gradle Plugin version 8.11.1 will soon be dropped ... at least Android Gradle Plugin version 9.0.1". With only settings.gradle:22 changed to 2.2.20, the same copy built:…
- **Verifier corrections:** Only 2.2.20 was verified to build with the unchanged `kotlinOptions { jvmTarget = "17" }` block. The recommended 2.3.20 was not tested, and newer KGP releases continue deprecating `kotlinOptions` in favour of `kotlin { compilerOptions { ... } }`. I verified only the 3.47.7 floors; the 3.44.x floors in the description were not rechecked. | Pick 2.2.20, the version verified on 3.47.7, over 2.3.20. A KGP 2.3.x build has not been tested against AGP 8.11.1 / Gradle 8.14.4 or Flutter 3.41.2 in this tree.
- **Recommendation:** Change android/settings.gradle:22 to `id "org.jetbrains.kotlin.android" version "2.2.20" apply false`. Add `flutter: ">=3.41.2"` under `environment:` in pubspec.yaml and correct the README toolchain lines. Schedule the AGP 9.0.1+/Gradle 9.1+ move as separate work.
- **Fix note:** KGP 2.1.0 -> 2.2.20

#### F012

**ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION ('Allow all the time') is requested at every launch and every START with no prominent disclosure, although the location-typed foreground service already covers screen-off GPS**

Severity: **high** · Category: privacy · Location: `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:97` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:14`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:97`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:368`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:43`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:63`, `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:97`

- **What is wrong:** As soon as when-in-use location is granted, ensureRequiredPermissions() requests Permission.locationAlways (ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION, AndroidManifest.xml:14), labelled 'Background location (screen-off tracking)'. The request runs from _bootstrapRuntimePrerequisites at every app launch and again on every START. No explanation is shown first; no disclosure UI exists anywhere in lib/. Screen-off recording does not need this permission. START launches BackgroundRecordingService through startForeground() while the app is visible (recording_screen.dart:414-416 -> BackgroundRecordingService.kt:43), and the service is declared foregroundServiceType="camera|microphone|location" (AndroidManifest.xml:63). Android treats location access by an app running a foreground service as foreground access, and that access continues after Home or screen-off. The permission therefore adds nothing for recording. It does give the app round-the-clock location rights, and because the location stream is never stopped (see the related finding), those rights are actually exercised. Google Play restricts this permission to apps that need it for core features, and requires a prominent disclosure and a declarati…
- **Failure scenario:** A rider grants 'While using the app'. MotoCam immediately requests background location (on Android 11+ this sends the rider to the system settings page) and repeats the request at every launch and START until the OS records the request as permanently denied. A rider who picks 'Allow all the time' gives the app unrestricted background location that recording never needed. A Play submission (with a non-placeholder applicationId) faces the background-location declaration review. That review rejects apps whose feature is covered by a while-in-use foreground service, and apps without a prominent in-app disclosure.
- **Evidence:** app_permission_service.dart:97-103: `if (hasLocationWhenInUse) { await ensure(Permission.locationAlways, 'Background location (screen-off tracking)', required: false); }`. ensure() calls `permission.request()` when `status.isDenied || status.isRestricted || status.isLimited` (:57-59). It is called at launch (recording_screen.dart:97) and on START (recording_screen.dart:368). AndroidManifest.xml:14 declares ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION and :63 declares android:foregroundServiceType="camera|microphone|location". BackgroundRecordingService.kt:43 calls `startForeground(NOTIFICATION_ID, buildNotification(elapsedMs))`. Android docs (location permissions) count as foreground: "Your app is running a foreground service ... Your app retains access when it's placed in the background, such as when the user presses the Home button on their device or turns their device's display off"; and "The Google P…
- **Verifier corrections:** The startForeground call is at BackgroundRecordingService.kt:122/128, not :43; line 43 is a KDoc comment for grantedForegroundServiceTypes. The location FGS type is applied dynamically at runtime, only when fine or coarse location is granted. The audit also re-runs from the Request Again and Open Settings paths (recording_screen.dart:161, :170). | Play rejection is only reachable once the placeholder applicationId com.example.motocam is replaced, because Play does not accept com.example packages. The working tree has uncommitted changes to the Android sources (BackgroundRecordingService.kt and MainActivity.kt); the start-up line cited from BackgroundRecordingService.kt:43 refers to HEAD.
- **Recommendation:** Delete the ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION <uses-permission> from android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml. Remove the locationAlways ensure() block (lines 97-103) from lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart and drop the 'background location' wording from its doc comment. Keep the location-typed FGS, started from the foreground on START, as the only screen-off GPS mechanism.
- **Fix note:** ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION removed from manifest and Dart

#### F013

**A startVideoRecording failure during a segment roll is only logged: state stays 'recording' (REC pill, timer, notification) while nothing records, every later roll fails, and the just-stopped segment is never registered**

Severity: **high** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1018` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1050-1079`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1468-1484`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:810-848`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1040`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1012-1029`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1471-1480`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1511-1544`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:300-307` …

- **What is wrong:** _rollSegmentIfNeeded registers the stopped file (1012) only after startVideoRecording succeeds (1016-1029). If the restart at 1018 throws, the catch just logs and re-arms the timer (1040-1044). _state stays 'recording', no listener is notified, and the REC badge, elapsed clock and FGS notification keep running. The controller's isRecordingVideo is now false, so every later roll throws CameraException('No video is recording') at 1012 (camera-0.12.0 camera_controller.dart:623-629) and re-arms again, indefinitely. recoverRecordingPipeline fails the same way (1063, 1073-1075). The stopped segment stays in the plugin's cache directory and is never moved, listed or referenced. At STOP, the race branch (1474-1480) substitutes _sessionSegmentPaths.last. If nothing was registered yet, it substitutes _currentVideoPath, which startRecording never resets (861-873), so the previous ride's last video can be registered into this ride (1493-1494). The provider has no controller error listener at all.
- **Failure scenario:** Storage is nearly full (there is no free-space check; see the storage finding), or another app takes the camera while MotoCam is in PiP. At the next segment boundary the CameraX restart fails, for example in getTempFilePath/File.createTempFile in the cache dir (SystemServicesManager.java:56-58) or in the bind, and startVideoRecording throws. For the rest of the ride the screen shows REC and a running clock while nothing is recorded. The last good segment is stranded in the cache, which the OS can clear, and the sidecar written at STOP leaves it out. If the failure happened at the first roll, the sidecar can point at the previous ride's last video.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1012 `final file = await _controller!.stopVideoRecording();`, 1016-1019 `if (!_isStoppingRecording) { ... await _controller!.startVideoRecording(); _armSegmentTimer();`, 1026 `await _registerCompletedSegment(file.path, deferMaintenance: true);`, 1040-1044 `} catch (e) { _log('Error while rolling segment: $e'); if (!_isStoppingRecording && isRecording) { _armSegmentTimer(); } }`; camera_controller.dart:625-629 `if (!value.isRecordingVideo) { throw CameraException('No video is recording', ...`; camera_provider.dart:1476-1480 `if (_sessionSegmentPaths.isNotEmpty) { cacheVideoPath = _sessionSegmentPaths.last; } else { cacheVideoPath = _currentVideoPath; }`.
- **Verifier corrections:** On resume, the only user-visible signal is recording_screen.dart:300-307, a generic 'If video appears frozen, stop and restart recording' snackbar shown when recoverRecordingPipeline returns false. It appears only after the app was backgrounded past the threshold, never during a ride in the foreground. | 'Silent' needs one qualification. In the normal foreground case nothing reaches the UI. If the app was backgrounded and resumed, recording_screen.dart:300-307 shows a one-off snackbar ('Recording resumed from background. If video appears frozen, stop and restart recording.') when recoverRecordingPipeline fails. The failure also has a second mechanism: insufficient storage and camera evictio…
- **Recommendation:** In _rollSegmentIfNeeded, register (persist) the stopped file before attempting the restart, or in its own try block. Wrap startVideoRecording in a timeout, retry once, and on failure set an explicit error state, call notifyListeners and stop the session so the screen and the FGS notification show an error. Set _currentVideoPath = null in startRecording.
- **Fix note:** roll failures enter a fault (lastRecordingError) shown by the screen

#### F014

**Retention is per-session only: earlier rides' footage and GPS trails are never aged out or deletable in-app and free space is never checked, so storage grows without bound and running out of space fails silently**

Severity: **high** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1117` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1180-1256`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:54-77`, `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:72-95`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:873-880`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1193`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:862`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1117`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1209` …

- **What is wrong:** Loop retention trims only the current session's list (1117-1138). The directory-wide sweep runs only in the over-locked case (1193-1198; see the retention-sweep finding). So every ride keeps up to _maxRollingSegments segments indefinitely, and segments orphaned by abnormal ride ends are never reclaimed. Nothing else in the app deletes recordings: grep for '.delete(' and 'deleteSync(' in lib/ outside the provider finds only the telemetry temp file and the write probe. Ride History has no delete action. There is no free-space check in Dart or Kotlin, before START or during a ride (no StatFs/getFreeSpace/usableSpace). History's unlock action promises 'Clip unlocked and moved to normal loop policy' (rides_list_screen.dart:269), but no loop policy ever applies to past rides. When storage fills, the only symptoms are the silent states described in the roll-restart finding. A failed sidecar write (integrity_utils.dart:82) escapes _stopRecordingFlow with no message to the rider.
- **Failure scenario:** Default settings (1080p, 5-min segments, loop 24) on a daily commute: every ride adds several GB that is never removed. After some weeks the device is full. The next roll's restart or copy fails and the app keeps showing REC. The rider cannot reclaim space in the app; Android/data is not browsable by normal file managers on Android 11+. The only way out is clearing app data, which deletes every ride.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1117 `while (_sessionSegmentPaths.length > _maxRollingSegments) {` (session-scoped list) and 1193 `if (_sessionSegmentPaths.length <= _maxRollingSegments) { ... return; }`; grep for '.delete(' / 'deleteSync(' in lib/ outside camera_provider.dart -> only integrity_utils.dart:86, 92 and recording_screen.dart:752; grep for 'freeSpace|getFreeSpace|StatFs|availableBytes|usableSpace' in lib/ and android/app/src/main -> no matches; rides_list_screen.dart:269 `content: Text('Clip unlocked and moved to normal loop policy')`.
- **Verifier corrections:** The fix must be coordinated with F005: the only existing cross-ride deletion is the unsafe sweep, which should be removed rather than broadened. | Recordings are in app-specific external storage (getExternalStorageDirectory, camera_provider.dart:663), so uninstalling the app also removes them. The growth matters while the app stays installed. The unlock snackbar's 'normal loop policy' never applies to past rides.
- **Recommendation:** Two non-destructive parts need no owner decision. First, add a free-space check (via a MethodChannel to StatFs, or path_provider plus a small native call) before START and at each roll, refusing to start or stopping with a visible error below a threshold. Second, add a 'Delete ride' action to Ride History that removes the ride's segments and its sidecar. Automatic age-out of old unlocked rides under a storage budget, in whole rides and never locked ones, is a separate policy change.
- **Owner decision:** Automatically ageing out earlier rides deletes user recordings, so the owner must choose the retention policy (byte budget or free-space threshold, and whether to auto-delete or only warn). The in-app delete action and the free-space check do not need that decision. | Automatically deleting a rider's past unlocked rides under a storage budget destroys user data. The owner must choose that policy…
- **Fix note:** free-space checks + native StatFs + in-app delete; global storage budget is owner-only

#### F015

**START is enabled while the previous stop is still finalizing and saving (the button checks only camera.isRecording, not _isStoppingRecording), so the new start and the old stop wipe each other's segment list, samples and session ID**

Severity: **high** · Category: concurrency · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1486` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1716-1734`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:828-898`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:851-873`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:362-460`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:456`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:376-377`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:437-454`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:834-838` …

- **What is wrong:** stopRecording sets _state = stopped at 1486 but only notifies at 1517. In between it registers the final segment (1494), queues exports and runs two retention/prune passes (1505-1515). On Android, registering is typically a full file copy: the CameraX temp file is in getCacheDir() (SystemServicesManager.java:56-58) while recordings live under getExternalStorageDirectory(), a different mount, so rename is expected to fail and fall back to copy (771-788). The main button sits in a Consumer3 that also rebuilds on every TelemetryProvider notification, about 10 per second (recording_screen.dart:1687). So it shows 'START RECORDING' within ~100 ms and decides start vs. stop from camera.isRecording alone (1721). The screen's _isStoppingRecording flag is checked only on the stop path (834-836). startRecording checks only init state (851-859). It resets the provider's _isStoppingRecording (871) and clears _sessionSegmentPaths, _sessionSegmentTimeline, _lockedSegmentPaths, _pendingGalleryExports and _lastSegmentEndMs (862-867) while the old stop is still writing to them. RecordingScreen reads those lists only after stopRecording returns (866-878). After the camera stop, _stopRecordingFlow st…
- **Failure scenario:** The rider taps STOP after a one-hour ride. Seeing START, they tap it to begin the next ride (or simply double-tap STOP) while the final segment is still being copied. The new startRecording clears the lists, and the old stop then registers its final segment into the new session. The old ride's sidecar is written with only that one segment, no locks, and a bogus timeline. The ride's other segments become unreferenced files that are invisible in history, and its locked clips are never exported. If the new ride's startRideSession runs before the old persist, the old sidecar gets the new ride's samples and _isRideActive is set to false. The new ride then records video with no telemetry, and at its STOP stopRideSessionAndPersist returns null (telemetry_provider.dart:369-374): no sidecar, so th…
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1486-1487 `_state = RecordingState.stopped; _recordingStartTime = null;` ... 1517 `notifyListeners();`; 862-872 `_sessionSegmentPaths.clear(); _sessionSegmentTimeline.clear(); _lockedSegmentPaths.clear(); _pendingGalleryExports.clear(); _pendingIncidentSegmentLocks = 0; _lastSegmentEndMs = 0; ... _isStoppingRecording = false; _recordingStartTime = DateTime.now();`; recording_screen.dart:1687 `Consumer3<CameraProvider, TelemetryProvider, AppSettingsProvider>(`, 1721-1732 `if (camera.isRecording) { await _stopRecordingFlow(...); } else { await _startRecordingFlow(...); }`, 866 `final sessionSegmentPaths = camera.lastSessionSegmentPaths;`; telemetry_provider.dart:318 (startRideSession) `_activeRideSamples.clear();`, 376 `_isRideActive = false;`, 456-457 `_activeRideSamples.clear(); _rideSessionId = null;`.
- **Verifier corrections:** The samples list is copied into telemetryPayloadCore before the long _buildIntegrityMetadata await. Two cases follow. If the new startRideSession runs during the hashing window, the old sidecar keeps its own samples, but the new ride loses the samples it had collected and its session ID; _isRideActive stays true, so it is not left with no telemetry. The worse outcome (old sidecar gets the new ride's samples, and the new ride ends with no sidecar) needs the new startRideSession to land before the old flow enters stopRideSessionAndPersist, i.e. during camera.stopRecording. _startRecordingFlow's permission audit, location check and (conditional) preflight sheet narrow that window but do not cl…
- **Recommendation:** In recording_screen.dart, disable the main button with a 'Saving…' label while the screen's _isStoppingRecording is true, calling setState when it changes. In CameraProvider.startRecording, return false while _isStoppingRecording is set, and notify at the start of stop. In TelemetryProvider.stopRideSessionAndPersist, capture the session ID at entry and clear _activeRideSamples and _rideSessionId only if they still belong to that session.
- **Fix note:** isStarting/isStoppingRecording guards; button disabled while saving

#### F017

**Ride JSON stores absolute iOS data-container paths (videoPath, segmentPaths, storageDirectory, segmentHashes keys), so past rides become unplayable and are flagged when the container path changes**

Severity: **high** · Category: ios-platform · Location: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:261` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:421`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:665`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:198`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:489`

- **What is wrong:** On iOS, recordings live in getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()/MotoCam Recordings (camera_provider.dart:665-666), that is /var/mobile/Containers/Data/Application/<UUID>/Documents. Writer side: - TelemetryProvider writes the absolute storageDirectory, videoPath and segmentPaths into the ride JSON (telemetry_provider.dart:421-425). - It keys the integrity segmentHashes by absolute path (:659). Reader side, RideRecord.fromTelemetryFile: 1. It reads videoPath and segmentPaths verbatim (ride_record.dart:261-275). 2. It adds the JSON's own, now stale, storageDirectory as a trusted root (:489-491). The stale paths are therefore allowed, not quarantined. 3. declaredSegmentPaths is non-empty, so the same-directory recovery (_recoverVideoPathFromTelemetryFile) is skipped (:282-284). Result: - getRecordings still finds the JSON under the new Documents path (rides_list_screen.dart:56-66), so the ride is listed. - Playback finds every path missing (ride_playback_screen.dart:160-165) and stops at `_segmentPaths.isEmpty` (:198-206). - verifySegmentIntegrity adds missing-segment-file issues (ride_record.dart:712), and the screen shows a 'Security warning' (ride_playback_screen.dart:76-112). Androi…
- **Failure scenario:** The app's data-container path changes. This happens on every simulator reinstall or run, where the container GUID rotates. On devices it has been reported after app updates and reinstalls. Afterwards, Ride History still lists every ride. Opening one shows 'Some ride segments are unavailable (N missing). Playback is running in incomplete mode.' and no video, followed by 'Security warning: this ride shows N integrity issues'. Every incident recording therefore appears both lost and tampered with, although the files are intact in the new Documents directory. There is no in-app recovery.
- **Evidence:** - telemetry_provider.dart:421 `'storageDirectory': canonicalStorageDirectory,`. :423 `'videoPath': canonicalVideoPath,`. :425 `'segmentPaths': resolvedSegmentPaths,`. :659 `segmentHashes[segmentPath] = hash;`. - ride_record.dart:489-491: `final storageDirectory = _readOptionalString(json, 'storageDirectory'); if (storageDirectory != null) { roots.add(storageDirectory); }`. - ride_record.dart:282: `if (declaredSegmentPaths.isEmpty) {`. Recovery runs only on this branch. - ride_playback_screen.dart:164: `if (!await file.exists()) { missingSegmentsCount++; continue; }`. - Apple DTS, developer.apple.com/forums/thread/766702: 'let's say an app stores its documents in the Documents directory but keeps an index of them in a separate file. If that index uses absolute paths to refer to the documents, then a change to the app's container's path will make it seems like all the documents have disap…
- **Verifier corrections:** segmentHashes is part of the integrity envelope and its keys are absolute paths. A key-scheme change must keep verifying existing rides, which have absolute keys; otherwise every existing ride fails integrity.
- **Recommendation:** In ride_record.dart, when a declared absolute segment or video path does not exist, retry File(join(telemetryFile.parent.path, basename(p))). Accept it only if basename(p) is a plain file name, and look up segmentHashes by the original absolute key. For new rides, also write relative 'segmentFileNames' alongside the existing fields in telemetry_provider.dart.
- **Fix note:** reader relocates stale absolute segment paths

#### F018

**_seekGlobal has no in-flight guard, so overlapping cross-segment seeks (+/-10 s buttons, scrub commits) each call _loadSegment and orphan a still-playing VideoPlayerController**

Severity: **high** · Category: concurrency · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:768` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:591`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:641-645`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:682-710`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:853-866`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1613-1649`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1669-1681`

- **What is wrong:** _seekGlobal (768-824) has no in-flight guard. The -10/+10 IconButtons (1613-1623, 1639-1649) and _commitScrub (853-866) call it even while _isSwitchingSegment or _isSeekingAcrossSegments is set. The flag set at 792 is never checked by the callers or by _seekGlobal. _activeSegmentIndex changes only inside _loadSegment's setState, after the new controller has initialized (628-635). So two requests inside one init window both take the cross-segment branch and both call _loadSegment. Each _loadSegment captures previous = _controller on entry (591) and, after installing its own controller, disposes only that previous (641-645). The controller installed by the first load is then replaced by the second and is never disposed. _seekGlobal calls _controller?.play() after each load (818-820), so both controllers play. dispose() disposes only the current _controller (1669-1681). video_player registers every initialized controller as a WidgetsBindingObserver (video_player-2.11.1/lib/video_player.dart:540-545, 1043-1045), so the orphan stays reachable and its native player is never released. The same race happens when a +/-10 press or a slider release lands during auto-advance (682-710).
- **Failure scenario:** The rider plays a multi-segment ride and double-taps 'forward 10' in the last 10 s of a segment, or presses it while a segment auto-advances. Two players are created for the next segment and both play, so the audio is doubled. Pause stops only one of them. After the rider leaves the screen, the orphan keeps playing audio and holds a native decoder until the process dies. Repeated orphans can exhaust hardware decoders, so later segment loads fail.
- **Evidence:** Reproduced by running the real RidePlaybackScreen against a fake VideoPlayerPlatform with a 300 ms init (scratch C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/corrhist/test/playback_race_test.dart, P1): 'after double tap: ... notDisposed=[4:seg1.mp4, 5:seg1.mp4] playing=[4:seg1.mp4, 5:seg1.mp4]'; 'after pause: ... playing=[4:seg1.mp4]'; 'after leaving screen: ... notDisposed=[4:seg1.mp4] playing=[4:seg1.mp4]'. Control P2 (single tap): 'after leaving screen: ... notDisposed=[] playing=[]'. Code: 591 'final previous = _controller;' ... 644 'unawaited(_stopAndDisposeController(previous));'; 1640-1646 'onPressed: () async { final target = position + const Duration(seconds: 10); await _seekGlobal(target, shouldResume: value.isPlaying); }'.
- **Verifier corrections:** _onControllerTick does not trigger the auto-advance variant of the race, because it checks !_isSwitchingSegment && !_isSeekingAcrossSegments. The race comes from a +/-10 press or a scrub commit landing while _advanceToNextSegment or another _seekGlobal is still awaiting _loadSegment, because those entry points do not check the flags. Only onChangeStart (:1423-1426) checks them. | Minor: 'previous' (A) is disposed twice. That is harmless because VideoPlayerController.dispose returns early when _isDisposed. The leak is the intermediate controller B only. The orphan's _onControllerTick listener also stays attached, but it reads _controller rather than B, so it does not drive auto-advance.
- **Recommendation:** In ride_playback_screen.dart, add an int _loadGeneration that _loadSegment increments on entry. After each await, if the generation has changed or _isDisposing is set, dispose the newly created controller and return false. At swap time, read the controller being replaced from _controller (not the one captured on entry) and dispose that. In _seekGlobal, return early (or store the latest pending target) while _isSwitchingSegment || _isSeekingAcrossSegments, and apply the same guard in _commitScrub.
- **Fix note:** segment loads serialised by generation

#### F019

**On iOS nothing disables the idle timer while recording, so Auto-Lock interrupts the capture session and silently ends recording, which the app later reports as 'auto-recovered'**

Severity: **high** · Category: ios-platform · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:414` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **deferred**

Also affects: `lib/core/services/background_recording_service.dart:53`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:237`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:294`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1050`, `ios/Runner/AppDelegate.swift:5`

- **What is wrong:** Android screen-off recording relies on BackgroundRecordingService, a foreground service plus a partial wake lock. On iOS the only recording-start hook, _backgroundRecordingService.startForegroundRecording (recording_screen.dart:413-416), returns immediately (background_recording_service.dart:52-55). There is no iOS substitute: - no UIApplication.isIdleTimerDisabled - no wakelock plugin in pubspec.yaml - no native iOS code beyond the template AppDelegate iOS forbids camera use in the background. When the idle timer locks an untouched, handlebar-mounted phone, the AVCaptureSession is interrupted. camera_avfoundation then only sets isRecordingDisconnected (DefaultCamera.swift:256-258), and it appends samples only while both capture sessions run (:1269-1270). The Dart isolate keeps running in the background because of the location background mode (see the background-GPS finding), so the segment timer keeps rolling. The lifecycle handler only timestamps the backgrounding (recording_screen.dart:237-245). On return after 8 s or more (:30-31), it calls recoverRecordingPipeline and shows 'Video feed may have stalled while screen was off. Recording was auto-recovered after Ns in background.…
- **Failure scenario:** A rider mounts an iPhone, starts recording and rides without touching the screen. Auto-Lock is anywhere from 30 s to 5 min unless set to Never, and Low Power Mode fixes it at 30 s. Blackout mode keeps the screen black but still gets no touches, so it does not help. When Auto-Lock fires, MotoCam is backgrounded and capture stops. Nothing is recorded until the rider unlocks the phone, including any crash in that window. On unlock the snackbar says recording was 'auto-recovered', so the rider believes the ride footage is complete.
- **Evidence:** - `grep -rni "wakelock|isIdleTimerDisabled|keepScreenOn|KEEP_SCREEN"` over *.dart, *.kt, *.swift, *.yaml and *.plist matches only android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:27-124 (PARTIAL_WAKE_LOCK). - background_recording_service.dart:52-55: `Future<void> startForegroundRecording({required Duration elapsed}) async { if (!Platform.isAndroid) { return; }` - Apple AVCaptureSession.InterruptionReason.videoDeviceNotAvailableInBackground: 'Camera usage is prohibited while in the background.' - Apple UIApplication.isIdleTimerDisabled: 'The default value of this property is false. When most apps have no touches as user input for a short period, the system puts the device into a "sleep" state ... The only apps that should disable the idle timer are mapping apps, games, or programs where the app needs to continue displaying content when user interaction is min…
- **Verifier corrections:** 'Silently ends recording' overstates it slightly. Capture is interrupted, isRecording stays true, and the segment timer keeps rolling, so the result is an unrecorded gap rather than a stopped recording.
- **Recommendation:** Add wakelock_plus to pubspec.yaml and call WakelockPlus.enable() when recording starts and disable() on stop and dispose. Alternatively, have BackgroundRecordingService on iOS call a small channel that sets UIApplication.shared.isIdleTimerDisabled. Also change the resume snackbar on iOS to report the background gap honestly instead of 'auto-recovered'.
- **Fix note:** deferred by owner (decision 5)

#### F020

**Incident auto-lock defers camera.markIncident to addPostFrameCallback, so incidents detected while hidden/paused (screen off) are not locked until frames resume and then all apply to the wrong segments**

Severity: **high** · Category: lifecycle · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:915` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:84-89`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:900-927`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1300-1322`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:700-711`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:915`

- **What is wrong:** _handleIncidentAutoLock marks the incident as handled at once (_lastHandledIncidentAt = detectedAt, 914) but defers camera.markIncident to WidgetsBinding.instance.addPostFrameCallback (915-926). Flutter disables frames in hidden and paused (C:/Users/Sarthak/flutter/packages/flutter/lib/src/scheduler/binding.dart:423-426), scheduleFrame returns early when frames are disabled (:947), and addPostFrameCallback 'does *not* request a new frame' (:793). Recording continues in the background under the camera/location foreground service, which holds a partial wake lock (BackgroundRecordingService.kt:40-43). TelemetryProvider keeps running _detectCrash on accelerometer events, but the lock runs only at the next rendered frame. If recording is stopped first, for example from the notification Stop, which runs _stopRecordingFlow in the background, the callback later returns at 916-918 because !camera.isRecording, and the incident is never locked. If the app is resumed while still recording, markIncident(protectPastSegments: 2) locks the two newest segments at resume time (camera_provider.dart:1305-1313), not the segments around the crash.
- **Failure scenario:** The rider records with the screen off or with a navigation app in the foreground, which is normal dashcam use. A crash is detected but no segment is locked. The per-session loop keeps deleting the oldest unlocked segments (camera_provider.dart:1117-1138); with small loop settings that takes minutes. The incident segments are not exported to the gallery at stop, because only locked segments are (camera_provider.dart:1505-1513), and the saved ride has no lock markers. If the rider opens the app 20 minutes later, 'Incident detected. Segments locked.' appears, but the locks land on the latest segments.
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:914-923: '_lastHandledIncidentAt = detectedAt; WidgetsBinding.instance.addPostFrameCallback((_) { if (!mounted || !camera.isRecording) { return; } camera.markIncident(protectPastSegments: 2, ...'. Flutter 3.41 scheduler/binding.dart:423-426: 'case AppLifecycleState.hidden: case AppLifecycleState.paused: case AppLifecycleState.detached: _setFramesEnabledState(false);'; :947 'if (_hasScheduledFrame || !framesEnabled) { return; }'; :793 'This method does *not* request a new frame.'
- **Verifier corrections:** The 'navigation app in the foreground' scenario is mostly unaffected. While recording, MainActivity.onUserLeaveHint/onPause calls maybeEnterRecordingPip (MainActivity.kt:300-327). PiP is lifecycle 'inactive', and frames stay enabled in that state. The failure needs the activity to be stopped: screen off with the power button, PiP dismissed, PiP disabled by the user or not supported (API < 26). With the default 24 x 5 min loop the incident segment usually survives on disk. It is still unlocked, can be overwritten by the loop, and is not exported to the gallery at stop. | The cited line numbers in scheduler/binding.dart are approximately correct (the switch is at 418-421). The bug does not de…
- **Recommendation:** In recording_screen.dart _handleIncidentAutoLock, call camera.markIncident(...) synchronously and keep addPostFrameCallback (or a plain call guarded by mounted) only for _showInAppNotice. A better option is to wire TelemetryProvider incident events to CameraProvider outside the widget tree, for example a listener in main/app setup, so the lock does not depend on the screen.
- **Fix note:** incident lock runs synchronously even when hidden

#### F021

**START has no in-flight guard: a double tap runs two start flows, and the second startRecording treats 'already started' as an unsupported preset and swaps out and disposes the recording controller**

Severity: **high** · Category: concurrency · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1717` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:362-430`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:851-873`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:910-972`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:400-421`

- **What is wrong:** The main button stays enabled for the whole start flow: permission audit, location check, preflight and camera start. When every check passes, no modal appears (539-544), and _startRecordingFlow (362-430) has no in-flight flag. CameraProvider.startRecording (851-873) checks only that the camera is initialized, not isRecording or a start already in progress, and it resets session lists and start time before starting. Verified path: when the second call reaches the controller after the first start has completed, startVideoRecording throws 'A video recording is already started.' (camera-0.12.0 camera_controller.dart:585-590). _startVideoRecordingWithFallback treats that as an unsupported preset and initializes a lower-preset controller; CameraX createCameraWithSettings calls unbindAll (android_camera_camerax.dart:411). It swaps the new controller in and only then disposes the recording one (948-956). CameraX dispose ignores the camera id, releases the current preview surface (now the new one) and unbinds everything (526-532). Finally it 'starts' the new controller, which CameraX silently skips because the old recording object is still set (1162-1166). Unverified variant: if both call…
- **Failure scenario:** A rider wearing gloves double-taps START. The preview goes black or freezes for the rest of the ride. No video is captured from the second start until the first segment roll (up to the segment length, e.g. 5 min): the old VideoCapture was unbound and the new one is never bound. The downgraded preset is written to settings by _syncSettingsWithCameraCapabilities (408-411, 970-986). startRideSession and startForegroundRecording both run twice.
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:1717-1733 `onPressed: _isApplyingSettings ? null : () async { ... if (camera.isRecording) { ... } else { await _startRecordingFlow(` (no in-flight check); camera_provider.dart:852-859 checks only `!_isInitialized || _controller == null || _recordingsDirectory == null`; 916-921 `try { await currentController.startVideoRecording(); return true; } catch (e) { _log(...) }` then the fallback loop; 948-956 `_controller = candidateController; ... await previousController.dispose();`; android_camera_camerax.dart:526-529 `Future<void> dispose(int cameraId) async { await preview?.releaseSurfaceProvider(); await liveCameraState?.removeObservers(); await processCameraProvider?.unbindAll();`; 1163-1166 `if (recording != null) { // There is currently an active recording, so do not start a new one. return; }`.
- **Verifier corrections:** The second start also clears _lockedSegmentPaths and the session lists, and resets _recordingStartTime, so the session bookkeeping of the first start is wiped as well. The _syncSettingsWithCameraCapabilities line references (970-986) are in recording_screen.dart, not camera_provider.dart. | The likely interleaving is the concurrent one, with both calls inside startVideoCapturing, not the sequential 'already started' one, because the two flows run in parallel with only a tap-interval offset. Either path reaches the same fallback. The second startRecording also clears _sessionSegmentPaths and resets _recordingStartTime, and startRideSession and startForegroundRecording run twice.
- **Recommendation:** Add a bool _isStartingRecording in _RecordingScreenState. Set it at the top of _startRecordingFlow, clear it in a finally block, and disable the button or return early while it is set. In CameraProvider.startRecording, return early when isRecording or a start is in progress. In _startVideoRecordingWithFallback, skip the preset fallback when the CameraException says a recording is already started.
- **Fix note:** start in-flight guard; no fallback when already recording

#### F022

**Ride samples, segment list, locks and timeline exist only in memory until a successful STOP, so a kill, crash or task swipe mid-ride orphans every segment and the ride never appears in Ride History**

Severity: **high** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:347` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

All GPS and speed data and the lock state are lost. The 5-minute segments already on disk, including incident-locked ones, never appear in Ride History or the gallery. Normal retention does not clean them either, because the directory sweep runs only when a session overflows (camera_provider.dart:1193-1198), so they keep using storage unseen.

When a later session overflows, _enforceRecordingRetention (camera_provider.dart:1200-1234) lists every video file in the directory recursively and deletes the oldest by mtime, whatever session it came from. _lockedSegmentPaths is per-session, so incident-locked orphans (and earlier rides' locked segments) are deleted silently. _pruneOrphanTelemetryFiles then removes sidecars whose segments are all gone. The relevant line for the release-notes claim is v0.1.10.md:20. | The retention claim is stronger than the finding states. The in-loop trim at camera_provider.dart:1116 keeps the session list at or below the m…
- **Recommendation:** In TelemetryProvider.startRideSession, write ride_<id>.telemetry.json with sessionComplete:false. Rewrite it (or append NDJSON samples) from CameraProvider._registerCompletedSegment, or on a 30-60 s timer, with the current segmentPaths, locks and timeline; finalize at stop. In rides_list_screen._loadRides, show incomplete sessions as recovered rides, and call RideRecord.fromVideoFile for video files no telemetry references. Optionally enqueue the gallery export as soon as a segment is locked.
- **Fix note:** periodic sealed checkpoints wired from the recording screen; the checkpoint trigger also tracks the newest segment path, so a roll at the loop limit (one segment in, one out) refreshes the checkpoint (found by the docs sync, fixed with tests)

#### F023

**Stopping a ride SHA-256-hashes every segment in pure Dart on the UI isolate before the ride's only index JSON is written**

Severity: **high** · Category: performance · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:655` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:65`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:437`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:456`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:863`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1717`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:50`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:710`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:832`

- **What is wrong:** stopRideSessionAndPersist awaits _buildIntegrityMetadata, which hashes every resolved segment in sequence through integrity_utils.hashFileSha256, i.e. sha256.bind(file.openRead()).first from package:crypto (pure Dart). openRead delivers 64 KB chunks (sky_engine/lib/io/file_impl.dart:8) and each chunk is hashed synchronously on the root isolate, which on Flutter 3.41 Android is also the platform main thread (FlutterLoader.java:432-434 rejects DisableMergedPlatformUIThread). The result is sustained CPU on the frame-building thread rather than one long freeze. Measured with the repo's own hashFileSha256 compiled AOT on an i7-1165G7: 26-30 MB/s (256 MB in 8.5-9.7 s, event-loop lateness up to 31 ms), versus 0.76 s for certutil on the same file. Phone big cores are at best roughly on par with that laptop core; mid and low-end cores are slower. A full default loop is 24 segments x 300 MB (8 Mbps x 300 s as requested; camera_android_camerax 0.7.1 ignores videoBitrate, so the real per-segment size is the device default and may be larger) = ~7.2 GB = ~4-4.6 minutes of hashing at the measured rate. The telemetry JSON, the only thing Ride History lists, is written only after all of it, plus a…
- **Failure scenario:** A rider taps STOP after a 2-hour ride (24 segments). recording_screen.dart:863 stops the foreground service and releases the PARTIAL_WAKE_LOCK, camera.stopRecording returns, then stopRideSessionAndPersist spends several minutes hashing ~7 GB on the UI thread: the preview stutters and 'Recording + telemetry saved' does not appear. If the rider locks the phone meanwhile, the process has no FGS or wake lock, so the CPU can suspend and the cached process can be frozen or killed before writeTelemetryAtomically runs; the ride then never appears in Ride History while its video stays on disk unreferenced. If the rider instead taps START during those minutes, the old persist later runs _activeRideSamples.clear() and _rideSessionId = null (telemetry_provider.dart:456-457), wiping the new ride's sam…
- **Evidence:** telemetry_provider.dart:654-656 'for (final segmentPath in segmentPaths) { final hash = await _hashFileSha256(segmentPath);' -> integrity_utils.dart:65 'final digest = await sha256.bind(file.openRead()).first;'. recording_screen.dart:861-865 'await _backgroundRecordingService.stopForegroundRecording(); final videoPath = await camera.stopRecording();' then :874 'await telemetry.stopRideSessionAndPersist(' and :885-887 the saved notice. BackgroundRecordingService.kt:50-53 ACTION_STOP -> releaseWakeLockIfHeld(); stopForeground(STOP_FOREGROUND_REMOVE); stopSelf(). recording_screen.dart:1717-1719 START/STOP onPressed is gated only by '_isApplyingSettings'. Benchmark (scratchpad bench/bin/bench.dart imports the repo's integrity_utils.dart; dart compile exe; i7-1165G7): 'sha256(openRead) 256MB run0: 8519 ms = 30.1 MB/s; ... max lateness=27ms', 'run1: 9702 ms = 26.4 MB/s', in-memory sha256.conv…
- **Verifier corrections:** The START race is overstated. The old ride's payload map (telemetry_provider.dart:415-435) is built before the await at 437, so the old ride's samples and stats are already snapshotted and safe. What lines 456-457 damage is the new ride: samples captured between the new START and the old persist finishing are cleared, and _rideSessionId is nulled (line 411's fallback then generates a fresh ID). Later samples accumulate normally. The duration figures are a laptop-benchmark extrapolation, not a measurement on a phone. | Calling this 'on the UI isolate' is accurate, but the effect is mostly sustained CPU with small per-chunk lateness (about 30 ms measured), not a hard freeze. The bigger impact…
- **Recommendation:** Write the telemetry JSON first. In stopRideSessionAndPersist, snapshot samples and session id and clear them synchronously before any await, persist the JSON without segment hashes, then compute the hashes in Isolate.run, or once per segment at _registerCompletedSegment time in the background, and rewrite the JSON. In recording_screen.dart, disable the START/STOP button while _isStoppingRecording and show a 'Saving...' state. Optionally call stopForegroundRecording only after persistence completes.
- **Fix note:** ride state captured before await; hashing off UI isolate
