#### F016

**Rides recorded by v0.1.10-v0.1.12 in earlier storage roots (including public Movies/MotoCam/Recordings with GPS JSON) are never migrated, discovered or cleaned up after upgrade**

Severity: **medium (scanner said high)** · Category: privacy · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1544` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **owner decision**

Also affects: `release-notes/v0.1.12.md:23`, `release-notes/v0.1.11.md:20`, `release-notes/v0.1.13.md:7`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:499`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1535`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:659`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:71`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:54`

- **What is wrong:** HEAD stores rides in app-specific storage, but it ignores where the previous public builds put them, and it ships no replacement build. Discovery looks only at _recordingsDirectory (camera_provider.dart:1544-1548). Retention and pruning walk only that directory. Nothing imports, deletes or even mentions the legacy folder /storage/emulated/0/Movies/MotoCam/Recordings: release-notes/v0.1.13.md lists only a version bump, and the folder survives only as a trusted root in ride_record.dart:499-501. Every ride recorded with a public build was written to that folder. At tags v0.1.11 and v0.1.12, _setupRecordingsDirectory stored every segment there (behind MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE), and TelemetryProvider wrote ride_<id>.telemetry.json beside the segments. That file holds the per-second latitude/longitude trail and is labelled 'storagePolicy': 'public-folder-only'. The GitHub release marked Latest (v0.1.13) has no APK, so the v0.1.12 app-release.apk is still the build people download. That APK is debug-signed and asks for 'All files access'. HEAD is release-signed, so moving to HEAD requires uninstalling, and on Android 11+ the new install neither owns nor can see the old shared-storage file…
- **Failure scenario:** A rider installs the v0.1.12 APK from GitHub Releases and uses it for a month. All MP4s, with audio, appear in gallery apps and can be read by any app with media-read permission. The ride_*.telemetry.json files, which reveal home and work routes, can be read by any app with READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE on Android 10 and earlier, and by any file manager with All-files access on Android 11+. The rider then switches to a release-signed HEAD build, which needs an uninstall because the signing key changed. Ride History is empty and the app never touches the old folder, so the public GPS traces stay on the device permanently.
- **Evidence:** `git show v0.1.12:lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart`: lines 37-38 `_androidPublicGalleryDirectory = '/storage/emulated/0/Movies/MotoCam/Recordings'`; lines 674-697 `final recordingsDir = Directory(_androidPublicGalleryDirectory); ... _recordingsDirectory = recordingsDir.path;`. `git show v0.1.12:lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart`: line 404 `'storagePolicy': 'public-folder-only'`, line 418 `'samples': ...`, lines 654-668 write the telemetry file in the parent directory of the first segment. release-notes/v0.1.12.md:23 says "Public folder only: `Movies/MotoCam/Recordings` (no app-private directory)". `aapt2 dump badging` on the v0.1.12 asset shows `uses-permission: name='android.permission.MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE'` and versionCode='1', and apksigner reports "CN=Android Debug". `gh release view` shows v0.1.13 assets [] and v0.1.12 app-release.apk dow…
- **Verifier corrections:** The v0.1.12 APK download count is 4, not 3. I could not verify the debug-signing claim (aapt/apksigner output) in this pass. v0.1.10 rides lived in getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()/MotoCam Recordings (internal, private), which HEAD does not scan either, so they are orphaned but not publicly exposed. HEAD itself still exports locked clips to Movies/MotoCam/Recordings by design (telemetry 'publicExportRelativePath'). | Severity is scoped to HEAD's migration gap; leaving the v0.1.12 APK as the effective latest download is a separate release-management decision. v0.1.10 stored rides in getApplicationDocumentsDirectory(), which is private: after an upgrade those rides are orphaned and invisibl…
- **Recommendation:** Add a one-time legacy step on Android. It finds Movies/MotoCam/Recordings/*.telemetry.json and the matching MP4s, then offers to import them into private storage or delete them: MediaStore.createDeleteRequest for media on API 30+, and an ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT_TREE grant for the JSON. Add a release note warning that earlier builds left location files in that folder. Separately, publish a release-signed current build as the Latest asset and withdraw or deprecate the v0.1.11 and v0.1.12 APKs.
- **Owner decision:** The fix needs owner decisions on publishing a release asset, withdrawing or deprecating the v0.1.11/v0.1.12 APKs, and whether the app may delete or relocate existing user recordings from the legacy folder. | Fixing this means deleting or relocating riders' existing recordings and GPS files, and withdrawing published release assets or publishing a new release. Both are the owner's decisions.
- **Fix note:** owner-only: legacy public-folder migration + re-release (F007, decision 7)

#### F024

**CI follows unpinned Flutter 'stable' and runs pub get without --enforce-lockfile, so it analyzes and tests a different SDK, lock resolution and analysis config than the repo's Flutter 3.41.2**

Severity: **medium** · Category: build-ci · Location: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:18` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:22`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:11`, `.metadata:7`, `pubspec.lock:888`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:18`, `pubspec.yaml:6`, `README.md:39`, `README.md:120`

- **What is wrong:** ci.yml:16-19 uses subosito/flutter-action@v2 with `channel: stable`, `cache: true` and no `flutter-version`. Each run therefore gets whatever stable is current: 3.44.x at the last run (2026-07-07) and 3.47.7 today. The project is developed and locked on 3.41.2 (.metadata:7; pubspec.lock:888-890). On 3.47.7, `flutter pub get` (ci.yml:22, no --enforce-lockfile) silently re-resolves the SDK-pinned packages and auto-migrates analysis_options.yaml. Analyze and tests then run on a tree that differs from the commit. The job has no `flutter build`/Gradle step (ci.yml:11-32), so the Android build failure on CI's own SDK stays green.
- **Failure scenario:** The next push or PR runs on 3.47.7. Pub prints 'Changed 4 dependencies!' and 'Upgrading analysis_options.yaml to exclude build and platform directories.', analyze and test pass, and the check is green, while `flutter build apk` on that same SDK fails. Reviewers read green as 'builds on stable'. Conversely, a future stable that adds a deprecation or lint can turn CI red for code that analyzes clean on 3.41.2 locally, with no repo change.
- **Evidence:** Scratch copy + Flutter 3.47.7 (C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/repro/r_pubget.log and r_lockdiff.log): '> test_api 0.7.12 (was 0.7.9)', '> vector_math 2.4.3 (was 2.2.0)', plus matcher 0.12.18->0.12.20 and meta 1.17.0->1.19.0, and `sdks: dart` '>=3.10.3 <4.0.0' -> '>=3.11.0-0 <4.0.0'; 'Changed 4 dependencies!'; 'Upgrading analysis_options.yaml to exclude build and platform directories.' (the diff adds `analyzer: exclude: [build/**, android/**, ios/**]`). analyze: 'No issues found!'; test: '+11: All tests passed!'; build apk: BUILD FAILED on the KGP floor. `gh run list -R Sarthak195/MotoCam`: the latest CI runs (2026-07-07) are all success. Job steps (gh api .../runs/28842091751/jobs): Set up job, Checkout, Set up Flutter, Install dependencies, Run analyzer, Run release security precheck, Run tes…
- **Verifier corrections:** pubspec.lock does not pin the toolchain to 3.41.2: its sdks block reads `dart: ">=3.10.3 <4.0.0"` / `flutter: ">=3.38.4"` (pubspec.lock:888-890). Only .metadata records the 3.41.2 revision.
- **Recommendation:** In .github/workflows/ci.yml, set `flutter-version: 3.41.2` (keeping channel: stable). Change the install step to `flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile`. Add an `actions/setup-java@v4` (temurin 17) step and a `flutter build apk --debug` step; a debug build needs no signing secrets. Bump the pinned version together with the Gradle plugin versions.
- **Fix note:** CI pins Flutter 3.41.2, enforce-lockfile, Temurin 17, debug APK

#### F025

**CI never builds Android or iOS, never checks formatting and collects no coverage**

Severity: **medium** · Category: build-ci · Location: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:21` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:1`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:1`, `android/app/build.gradle.kts:93`, `scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:65`

- **What is wrong:** The single CI job runs `flutter pub get`, `flutter analyze`, the precheck with -SkipTests -SkipAnalyze, and `flutter test` (ci.yml:21-32). Several things are therefore never checked: - No build compiles the Kotlin sources (MainActivity.kt, BackgroundRecordingService.kt), the Gradle scripts with their signing gate, the R8/proguard configuration or the iOS project. - There is no `dart format --set-exit-if-changed` step. Today 15 of 25 Dart files are unformatted ('Formatted 25 files (15 changed)', exit 1), and rides_list_screen.dart is tab-indented. - No coverage is collected; coverage today is 18%. - There is no .gitattributes. The index is LF, but local core.autocrlf=true produces CRLF working copies (18 of 25 Dart files).
- **Failure scenario:** A change renames a channel method in MainActivity.kt, breaks Kotlin compilation, or introduces a Gradle or R8 misconfiguration. `flutter analyze` and `flutter test` never invoke Gradle, so CI stays green; for a33a583, runs 28842091751 and 28842099241 executed only analyze and test. The break surfaces only when the maintainer runs `flutter build apk --release` on release day. A format gate added later fails on 15 files at once, so it keeps being postponed.
- **Evidence:** .github/workflows/ci.yml:21-32 contains four run steps, none of which builds or formats. `dart format --output=none --set-exit-if-changed lib test` reports 'Formatted 25 files (15 changed)' (baseline exit 1; re-run in the scratch copy). `git ls-files --eol lib test` shows 18 files 'i/lf w/crlf' and 7 'i/lf w/lf'. No .gitattributes file exists.
- **Verifier corrections:** Coverage at 18% was not re-verified, because running tests is not allowed here. The format baseline depends on the package language version (sdk lower bound 3.0, which gives the short style; see F070). Make the formatting commit after any SDK-floor change, or it will churn twice. | Gate the format check only after the SDK lower bound is settled (see F070). Raising pubspec's sdk lower bound to >=3.7 moves dart format to the tall style and changes the format diff completely, so one reformat commit should follow the language-version change, not come before it. A debug build will not exercise R8 or proguard, because isMinifyEnabled is set only for release.
- **Recommendation:** In .github/workflows/ci.yml, add a `flutter build apk --debug` step; it compiles the Kotlin and Gradle scripts without signing secrets. Pin the Flutter version in subosito/flutter-action (e.g. flutter-version: 3.41.2). After a single dedicated `dart format lib test` commit, add `dart format --output=none --set-exit-if-changed lib test`. Add a .gitattributes with `* text=auto eol=lf`. An optional macos-latest `flutter build ios --no-codesign` job and `flutter test --coverage` can follow.
- **Fix note:** format/coverage/APK/iOS CI + .gitattributes; coverage floor and signed R8 build are owner-only

#### F026

**Gallery export of locked clips cannot succeed on Android 7-9: WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE (maxSdk 28) is declared but never requested, and the pre-Q native branch ignores the requested relative path**

Severity: **medium** · Category: android-platform · Location: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:213` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:23`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:206`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1404`, `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:78`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:205`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1374-1431`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:164`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:204` …

- **What is wrong:** Below API 29, exportVideoToGallery writes with FileOutputStream into Environment.getExternalStoragePublicDirectory(MOVIES)/MotoCam/Videos (lines 204-216) and ignores the relativePath argument. That write needs the WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE runtime permission, which is declared at AndroidManifest.xml:23-24 with maxSdkVersion 28. No Dart code requests Permission.storage: a grep over lib/ finds none, and neither the audit (app_permission_service.dart:78-104) nor camera_provider.dart:372-378 asks for it. The Dart fallback copy into /storage/emulated/0/Movies/MotoCam/Recordings (camera_provider.dart:1404-1431) needs the same permission. minSdk is 24 (flutter.minSdkVersion).
- **Failure scenario:** On an Android 8 or 9 phone the rider locks an incident. At stop, the export throws EXPORT_FAILED (EACCES) and the fallback copy fails too. The only copy stays in app-specific storage, which is deleted on uninstall and is subject to retention. If the rider grants storage manually in Settings, the clip lands in Movies/MotoCam/Videos instead of the Recordings folder that every other path uses.
- **Evidence:** `val targetDir = File(moviesDir, "MotoCam/Videos")` ... `FileOutputStream(targetFile)` (MainActivity.kt:206, 213); `<uses-permission android:name="android.permission.WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE" android:maxSdkVersion="28" />` (AndroidManifest.xml:23-24); a grep for Permission.storage, manageExternalStorage, videos or photos in lib/ returns no matches.
- **Verifier corrections:** Android 7-9 is accurate. Android 6 (API 23) is affected too, because WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE became a runtime permission at API 23, though minSdk 24 excludes it. A Permission.storage request must be SDK-gated, because on API 33+ permission_handler reports it as denied. The app has no device_info_plus, so the SDK level must come from the existing native channel. Raising minSdk to 29 is an alternative but is a product decision, not the minimal fix. | Exports are queued only for locked segments (exportCandidates filtered by _lockedSegmentPaths) through _enqueueDeferredGalleryExport (camera_provider.dart:1338, called at 1509), so normal unlocked segments are unaffected. On failure the code still…
- **Recommendation:** In AppPermissionService (or before _enqueueDeferredGalleryExport), request Permission.storage when Android SDK_INT < 29, and show a notice if it is denied or the export fails. In MainActivity's pre-Q branch, build the target directory from the relativePath argument (Environment.getExternalStorageDirectory() + relativePath) instead of the hard-coded 'MotoCam/Videos'.
- **Fix note:** pre-Q export honours relativePath + permission check; storage asked only when gallery export is on (untested on API < 29)

#### F027

**Documented release-signing setup fails: key.properties.template's storeFile is resolved by Gradle against android/app so the keystore is never found, and generate-keystore.ps1's password-reuse prompt and Invoke-Expression handling are broken**

Severity: **medium** · Category: docs-drift · Location: `android/key.properties.template:4` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/key.properties.template:5`, `scripts/generate-keystore.ps1:2`, `android/app/build.gradle.kts:85`, `README.md:203`, `README.md:206`, `scripts/generate-keystore.ps1:17`, `scripts/generate-keystore.ps1:19`, `scripts/generate-keystore.ps1:33` …

- **What is wrong:** The template says 'Path to the keystore file, relative to the repo root or absolute.' and defaults to storeFile=android/release.keystore. README.md:200-213 tells contributors to run generate-keystore.ps1 from the repo root (its default OutFile is 'android/release.keystore', relative to the current directory) and then copy the template. The path does not resolve where the template says: - android/app/build.gradle.kts:85 resolves the value with `file(releaseStoreFilePath)` inside the :app project. - A relative path therefore resolves against android/app/, giving android/app/android/release.keystore, which does not exist. - hasReleaseSigningConfig (:43-47) only checks that the four strings are non-blank, so the Gradle gate passes and the failure surfaces later in AGP's signing task. The maintainer's own setup confirms this: - Their working key.properties uses the app-relative value `upload-keystore.jks`, and that file sits at android/app/upload-keystore.jks. - The android/release.keystore that the README's script produced is unused.
- **Failure scenario:** A contributor follows README steps 1-3 exactly: 1. Generate the keystore from the repo root. 2. Copy the template and fill in the passwords. 3. Run `flutter build apk --release`. The signing gate passes. The release signing step then fails because no keystore exists at <repo>/android/app/android/release.keystore.
- **Evidence:** Documented path: - android/key.properties.template:4-5: '# Path to the keystore file, relative to the repo root or absolute.' / 'storeFile=android/release.keystore'. - scripts/generate-keystore.ps1:2: '[string]$OutFile = "android/release.keystore",'. How Gradle resolves it: - android/app/build.gradle.kts:84-86: 'if (!releaseStoreFilePath.isNullOrBlank()) { storeFile = file(releaseStoreFilePath) }'. - build.gradle.kts:43-47 checks only that the values are non-blank. On this machine: - android/key.properties, with only the storeFile line read: 'storeFile=upload-keystore.jks'. - `ls` shows android/app/upload-keystore.jks and android/release.keystore exist, and android/app/android does not.
- **Verifier corrections:** The line numbers (85, 43-47) are accurate. The README steps are at README.md:196-213. The broken password reuse probably makes keytool fail outright: it takes '-validity' as the key password and then rejects '10000' as an illegal option, after which the script throws 'Failed to generate keystore.' This was inferred from the code, not run. | The same app-relative resolution applies to the MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_FILE env var (build.gradle.kts:38), so a relative path in CI hits the same trap. Of the two fix options, prefer changing the docs and template: resolving relative paths against the repo root in Gradle would break the maintainer's existing, working key.properties ('storeFile=upload-keystore.…
- **Recommendation:** In android/key.properties.template, change the comment to 'relative to android/app/ or absolute' and the default to `storeFile=../release.keystore`, which matches generate-keystore.ps1's default output. In build.gradle.kts, extend the release gate to throw a clear GradleException when `file(releaseStoreFilePath).exists()` is false. In generate-keystore.ps1, replace `if (-not $keyPass)` with `if ($keyPass.Length -eq 0)`.
- **Fix note:** keystore path documented; existence check

#### F028

**iOS declares the 'audio' UIBackgroundMode although no feature plays or records audio in the background (App Review 2.5.4 risk)**

Severity: **medium** · Category: ios-platform · Location: `ios/Runner/Info.plist:23` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `ios/Runner/Info.plist:14`, `ios/Runner/Info.plist:22`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:143`

- **What is wrong:** UIBackgroundModes lists location and audio (Info.plist:20-24). 'location' is consumed by geolocator (see the background-GPS finding). Nothing uses 'audio': - Ride playback stops on inactive, paused or detached (ride_playback_screen.dart:143-149). - lib/ configures no audio session, background playback or mixWithOthers. - Camera capture is prohibited in the background, and camera_avfoundation appends samples only while both its video and audio capture sessions run (DefaultCamera.swift:1269-1270). Commit cd39097 removed 'processing' but kept 'audio'. Related: NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription ('...even when in background', Info.plist:14-15) is never needed for a request on iOS: - geolocator requests WhenInUse whenever NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription exists (PermissionHandler.m:72-73). - The locationAlways audit runs only on Android (app_permission_service.dart:90-103).
- **Failure scenario:** The build issues are fixed and the app is submitted for review. The reviewer backgrounds MotoCam while it is recording. No audible content plays and no recording continues, so the reviewer rejects the app under Guideline 2.5.4: 'Multitasking apps may only use background services for their intended purposes: VoIP, audio playback, location, task completion, local notifications, etc.'
- **Evidence:** - Info.plist:20-24: `<key>UIBackgroundModes</key> <array> <string>location</string> <string>audio</string> </array>`. - ride_playback_screen.dart:143-149: `if (state == AppLifecycleState.inactive || state == AppLifecycleState.paused || state == AppLifecycleState.detached) { _stopPlayback(); }`. - `grep -rni "allowBackgroundPlayback|VideoPlayerOptions|mixWithOthers|AudioSession" lib` finds no matches. - <pub-cache>/camera_avfoundation-0.10.1/.../DefaultCamera.swift:1269-1270: `if isRecording && !isRecordingPaused && videoCaptureSession.isRunning && audioCaptureSession.isRunning`. - `git show cd39097 -- ios/Runner/Info.plist` removes only `<string>processing</string>` and the local-network keys.
- **Verifier corrections:** Keeping NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription is harmless and is not itself a rejection risk. Treat it as cleanup, not a defect.
- **Recommendation:** Remove `<string>audio</string>` from UIBackgroundModes in ios/Runner/Info.plist, ideally as part of the F002 plist restore. Keep 'location' only while background GPS during active rides is intended. Drop NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription since iOS never requests Always.
- **Fix note:** audio background mode and Always-location string removed

#### F029

**ios/Runner/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy is not referenced by the Xcode project (never bundled) and declares API categories and reason codes that Apple does not define**

Severity: **medium** · Category: ios-platform · Location: `ios/Runner/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy:13` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `ios/Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:214`, `ios/Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:6`, `lib/features/map/map_source_config.dart:9`, `ios/Runner/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy:13`, `ios/Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:1`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1214`

- **What is wrong:** The file was added in eec6c2d, but project.pbxproj never references it. It is neither a PBXFileReference nor in the Runner Resources phase, which lists only LaunchScreen.storyboard, AppFrameworkInfo.plist, Assets.xcassets and Main.storyboard (:214-224). With objectVersion 54 (:6) there is no folder sync, so the file is never copied into Runner.app. Its content is also invalid against Apple's schema: - NSPrivacyAccessedAPIType must be one of FileTimestamp, SystemBootTime, DiskSpace, ActiveKeyboards or UserDefaults. The file uses NSPrivacyAccessedAPICategoryFileTimestampAPIs plus invented Camera, Microphone, Location and Motion 'APIs' categories. - Reasons must be Apple codes such as C617.1. The file uses UUIDs. - There is no NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypes. Today it is dead configuration with no review impact: - Runner's own sources (AppDelegate, SceneDelegate, GeneratedPluginRegistrant) use no required-reason API. - Flutter.framework's manifest declares FileTimestamp 0A2A.1/C617.1 and SystemBootTime 35F9.1. - Every iOS plugin in the lockfile ships its own manifest through resource_bundles. The risk is that it gives a false sense of compliance and will break an upload once it is wired…
- **Failure scenario:** While preparing an App Store submission, a maintainer sees that PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy is not in the target and adds it to the Runner Resources phase, which is clearly what the file was meant for. The upload is then rejected with ITMS-91056 (invalid privacy manifest), because five NSPrivacyAccessedAPIType values and five reason strings match no allowed value. The file also suggests that camera, microphone, location and motion are 'required-reason APIs', which misleads whoever fills in the App Privacy details.
- **Evidence:** - PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy:13 `<string>NSPrivacyAccessedAPICategoryFileTimestampAPIs</string>`; :16 `<string>EA3D2B7E-11EA-454D-B1AC-1D706DBBAE63</string>`; :21 `NSPrivacyAccessedAPICategoryCameraAPIs`; :29 `...MicrophoneAPIs`; :37 `...LocationAPIs`; :45 `...MotionAPIs`. - `grep -c PrivacyInfo ios/Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` returns 0. - Apple's documentation JSON for NSPrivacyAccessedAPIType (developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/bundleresources/app-privacy-configuration/nsprivacyaccessedapitypes/nsprivacyaccessedapitype.json) lists possibleValues = NSPrivacyAccessedAPICategoryFileTimestamp, SystemBootTime, DiskSpace, ActiveKeyboards, UserDefaults. FileTimestamp reasons are DDA9.1, C617.1, 3B52.1 and 0A2A.1. - TN3181: a value is invalid if it is 'a string whose value doesn't match a category of required reason APIs', and reasons are invalid if they 'don't match the expect…
- **Verifier corrections:** Even the FileTimestamp entry is invalid: the allowed value is 'NSPrivacyAccessedAPICategoryFileTimestamp', without the 'APIs' suffix.
- **Recommendation:** Minimal fix: delete ios/Runner/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy. It is unbundled, and Runner's own Swift code uses no required-reason API; Flutter.framework and the plugins ship their own manifests. Authoring a real Runner manifest is a separate, later step.
- **Fix note:** invalid, unbundled PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy removed

#### F030

**Locked permission_handler_android 13.0.1 reports 'Ask every time' permissions as permanentlyDenied, and ensure()'s status-first flow then never re-prompts**

Severity: **medium** · Category: dependencies · Location: `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:57` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `pubspec.yaml:28`, `pubspec.lock:539`, `android/app/build.gradle.kts:62`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:108`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:160`

- **What is wrong:** ensure() reads `permission.status` and calls `request()` only when the status is denied, restricted or limited (app_permission_service.dart:52-58). It then sets hasPermanentlyDenied from `status.isPermanentlyDenied` (:66). In the locked permission_handler_android 13.0.1 (pubspec.lock:539-546), status is derived from a sticky per-permission SharedPreferences 'was denied before' flag combined with shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale. PermissionUtils.determineDeniedVariant returns NEVER_ASK_AGAIN, which surfaces as permanentlyDenied, whenever wasDeniedBefore && !shouldShowRationale. That also matches a permission the user has reset to 'Ask every time' in system settings, a bug upstream fixed in permission_handler_android 14.1.0. That version is only reachable through permission_handler 13.0.2, a major bump blocked by `^12.0.1` (pubspec.yaml:28). 14.x also requires compileSdk 37, and its own build uses compileSdk 37, AGP 9.0.1 and KGP 2.3.20. The app hard-codes compileSdk 36 (android/app/build.gradle.kts:62), and only platforms android-35/36 are installed locally. MotoCam's Dart code needs no change for the 13.x semantics, because it already evaluates the result of request().
- **Failure scenario:** A rider denies the camera (or mic, or location) prompt twice, and MotoCam shows 'Some permissions are permanently denied. Use Open Settings to enable them.' (recording_screen.dart:126-127). In Android settings the rider picks 'Ask every time' and returns. ensureRequiredPermissions still sees status=permanentlyDenied, skips request() and reports a blocking issue. _bootstrapRuntimePrerequisites then returns before `_initializeCamera()` (recording_screen.dart:108-112), so CameraProvider's own unconditional `Permission.camera.request()` (camera_provider.dart:372) is never reached. 'Request Again' (recording_screen.dart:160) repeats the same status check. The rider cannot record until they choose 'Allow' directly in settings.
- **Evidence:** app_permission_service.dart:52-58 `var status = await permission.status; ... if (status.isDenied || status.isRestricted || status.isLimited) { status = await permission.request(); }` and :66 `if (status.isPermanentlyDenied) {`. [pub cache] permission_handler_android-13.0.1 PermissionUtils.java:521-533 `final boolean isDenied = wasDeniedBefore ? !shouldShowRational : shouldShowRational; ... if (wasDeniedBefore && isDenied) { return PermissionConstants.PERMISSION_STATUS_NEVER_ASK_AGAIN; }`. permission_handler_android CHANGELOG 14.1.0: 'Fixes `Permission.status` reporting `permanentlyDenied` after the user reset a permanently denied permission to "Ask every time" in the Android app settings (Android 11+)'; 14.0.0: 'BREAKING CHANGES: When updating to version 14.0.0 make sure to also set the `compileSdkVersion` in the `app/build.gradle` file to `37`'. permission_handler 13.0.2: 'Updates `per…
- **Recommendation:** In lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart ensure(), call permission.request() whenever the status is not granted, including permanentlyDenied, and set hasPermanentlyDenied only from the request() result. Android returns immediately without a dialog when the permission is truly blocked. Defer the permission_handler ^13.0.2 bump until the compileSdk 37 / AGP / Kotlin toolchain upgrade.
- **Fix note:** ensure() re-requests non-granted permissions; permission_handler 13 bump deferred (needs compileSdk 37/AGP 9)

#### F031

**The battery-optimization exemption intent fires on every launch and START (and again after recording starts) via a Play-restricted direct intent, never remembers refusal, and is re-checked before the user answers**

Severity: **medium** · Category: android-platform · Location: `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:105` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/core/services/background_recording_service.dart:56`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:101`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:21`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:97`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:368`, `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:109`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:101-117`, `lib/core/services/background_recording_service.dart:52-78` …

- **What is wrong:** Every audit calls requestIgnoreBatteryOptimizations whenever the app is not exempt (lines 105-115), and it never remembers the user's answer. The audit runs at bootstrap (recording_screen.dart:97) and on every START (:368). startForegroundRecording asks again once per process (background_recording_service.dart:56, 62-78), after the camera has already started. The native handler fires ACTION_REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS and returns true immediately (MainActivity.kt:101-117). The audit's re-check (lines 110-111) and the 'Recommended for reliable background recording' SnackBar (recording_screen.dart:188-197) are therefore computed before the user answers. Google Play restricts REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS (AndroidManifest.xml:21) to listed use cases, and a dashcam that already runs a foreground service is not one of them.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider who denies the exemption sees 'Let app always run in background?' on every cold start and on every START. On the first START it appears a second time over the live REC screen, about 1 s after recording begins. Leaving that dialog open for 8 s or more also triggers the spurious stall recovery (recording_screen.dart:249-268). Play review may flag the restricted permission.
- **Evidence:** Emulator sequence of `START {act=android.settings.REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS ...}` launches: - 20:54:12 on launch 1 (denied). - 20:55:43 on launch 2. - 20:56:32 on START (denied). - 20:58:17.714, 1.3 s after `20:58:16.404 [MotoCam] Recording started successfully`, over REC 00:00:15 (C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/androidscan/shot13.png). Logs: .../androidscan/start-double-battery.txt, .../androidscan/second-launch-prompts.txt. developer.android.com/training/monitoring-device-state/doze-standby: 'Google Play policies prohibit apps from requesting direct exemption from Power Management features ... unless the core function of the app is adversely affected.'
- **Verifier corrections:** The second prompt fires once per process, on the first START only, and only if the user has not granted the exemption. | The doze-standby page says a foreground service keeps the app from being treated as idle for App Standby, but it does not say an FGS exempts the app from Doze. The recommendation should not claim Doze exemption; wake lock plus FGS is the actual mechanism. A dashcam might argue the 'Safety app' row, so the Play outcome is a risk rather than certain.
- **Recommendation:** In lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart, replace the request + immediate re-check with a status-only check that adds 'Ignore battery optimizations' to missingRecommended without launching the intent. Remove the _ensureBatteryOptimizationExemption call from startForegroundRecording in lib/core/services/background_recording_service.dart. Either drop REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS and the direct intent in MainActivity.kt, or switch to ACTION_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATION_SETTINGS behind a one-time, persisted, user-initiated prompt.
- **Fix note:** no automatic exemption request; offer-once dialog with persisted flag; after the one-time offer the battery row no longer opens the START checklist by itself (owner choice, 2026-10-21)

#### F032

**The integrity envelope is an unkeyed SHA-256 and segmentHashes sit outside payloadHash, so anyone who can write the files can forge a ride that shows VERIFIED**

Severity: **medium** · Category: security · Location: `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:19` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:662`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:693`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:701`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:204`, `README.md:114`

- **What is wrong:** payloadHash is a plain SHA-256 of the canonical JSON minus the 'integrity' key. There is no key, signature or device binding, and the algorithm is published in this repo. The per-segment hashes, segmentCount and generatedAt sit inside 'integrity', which no hash covers. Anyone who can write the telemetry file and segments can forge a ride that fully verifies. That includes the device owner via USB/adb, anyone holding a copy of the files, and other apps on Android 9 and earlier (see the storage finding). They can edit route, speed or distance and recompute payloadHash, or swap a video and update only its entry in segmentHashes. The UI then shows a green VERIFIED badge, and README.md:114 says each ride 'is protected by a SHA-256 integrity envelope'. That implies tamper evidence the scheme cannot give; it only detects accidental corruption.
- **Failure scenario:** To change a ride's route and speed, an editor changes samples[*].latitude/speed and distanceKm in ride_<id>.telemetry.json and recomputes sha256 over the key-sorted JSON without 'integrity'. They also replace one MP4 and update only integrity.segmentHashes[thatPath]. Ride History shows VERIFIED, and playback's full segment verification shows no security warning. The forged ride is presented as authenticated evidence.
- **Evidence:** integrity_utils.dart:19-23: `final canonical = canonicalizeJsonValue(payload); final encoded = utf8.encode(jsonEncode(canonical)); return sha256.convert(encoded).toString();`. telemetry_provider.dart:662-669 builds the envelope {'algo': 'sha256', 'payloadHash': _hashJsonCanonical(payload), 'segmentCount': ..., 'segmentHashes': segmentHashes, 'generatedAt': ...}. ride_record.dart:693-695 hashes the payload after `..remove('integrity')`. :701-718 compares segment files with `_readExpectedSegmentHashes(integrity)`, the unprotected map. Measured with flutter test on a copy of lib/ (Windows): "A1 forged payload: verified issues=[] quarantined=0 segments=1 label=Verified distance=42.0" and "A2 payloadHash unchanged=true full-load: verified issues=[] ... | verifySegmentIntegrity: verified issues=[]". README.md:114 makes the claim, and rides_list_screen.dart:202-207 shows the VERIFIED badge.
- **Verifier corrections:** The envelope is built in telemetry_provider.dart:648-667 (_buildIntegrityMetadata); the call at ~line 662 is inside it. The envelope also carries 'hashedSegmentCount', which is likewise unprotected. The 'other apps on Android 9 and earlier' vector depends on the separate storage-location finding and was not verified here. | Moving segmentHashes under payloadHash alone fixes nothing while the hash has no key; the core problem is the missing key. Option (a) would need new native Keystore code in MainActivity.kt and has no iOS counterpart, because iOS has no channel handlers.
- **Recommendation:** Minimal fix: relabel it as a corruption checksum. Change the VERIFIED badge to 'CHECKSUM OK' in rides_list_screen.dart, soften the 'Security warning' text in ride_playback_screen.dart, and reword README.md:114 so it does not claim tamper protection. Real tamper evidence would mean an HMAC/ECDSA signature from an Android Keystore key over the canonical payload including segmentHashes, added through a new background_recording or media channel method.
- **Owner decision:** The owner must choose whether MotoCam markets rides as tamper-evident evidence, which requires device-bound signing and a native Keystore channel that iOS does not have, or relabels the feature as a corruption checksum. | Whether MotoCam claims evidentiary tamper resistance (and builds Keystore signing) or presents the hash as a corruption checksum is a product decision about how ride footage is…
- **Fix note:** bound envelope v2 + honest wording; keyed integrity (HMAC) is owner-only

#### F033

**writeTelemetryAtomically deletes the target before renaming and every writer shares one '<target>.tmp' path, so overlapping writes or a kill in the gap lose the ride JSON**

Severity: **medium** · Category: concurrency · Location: `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:86` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:659-665`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:258-273`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:606-616`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1258-1263`, `README.md:63`

- **What is wrong:** The helper writes '<target>.tmp' (81-82), deletes the target (85-87) and renames the temp file (88). On any error it copies the temp file to the target (89-94). The delete is not needed, because File.rename already replaces an existing file: verified on Windows, and POSIX rename is atomic. It opens a window in which no target exists. Every writer uses the same temp path, and nothing serialises them. The Unlock button has no in-flight guard (rides_list_screen.dart:258-273), while setLockState re-hashes every segment for minutes with no progress UI and swallows every exception (ride_record.dart:663-665). The dartdoc (69-71) and README.md:63 call the function atomic.
- **Failure scenario:** The rider taps Unlock twice on a long ride. Both calls hash the segments and then write about one tap interval apart. One possible order: A writes tmp, B writes tmp, A deletes the target, A renames tmp to the target, B deletes the target, B's rename fails because tmp is gone, and B's copy throws and is swallowed. ride_<id>.telemetry.json no longer exists, and the ride disappears from Ride History. A process kill between the delete and the rename has the same result. The leftover .tmp is never read or pruned, because camera_provider.dart:1261-1263 matches only '.telemetry.json'.
- **Evidence:** Re-ran .../scratchpad/exp/motocam/test/exp_history_test.dart K (200 trials, two concurrent calls on one file): 'K lost=34 errors=86 of 200 (platform=windows)'. .../scratchpad/renamecheck/r.dart (rename onto an existing file without deleting it first): 'rename over existing ok: NEW tmpExists=false'. Code 85-88: 'if (await telemetryFile.exists()) { await telemetryFile.delete(); } await tempFile.rename(telemetryFile.path);'. The Android interleaving is inferred.
- **Verifier corrections:** The only concurrent-writer path is setLockState racing itself: a double Unlock, or a second unlock from a reloaded card. The ride-stop writer (telemetry_provider.dart:451) writes a different, new file, so it does not collide in practice. The process-kill window between delete and rename is real but very small. The temp file is never cross-filesystem, so the copy fallback is dead in normal operation. | The writers that can overlap in practice are two setLockState calls on the same ride. The stop-time write in telemetry_provider.dart:451 runs once per ride, normally before the target exists. The dartdoc comment at integrity_utils.dart:69-71 calls the write 'atomic' and says it falls back to '…
- **Recommendation:** In integrity_utils.dart, remove the exists/delete block and rename straight over the target. Use a unique temp name (for example `${path}.${DateTime.now().microsecondsSinceEpoch}.tmp`) and delete it in a finally block. In rides_list_screen.dart, add an _isUnlocking flag that disables the Unlock button while setLockState runs (same pattern as _isOpeningRide).
- **Fix note:** atomic unique-temp write + per-path lock

#### F034

**initializeCamera has no re-entrancy guard, so START during the launch or history-return init runs two concurrent inits that interfere and overwrite a controller without disposing it**

Severity: **medium** · Category: concurrency · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:315` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:929-968`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:496-531`

- **What is wrong:** initializeCamera is called from the bootstrap (recording_screen.dart:112), after returning from history (1021), from the START preflight whenever !isInitialized (569-571), and via applyRecordingSettings (camera_provider.dart:529). The START button is enabled during the first two. Nothing serializes these calls. Each call captures _controller, which is still null while another init is running (326), builds its own controller, and assigns it at 469, so one controller is overwritten without being disposed. CameraX keeps a single global camera state, and createCameraWithSettings unbinds everything (android_camera_camerax.dart:411), so the two inits interfere with each other. Their failures are cached as unsupported FPS/presets (439, 457-459) and then persisted by the settings sync (see the previous finding).
- **Failure scenario:** The rider opens the app and taps START while the preview spinner is still showing, so the preflight starts a second init. Possible outcomes: one init fails, presets are marked unsupported, and the camera comes up at a lower preset that is saved to settings; or a CameraController is leaked; or the preflight reports 'Camera Ready' as failed and blocks the start.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:326 `final previousController = _controller;` ... 469 `_controller = initializedController;` (no in-progress flag anywhere in 315-494); recording_screen.dart:112 `await _initializeCamera();`, 569-571 `if (!camera.isInitialized && !camera.isRecording) { await _initializeCamera(); }`, 1021 `await _initializeCamera();`; android_camera_camerax.dart:411 `unawaited(processCameraProvider!.unbindAll());`.
- **Verifier corrections:** There is a further interference path. When the two inits overlap during their Permission.*.request() calls, permission_handler on Android can reject the second concurrent request ('A request for permissions is already running'). That exception lands in the catch at 489-493, which sets _isInitialized=false and notifies while the first init is still running. The 'previous finding' cross-reference concerns the settings sync at recording_screen.dart:970-986. | The quoted line numbers are accurate. One more interaction: if the bootstrap is inside `Permission.camera.request()` (line 372) when the START flow calls `ensureRequiredPermissions`, permission_handler can reject the overlapping request.…
- **Recommendation:** In CameraProvider add a `Future<void>? _initFuture`. Make initializeCamera return the in-flight future when one exists (apply any new parameters to fields and re-run once afterwards if they differ), and dispose any controller that is built but not installed. Expose `isInitializing` and disable START in recording_screen.dart while it is true.
- **Fix note:** initializeCamera serialised; no init during a session

#### F035

**The bitrate setting is a no-op on Android: locked camera_android_camerax 0.7.1 never passes videoBitrate to the CameraX Recorder (fixed in 0.7.3, unreachable on Flutter 3.41.2), yet the sidecar records it as applied**

Severity: **medium** · Category: android-platform · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:643` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:854`, `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:48`, `lib/features/recording/widgets/recording_settings_sheet.dart:409`, `pub-cache camera_android_camerax-0.7.1/lib/src/android_camera_camerax.dart:430`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:643`, `pubspec.lock:36`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1394`, `pubspec.yaml:16`

- **What is wrong:** The provider passes videoBitrate: _videoBitrateBps, one of 4/8/12/16 Mbps from AppSettingsProvider.bitrateOptionsMbps (app_settings_provider.dart:48). The Android implementation builds `recorder = Recorder(qualitySelector: presetQualitySelector);` (pub-cache camera_android_camerax-0.7.1/lib/src/android_camera_camerax.dart:430) and reads only resolutionPreset, fps and enableAudio from MediaSettings. The pigeon Recorder supports targetVideoEncodingBitRate, but the plugin never sets it. The sidecar still records the requested value as applied (recording_screen.dart:854, 'appliedBitrateMbps': camera.videoBitrateMbps).
- **Failure scenario:** A rider picks 4 Mbps to fit more loop segments on a small phone. Segments are still encoded at CameraX's default bitrate for the quality, so storage fills faster than expected, and the integrity-protected sidecar claims 4 Mbps was applied.
- **Evidence:** createCameraWithSettings reads `final int? targetFps = mediaSettings?.fps;` and builds `recorder = Recorder(qualitySelector: presetQualitySelector);`. android_camera_camerax.dart never references mediaSettings.videoBitrate; a grep for 'bitrate' in the plugin matches only generated pigeon code.
- **Verifier corrections:** The highest version reachable on Dart 3.11 is 0.7.2 (0.7.2+1 already needs Dart 3.12). The fix therefore becomes reachable only after moving the toolchain to Dart 3.12 / Flutter 3.44+. | Precisely: 0.7.2+1 and later need Dart 3.12 / Flutter 3.44, so on the local Flutter 3.41.2 / Dart 3.11 the resolvable ceiling is 0.7.2, and 0.7.2 still ignores the bitrate.
- **Recommendation:** Short term: on Android, write `appliedBitrateMbps` as null (or 'not applied') in the sidecar at recording_screen.dart:854, and mark the bitrate control in recording_settings_sheet.dart as having no effect on Android. Proper fix: move the toolchain to Flutter 3.44 / Dart 3.12+ so camera_android_camerax resolves to 0.7.3 or later, then check the output bitrate on a device.
- **Fix note:** bitrate support exposed and recorded; real fix needs plugin fork/Flutter 3.44 (F098, owner)

#### F036

**Ride videos and per-second GPS traces are stored in app-specific external storage, readable by other apps with storage permission on Android 7-9 (minSdk 24)**

Severity: **medium** · Category: privacy · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:663` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **owner decision**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:434`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:697`, `android/app/build.gradle.kts:76`, `README.md:158`

- **What is wrong:** On Android the recordings directory is getExternalStorageDirectory() + '/MotoCam Recordings', which resolves to /storage/emulated/0/Android/data/com.example.motocam/files/MotoCam Recordings. Every segment is written there. So is every ride_<id>.telemetry.json, which holds unencrypted latitude, longitude, speed and timestamp for each second of the ride. minSdk is flutter.minSdkVersion (24). Android documents that on Android 9 and lower, other apps holding storage permission can read another app's app-specific external files. The same folder is also browsable over USB/MTP. No code uses internal storage (filesDir / getApplicationSupportDirectory) or encryption for the location trail. README.md:158 nevertheless describes storage as 'App Storage (private-first)'.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider uses an old Android 8/9 phone as a dedicated dashcam, which is common. Any installed app that holds READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE (gallery, file manager, messaging) can list Android/data/com.example.motocam/files/MotoCam Recordings. It can read every ride's MP4 with audio, and the JSON trails that show home, work and daily routes with timestamps.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:662-673: `if (Platform.isAndroid) { baseDirectory = (await getExternalStorageDirectory()) ?? await getApplicationDocumentsDirectory(); } ... Directory('${baseDirectory.path}${Platform.pathSeparator}$_recordingsDirectoryName')`. telemetry_provider.dart:434 adds `'samples': _activeRideSamples.map((sample) => sample.toJson()).toList()`, and each sample serialises latitude and longitude (telemetry_data.dart:81-92). telemetry_provider.dart:678-698 writes the file next to the first segment. android/app/build.gradle.kts:76 `minSdk = flutter.minSdkVersion`, which is 24 in Flutter 3.41.2. Android docs (Access app-specific files): "On devices that run Android 9 (API level 28) or lower, your app can access the app-specific files that belong to other apps, provided that your app has the appropriate storage permissions." README.md:158: "App Storage (private-first)".
- **Recommendation:** Change `_setupRecordingsDirectory` in camera_provider.dart to use `getApplicationSupportDirectory()` (internal filesDir) on Android, and write the telemetry JSON beside the segments, as telemetry_provider.dart already does. Add a one-time migration of existing recordings from the external folder. Reword README.md:158, or raise minSdk to 29 instead.
- **Owner decision:** Moving the recordings root means relocating existing recordings and telemetry, or orphaning them (rides list, retention sweep and gallery export all depend on this directory). Raising minSdk to 29 drops support for Android 7-9 devices. Either choice is a product decision for the owner. | Fixing it means relocating existing riders' recordings, or raising minSdk and dropping Android 7-9 users. It a…
- **Fix note:** owner-only (decision 7)

#### F037

**On Android every finished segment is copied rather than renamed because CameraX's cache dir and the recordings dir are on different mounts**

Severity: **medium** · Category: performance · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:761` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:662`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1097`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1457`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:233`

- **What is wrong:** CameraX writes each segment with File.createTempFile(..., getContext().getCacheDir()), i.e. /data/user/0/<pkg>/cache. The recordings root is getExternalStorageDirectory(), i.e. /storage/emulated/0/Android/data/<pkg>/files. Dart's File.rename cannot move a file between file systems (its own doc says to copy instead), and the kernel refuses rename(2) across these mounts. _persistSegmentToRecordingsDirectory therefore falls into its catch, copies the whole segment, then deletes the source. That doubles flash writes per segment (encoder write plus copy read and write) while the encoder writes the next segment. The copy also sits inside the timed part of registration: the segment's endMs, and therefore the next segment's startMs, is stamped only after the copy finishes.
- **Failure scenario:** With default 300 s segments, every 5 minutes a ~300 MB file is read and re-written while recording continues: about 2x write amplification, plus extra power and heat over a long ride. Because _registerCompletedSegment computes endMs after persisting (lines 1085-1100), segment N+1's startMs is late by the copy time (device-dependent, likely seconds), so in playback the speed and GPS overlay of every segment after the first leads the video by that amount. If the rider presses STOP right after a roll, stopRecording waits at most 20 x 100 ms for _isRollingSegment (lines 1457-1461) and can return lastSessionSegmentPaths before the rolled segment is appended. That segment is then left out of the ride's telemetry and becomes an unreferenced file.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:662-664 'baseDirectory = (await getExternalStorageDirectory()) ?? await getApplicationDocumentsDirectory();'; :761 'final movedFile = await sourceFile.rename(targetPath);'; :771-777 'catch (renameError) { ... final copiedFile = await sourceFile.copy(targetPath); ... await sourceFile.delete();'. camera_android_camerax-0.7.1 SystemServicesManager.java:57 'final File path = File.createTempFile(prefix, suffix, getContext().getCacheDir());'. sky_engine/lib/io/file.dart:277-279 'On some platforms, a rename operation cannot move a file between different file systems. If that is the case, instead [copy] the file to the new location and then remove the original.' camera_provider.dart:1085-1086 persist first, then :1098-1100 'DateTime.now().difference(_recordingStartTime!).inMilliseconds'. Not run on a device; the cross-mount layout is standard Android.
- **Verifier corrections:** The timeline skew does not accumulate: it equals the previous boundary's persist (copy) time for each segment. The STOP race is timing-dependent. If stopRecording's own stopVideoRecording finds no active recording, the plugin throws CameraException code 'videoRecordingFailed', not 'No video is recording', so the race branch at 1474 is not taken and the outer catch returns _sessionSegmentPaths.last. | Stop-race mechanics: when STOP lands after the roll's restart, stopRecording stops and registers N+1 while N's copy is still running. N can then be appended after N+1 (the timeline is out of order) or after the caller has read the session paths. When STOP lands between the roll's stop and resta…
- **Recommendation:** Relocate the Android recordings root to internal storage (`getApplicationSupportDirectory()`, same mount as the cache dir) so the rename is O(1); this is the same change as F036. Independently of that, in camera_provider.dart stamp segment boundaries in `_rollSegmentIfNeeded` at the stop and restart instants and pass them into `_registerCompletedSegment`. Replace the 2 s polling in stopRecording with an awaited Completer for the in-flight roll.
- **Owner decision:** Removing the copy requires relocating the recordings root (and existing recordings) to internal storage, which is the same owner decision as F036. The timestamp and Completer fixes need no decision. | Removing the copy requires relocating the recordings root and migrating existing recordings, which is the same owner decision as F036. The timestamp and stop-await fix needs no owner decision.
- **Fix note:** boundaries stamped at stop/restart; same-mount relocation is F036 (owner)

#### F038

**Recording, retention, playback-timeline, persistence and settings-migration logic is untested (about 18% line coverage)**

Severity: **medium** · Category: testing · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1001` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1180`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1444`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:355`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1047`, `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:246`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:362`, `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:72`, `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:146` …

- **What is wrong:** The suite has 11 tests in 3 files. With `flutter test --coverage`, 748 of 4164 lines in the loaded files are hit (18.0%). location_service.dart and camera_preview_pip.dart are never even loaded. Per file: - camera_provider: 22/747 lines (2.9%). The hits are only the constructor, a few getters, presetForResolution, resolvePresetForRequestedResolution and dispose. These all have 0 hits: _rollSegmentIfNeeded (1001-1048), _registerCompletedSegment with the rolling deletion (1081-1155), _enforceRecordingRetention (1180-1256), _pruneOrphanTelemetryFiles (1258-1298), _persistSegmentToRecordingsDirectory (734-790) and stopRecording (1444-1532). - ride_playback_screen: 2/850. Timeline synthesis (355-434), the missing-range merge (459-509) and _locateSegmentForGlobalPosition (1047-1071) are not exercised. - app_settings_provider: 19/199. load(), including the legacy-key migration at 246-275, has 0 hits. - telemetry_provider: 157/363. stopRideSessionAndPersist (362-460) and its helpers (462-698) have 0 hits. - integrity_utils.writeTelemetryAtomically (72-95): 0 hits. - ride_record: 201/358. sampleForPosition, the successful setLockState write and 7 of the 8 issue codes (lines 685, 691, 697,…
- **Failure scenario:** A refactor changes the rolling-limit condition or the ordering used by the directory-wide sweep (camera_provider.dart:1117-1138, 1209-1234). The change deletes incident-locked or other rides' segments, while all 11 tests and CI stay green and the build ships. The same applies to a typo in a SharedPreferences key or in the minutes-to-seconds migration (app_settings_provider.dart:258-266), which would silently reset every rider's settings.
- **Evidence:** Command: `flutter test --no-pub --coverage --coverage-path C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/coverage/lcov.info`, run in a scratch copy of lib/ and test/; 11 tests passed. Summary in C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/coverage/summary.txt: 'TOTAL 748/4164 = 18.0%'. The only camera_provider lines hit are 40, 108-112, 160-161, 199-206, 227-231 and 1621-1628. `grep -rn "setMockInitialValues|setMockMethodCallHandler|TestDefaultBinaryMessenger" test` finds nothing.
- **Recommendation:** Add focused unit tests under test/. First, rolling-limit and retention deletion against temp directories, asserting that locked and other-session files survive. Then `AppSettingsProvider.load` with `SharedPreferences.setMockInitialValues` for the legacy keys, and the playback timeline gap and overlap cases. Finally, add `--coverage` with a floor to the CI flutter test step.

#### F039

**Each segment roll stops and re-binds CameraX before restarting, losing roughly a second of footage at every boundary (7-19% with the 5-15 s segment options)**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1012` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **deferred**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1018`, `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:50`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:177`, `release-notes/v0.1.12.md:61`

- **What is wrong:** _rollSegmentIfNeeded awaits stopVideoRecording() and only then startVideoRecording(). In camera_android_camerax 0.7.1, stop closes the recording, waits for VideoRecordEventFinalize and unbinds the VideoCapture use case. Start re-binds VideoCapture (a camera session reconfiguration), creates a new temp file, prepares and starts a new Recording and waits for VideoRecordEventStart. Nothing is captured in between, so the gap is lost footage. The app already defers registration until after the restart, so the remaining gap is inside the plugin and cannot be overlapped with a single VideoCapture. release-notes/v0.1.12.md:61 reports ~1000-1300 ms on target devices; this was not measured here. Per-roll timings are recorded by _recordRollMetrics but never displayed or persisted, and the timeline does not mark the gaps.
- **Failure scenario:** Using the release-notes figure (~1.15 s per roll): at the default 300 s segments ~0.4% of footage is missing, so an incident at a segment boundary can be absent from the recording. The settings sheet offers 5, 10 and 15 s segments. At those values about 19%, 10% and 7% of all footage is dropped, with a full CameraX rebind every few seconds (extra power and preview hitches) and no warning to the user.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1012 'final file = await _controller!.stopVideoRecording();' then :1018 'await _controller!.startVideoRecording();'. android_camera_camerax.dart:1234-1236 'await recording!.close(); ... while (event is! VideoRecordEventFinalize)', :1252 'await _unbindUseCaseFromLifecycle(videoCapture!);', :1176 'await _bindUseCaseToLifecycle(videoCapture!, options.cameraId);', :1186 'getTempFilePath(', :1212 'while (event is! VideoRecordEventStart)'. app_settings_provider.dart:50-60 segmentDurationOptionsSeconds [5, 10, 15, 30, 60, 120, 180, 300, 600]. release-notes/v0.1.12.md:61 '~1000-1300ms (camera encoder initialization baseline on target devices)'. camera_provider.dart:177-193 stores the metrics; the getters at :135-147 have no reader (camera map, grep).
- **Verifier corrections:** The release-notes figure is the total roll time, which by the instrumentation's own definition includes the register/persist step. Footage is actually lost only during the stop (finalize + unbind) and restart portions, so the per-roll gap and the 7-19% percentages may be overstated. The lost-footage mechanism itself is real. | The release-notes sentence is ambiguous: it describes total roll latency, which includes registration, and it says 'reduces' while the numbers go up. The capture gap is roughly stopMs + restartMs, not the total, so the 7-19% figures for 5-15 s segments are upper-bound estimates until the gap is measured on a device.
- **Recommendation:** Minimal fix: drop the 5/10/15 s values from `segmentDurationOptionsSeconds` in app_settings_provider.dart, and clamp legacy saved values to at least 30 s in `load()`. In camera_provider.dart, record each roll's stopMs + restartMs gap in the segment timeline (or sidecar) so playback can mark it. Gapless recording needs a native MediaCodec/MediaMuxer rotation, which is a separate project.
- **Fix note:** deferred by owner (decision 5)

#### F040

**recoverRecordingPipeline registers (copies) the segment and runs a full telemetry prune between stop and restart, widening the footage gap**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1064` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1012`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1153`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:264`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:283`

- **What is wrong:** RecordingScreen calls recoverRecordingPipeline on every resume after at least 8 s in the background while recording, without detecting an actual stall. _rollSegmentIfNeeded restarts first and registers later; recovery does the opposite. It awaits the non-deferred _registerCompletedSegment between stop and start. That persists the segment (a full-file copy on Android, see the copy finding), runs the per-session loop, and awaits _enforceRecordingRetention(includeTelemetryPrune: true), which reads and jsonDecodes every stored ride file. Only then does it call startVideoRecording(). Nothing is recorded during that time.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider rides with the screen off and wakes the phone after 4 minutes. The stopped segment (up to ~300 MB at the requested bitrate) is copied and every ride JSON is parsed before recording resumes, so several seconds of riding, on top of the normal ~1 s CameraX restart, are missing. This happens exactly when the rider interacts with the phone, and repeats on each screen-on or app switch longer than 8 s.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1063-1066 'final file = await _controller!.stopVideoRecording(); await _registerCompletedSegment(file.path); await _controller!.startVideoRecording(); _armSegmentTimer();' versus the roll at :1012-1029 (restart before register, deferMaintenance: true); :1152-1153 non-deferred -> 'await _enforceRecordingRetention(includeTelemetryPrune: true);'; :1267-1268 readAsString + jsonDecode per telemetry file. recording_screen.dart:249-268 triggers recovery on background duration only; :31-32 threshold 'Duration(seconds: 8)'.
- **Verifier corrections:** The copy is conditional: rename is tried first (camera_provider.dart:761) and copy is only the fallback. It is a full copy on Android because the cache temp file and the external app-files directory are on different filesystems. The gap also includes the time the stop itself takes to finalize, not only register and prune. | The copy happens because rename fails with EXDEV and the code falls back to copy at :771-788. It is not an unconditional copy: when getExternalStorageDirectory() returns null, the recordings directory is on /data and rename succeeds. When mirroring the roll path, the fix must also keep a guard before the restart. As written, recovery restarts recording without checking `…
- **Recommendation:** In camera_provider.dart recoverRecordingPipeline, call stopVideoRecording, then call startVideoRecording and _armSegmentTimer only if isRecording is still true, then run `_registerCompletedSegment(file.path, deferMaintenance: true)`. Optionally gate the trigger in recording_screen.dart on a real stall check, such as the current temp file not growing, rather than on background duration alone.
- **Fix note:** recovery shares the roll cycle; no telemetry prune

#### F041

**Segment timeline is stamped at registration time (after restart and copy) against the camera's start clock while telemetry counts from a later ride start, so boundaries drift, the final segment has zero length and the playback overlay misaligns**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1098` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1011-1031`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:300-303`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:220-260`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1240`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:872-879`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1012-1029`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1097-1109`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:403-413` …

- **What is wrong:** _registerCompletedSegment sets endMs = now - _recordingStartTime at the moment it runs (1097-1101) and uses that value as the next segment's startMs (1109). For rolls, that moment comes after the restart and after the file is persisted (1016-1029; a full copy on Android). So segment k's stored endMs is late by restart plus copy time. Segment k+1's stored startMs is late by roughly the copy time compared with its real start, because the restart happened before the copy. stopRecording clears _recordingStartTime (1487) before it registers the final segment (1494), so the final entry is {startMs: x, endMs: x}. The camera's zero point (872, set before startVideoRecording) also differs from telemetry's ride zero, which startRideSession sets later (telemetry_provider.dart:302; recording_screen.dart:403-413). These values are stored and covered by the integrity payload hash (telemetry_provider.dart:427, 437-440). Playback places each segment at its stored startMs (ride_playback_screen.dart:233-235) and matches telemetry samples by elapsedMs.
- **Failure scenario:** A 30-min ride with 5-min 1080p segments: each segment from the second onward is placed several seconds late on the ride timeline. In playback, the speed and position overlay and the map marker are offset by seconds from the video, which matters when reviewing the speed at an incident. The last segment's stored span is 0 ms, so any consumer of segmentTimeline sees the ride ending at the last roll.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1097-1101 `final startMs = _lastSegmentEndMs; final endMs = _recordingStartTime == null ? startMs : DateTime.now().difference(_recordingStartTime!).inMilliseconds;`; 1486-1494 `_state = RecordingState.stopped; _recordingStartTime = null; ... await _registerCompletedSegment(cacheVideoPath);`; ride_playback_screen.dart:233-235 `var offset = timelineEntry != null ? Duration(milliseconds: timelineEntry.startMs) : runningFallbackOffset;`.
- **Verifier corrections:** Playback partly hides the zero-length final entry: _buildEffectiveTimelineEntries forces endMs >= startMs+1 (ride_playback_screen.dart ~389), and the offset loop uses max(timelineEnd, offset + probed duration) (~247-250). The playback screen therefore still shows the full last segment. The stored sidecar value is still wrong and is covered by the hash. Per-boundary error is bounded by that roll's copy time and does not accumulate. 'Several seconds' depends on copy throughput (roughly 1-3 s for a 300 MB file on typical flash). | Playback already partly masks the zero-length final entry. ride_playback_screen.dart:246-250 uses `effectiveEnd = max(timelineEnd, offset + actual duration)` and :39…
- **Recommendation:** In camera_provider.dart, record `stoppedAtMs` right after stopVideoRecording returns and `startedAtMs` right after startVideoRecording returns, and pass both into _registerCompletedSegment instead of reading DateTime.now() there. In stopRecording, compute the final segment's end before nulling _recordingStartTime. Use a single ride-zero timestamp, set by CameraProvider and handed to TelemetryProvider.startRideSession, for both timelines.
- **Fix note:** segment timeline stamped from real start/stop instants

#### F042

**When locked segments fill the loop budget, the per-registration trim deletes each newly registered unlocked segment immediately, leaving no normal loop footage**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1118` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1193-1234`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1300-1322`

- **What is wrong:** The trim removes the first unlocked entry of _sessionSegmentPaths (1118-1119), which is meant to be the oldest one. Locked entries are never removed. Once a ride has _maxRollingSegments locked segments, the list contains only them. When a new unlocked segment registers, the only unlocked entry is that newest one, so it is removed and its file deleted immediately (1126-1134). The loop buffer for normal footage drops to zero. markIncident's protectPastSegments (1305-1313) then locks the last entries in the list, which are old segments that are already locked, so the footage just before a new incident no longer exists. The pending lock on the current segment then creates the 'N+1 locked' state that triggers the directory-wide sweep.
- **Failure scenario:** Loop 6, 5-min segments. Two incidents spaced a few segments apart lock 6 segments in total. From then on, every completed segment is deleted as soon as it is registered. A crash happens at 10:01, inside the segment that started at 10:00. The 09:55-10:00 segment was deleted at 10:00, so the incident's lead-up is gone. Only the current segment gets a pending lock, and when it registers, the retention sweep deletes earlier rides (see the retention-sweep finding).
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1117-1119 `while (_sessionSegmentPaths.length > _maxRollingSegments) { final removableIndex = _sessionSegmentPaths.indexWhere((path) => !_lockedSegmentPaths.contains(path));`; 1126 `final oldestPath = _sessionSegmentPaths.removeAt(removableIndex);`; 1130-1132 `if (await oldestFile.exists()) { await oldestFile.delete();`; 1306-1313 markIncident locks indices from `_sessionSegmentPaths.length - clampedPast` to the end of the list.
- **Verifier corrections:** The pending lock is applied at :1111-1115, before the trim at :1117, so a segment that carries a pending incident lock survives. What gets deleted is the unlocked footage leading up to the next incident, not the incident segment itself. The cross-ride directory sweep is a separate finding and is not counted here. The '24 newer segments' wording elsewhere assumes the default loop size.
- **Recommendation:** In camera_provider.dart _registerCompletedSegment, count only unlocked paths against _maxRollingSegments (e.g. `while (unlockedCount > _maxRollingSegments)`), and never pick the path that was just appended as the removal candidate. Optionally show the rider a notice when locks reach the loop size.
- **Fix note:** only unlocked segments count/evict

#### F043

**markIncident's pending-lock counter is not tied to a segment, so during a roll the current-segment lock lands on the previous segment and repeated presses pre-lock future segments**

Severity: **medium** · Category: concurrency · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1316` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1001-1048`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1824-1834`

- **What is wrong:** markIncident locks the last N registered paths and increments a bare counter that the next _registerCompletedSegment calls consume (1111-1115, 1305-1317). During a roll, the camera is already recording segment k+1 (restart at 1018) while segment k is still being persisted and registered (1025-1029); on Android that is typically a full copy. An incident in that window locks k-2 and k-1 directly, and k through the counter. Segment k+1, which contains the incident moment, is not locked and is later subject to loop deletion. Because the counter is not tied to a particular segment, each extra LOCK press within one segment pre-locks one more future segment, using up loop budget (see the loop-budget finding).
- **Failure scenario:** A crash is detected 2 s after a 5-min boundary, while the previous segment is still being copied. Segments k-2 through k are locked; the segment that shows the crash and its aftermath is not. It is deleted once 24 newer unlocked segments exist.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1315-1317 `if (protectCurrentSegment && isRecording) { _pendingIncidentSegmentLocks++; }`; 1111-1113 `if (_pendingIncidentSegmentLocks > 0) { _lockedSegmentPaths.add(persistedVideoPath); _pendingIncidentSegmentLocks--;`; the restart at 1018 `await _controller!.startVideoRecording();` comes before 1026 `await _registerCompletedSegment(file.path, deferMaintenance: true);`.
- **Verifier corrections:** The recommendation's alternative ('register the stopped file before restarting') conflicts with the F040 fix and would widen the restart gap, so it should be dropped. Tie the pending lock to the segment instead. | The recommendation's alternative, registering before the restart, would widen the recording gap (see F040) and should not be used. The 'deleted once 24 newer unlocked segments exist' figure assumes the default loop size and continued recording in the same ride.
- **Recommendation:** In camera_provider.dart, assign a sequence id at each start/restart (startRecording, roll, recovery) and keep the in-progress id in a field. markIncident adds that id to a Set of pending locks, so repeat presses are idempotent, and _registerCompletedSegment locks a segment only when its id is in the set. Capture the id of the segment being registered before the restart.
- **Fix note:** per-segment lock sequence numbers

#### F044

**stopRecording waits only 2 s for an in-flight segment roll and then stops and registers concurrently, which can drop or misorder the last segment or hang the stop awaiting a second finalize event**

Severity: **medium** · Category: concurrency · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1458` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1001-1048`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:865-880`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1458`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1012`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1469`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:863`

- **What is wrong:** stopRecording polls _isRollingSegment 20 times at 100 ms (1457-1461) and then proceeds no matter what. On Android, a roll's register step copies the whole finished segment into the recordings directory (761-788), which can take more than 2 s. Case 1, the roll has not restarted yet: the stop's stopVideoRecording hits 'No video is recording' and falls back to _sessionSegmentPaths.last, i.e. segment k-1 (1474-1477). It returns while k is still copying. RecordingScreen snapshots the lists right away (recording_screen.dart:866-878), so k is missing from the sidecar and becomes an unreferenced file.
