Case 2, the roll has restarted: the stop stops and registers the short segment k+1 (1493-1494) in parallel with k. The faster copy, k+1, is appended first. Both timeline entries get startMs == endMs because _recordingStartTime was already cleared (1487, 1098-1100).
- **Failure scenario:** The rider taps STOP one second after a 5-min boundary at 1080p, while segment k (hundreds of MB) is being copied. The sidecar either leaves out the ride's last full segment, or lists ..., k+1, k, so playback plays the final two segments out of order.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1457-1461 `var waitAttempts = 0; while (_isRollingSegment && waitAttempts < 20) { await Future<void>.delayed(const Duration(milliseconds: 100)); waitAttempts++; }`; 1474-1477 `if (e.code == 'No video is recording') { ... if (_sessionSegmentPaths.isNotEmpty) { cacheVideoPath = _sessionSegmentPaths.last;`; 1095 `_sessionSegmentPaths.add(persistedVideoPath);` (append order is completion order).
- **Verifier corrections:** Case 1 does not mean the roll 'has not restarted yet'. The roll has finished stopping and will never restart, because _isStoppingRecording is set. There is also a third sub-case (the alt description), which depends on runtime timing. If the roll is still inside the plugin waiting for VideoRecordEventFinalize when the 2 s expire, isRecordingVideo and `recording` are both still set. The stop then calls recording.close() again and queues a second videoRecordingEventStreamQueue.next. The single Finalize goes to the roll's earlier request, so stopRecording never returns and no sidecar is written. That only happens if finalize takes more than 2 s. | Case 1 in the roll path (STOP before the restar…
- **Recommendation:** In camera_provider.dart, store the roll or recovery work as `Future<void>? _inFlightSegmentOp` and await it in stopRecording instead of the 20 x 100 ms poll. In both the roll and recoverRecordingPipeline, call startVideoRecording only if `isRecording` is still true. Have _registerCompletedSegment insert by sequence number rather than append, so completion order cannot reorder segments.
- **Fix note:** stop awaits the in-flight roll

#### F045

**Locked clips, including automatic crash-detector locks, are silently exported with audio to the shared Movies gallery and the public copy is never removed**

Severity: **medium** · Category: privacy · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1505` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:920`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1826`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1385`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:269`, `README.md:159`

- **What is wrong:** At stopRecording(), every locked session segment is queued for export. About two seconds later it is inserted into the shared MediaStore collection Movies/MotoCam/Recordings. That insert works on Android 10+. On API < 29 the copy needs WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, which the app never requests, so the code falls back to scanning the private file (see the MediaScanner finding). Segments get locked by the LOCK button and by the accelerometer crash detector (reason 'auto-crash'). There is no setting, prompt or notice about export: the only feedback is 'Incident detected. Segments locked.' or 'Emergency lock enabled (N)'. app_settings_provider.dart has no export option, and no UI string mentions the gallery. Audio is on by default. Nothing ever deletes the exported copy. Retention and pruning only walk the private directory. Unlock only rewrites lockedSegmentPaths, while telling the rider the clip 'moved to normal loop policy'. There is no delete UI. README.md:159 presents this as 'Optional Public Export ... selected clips'.
- **Failure scenario:** A pothole trips the crash detector on a commute, or the rider taps LOCK to keep a near-miss. At stop, 2-3 segments (5 minutes each by default, with cabin audio) are copied into the public Movies folder. Every gallery app shows them, and any app with media-read permission or a cloud-photo backup can pick them up. Later the rider unlocks the clip in Ride History, expecting it to be overwritten. The private copy may age out, but the public copy stays.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1505-1513: `// In hybrid mode, only explicitly protected clips are auto-exported. ... _enqueueDeferredGalleryExport(exportCandidates);`. :1324-1336 picks the candidates as locked session segments. :1365-1389: `Future<void>.delayed(const Duration(seconds: 2), ...)` then `platform.invokeMethod<String>('exportVideoToGallery', {..., 'relativePath': _galleryRelativePath})`. MainActivity.kt:181-203 performs the MediaStore insert. recording_screen.dart:920-925: `camera.markIncident(... reason: 'auto-crash'); _showInAppNotice('Incident detected. Segments locked.');`. :1826-1833 is the manual LOCK. app_settings_provider.dart:98 `bool _audioEnabled = true;` and :100-101 300 s x 24. `grep -i "export|gallery|public"` on app_settings_provider.dart finds nothing, and `grep -i "gallery|exported|Movies"` over lib/features/recording, lib/features/history and lib/core finds no UI tex…
- **Verifier corrections:** On API < 29 the native branch writes to Movies/MotoCam/Videos, not Recordings (MainActivity.kt:206-207). The Dart fallback copy (_copySegmentToAndroidPublicGallery) targets /storage/emulated/0/Movies/MotoCam/Recordings. Both need a runtime WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE grant that is never requested, so on API 24-28 they fail and the code only media-scans the private file. ride_record.dart:500 also adds the public export directory as a path-resolution root on Android. | The pre-Q branch in MainActivity.kt:205-206 writes to Movies/MotoCam/Videos and ignores relativePath, while Dart's _androidPublicExportDirectory (camera_provider.dart:45-46) and the trusted root in ride_record.dart:71/500 use /stora…
- **Recommendation:** Add an 'Export locked clips to gallery' setting to app_settings_provider.dart and the settings sheet, defaulting to off (or add a per-ride 'Save to gallery' action), and have stopRecording export only when it is enabled. Show a notice when an export happens. Store the content URI returned by exportVideoToGallery in the sidecar, so that Unlock or delete can offer to remove the copy through ContentResolver.delete / createDeleteRequest. Update README.md:159 to match.
- **Owner decision:** Whether public export should be opt-in (off by default) or stay automatic for locked or crash clips is a product and privacy choice. Turning it off changes behaviour that riders who rely on gallery copies of incidents may expect. | Making the export opt-in, or removing it, changes behaviour riders may rely on: locked incident clips would no longer appear in the gallery automatically. The owner ha…
- **Fix note:** gallery export opt-in (default off, persisted toggle); storing content URIs for removal not done

#### F046

**Unlock (setLockState) re-hashes whatever segment bytes exist and re-signs the envelope after only the payload-only list check, sealing swapped or legacy 'Unknown' rides as VERIFIED**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:656` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:626-629`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:815-847`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:66`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:164-169`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:258-273`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:626`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:262`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:656` …

- **What is wrong:** setLockState refuses only when the record's status is 'modified' or the ride has quarantined paths (626-629). The list builds records with fromTelemetryFile and no segment verification (rides_list_screen.dart:66), so that status covers only the payload hash. A ride with altered segment files therefore shows VERIFIED, a ride with no envelope shows Unknown, and in both cases Unlock is enabled (rides_list_screen.dart:164-169, 258). setLockState then rebuilds the envelope with _buildIntegrityMetadataForPayload (656, 815-847). That function hashes whatever bytes are on disk now and recomputes the payload hash. After one tap, full verification reports verified. The notice 'Clip unlocked and moved to normal loop policy' (rides_list_screen.dart:269) is also wrong: no retention code reads the saved lock state (camera_provider.dart:1180-1298 reads only segmentPaths).
- **Failure scenario:** A segment of a locked incident ride is replaced or corrupted on disk. Playback would warn 'segment-hash-mismatch'. If the rider taps Unlock in Ride History, the envelope is regenerated over the altered bytes. From then on the ride shows VERIFIED in the list and passes playback verification, and the evidence of tampering is gone. A legacy ride with no envelope is likewise upgraded from Unknown to Verified.
- **Evidence:** Re-ran scratch C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/exp/motocam/test/exp_history_test.dart: 'B lazy status=RideIntegrityStatus.verified locked=true', 'B full status=RideIntegrityStatus.modified issues=[segment-hash-mismatch]', 'B after-unlock full status=RideIntegrityStatus.verified locked=false'; 'C before status=RideIntegrityStatus.unknown', then 'C after status=RideIntegrityStatus.verified'. Code 656: 'final integrity = await _buildIntegrityMetadataForPayload(json);'.
- **Verifier corrections:** Unlock is reachable only for rides that are currently locked (the button renders only when ride.isLocked), so the legacy-Unknown upgrade applies to locked legacy rides. The loop-policy claim is wrong in a stronger sense than stated: retention protects locked files neither before nor after unlock, so lock and unlock make no difference to deletion. | Retention is worse than 'lock state is not read'. The directory-wide pass in _enforceRecordingRetention (camera_provider.dart:1210-1225) deletes the oldest videos without checking any lock, so 'Locked' rides from earlier sessions are not protected either way. That probably deserves a separate finding.
- **Recommendation:** In ride_record.dart setLockState, re-read the JSON and run _verifyIntegrity(verifySegments: true) on the trusted segment paths. Return without writing unless the result is verified. When it is verified, keep the existing integrity.segmentHashes and recompute only payloadHash rather than calling _buildIntegrityMetadataForPayload. Have setLockState return a bool so rides_list_screen.dart can show 'Unlocked' or 'Unlock refused: integrity check failed', and drop the loop-policy wording.
- **Fix note:** setLockState re-verifies and never re-seals unverified data

#### F048

**Opening a ride re-hashes every segment on the UI isolate, unawaited and never cancelled, only to drive a one-shot SnackBar**

Severity: **medium** · Category: performance · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:61` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:734`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:702`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:625`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:262`, `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:65`

- **What is wrong:** initState fires unawaited(_verifySegmentsAsync()) alongside _initializePlayback(). verifySegmentIntegrity re-reads and re-decodes the whole telemetry JSON, recomputes its canonical hash, then hashes every trusted declared segment (_verifyIntegrity with verifySegments: true) using the same pure-Dart SHA-256 measured at 26-30 MB/s on an i7-1165G7. Nothing checks mounted or _isDisposing between segments; the only mounted check runs after the whole job (line 67). Leaving the screen therefore does not stop it, and each re-open starts another full pass. The verified RideRecord is read only by _showIntegrityWarningIfNeeded. It is not persisted, and Ride History never shows it. Unlocking a clip (rides_list_screen.dart:262 -> setLockState) also re-hashes every segment to rebuild the integrity block, although only lockedSegmentPaths changed, with no progress indicator or re-entry guard.
- **Failure scenario:** Opening a 24-segment ride (~7.2 GB at the requested bitrate) keeps the UI thread processing a 64 KB hash chunk every few milliseconds for roughly 4+ minutes at the measured rate, while ExoPlayer probes and then plays. Playback, the 10 Hz overlay and scrubbing stutter and the device heats up. Backing out after 20 s and opening two more rides leaves three full hash jobs running concurrently on the same thread. Tapping 'Unlock clip' twice starts two concurrent full re-hashes and rewrites of the same file.
- **Evidence:** ride_playback_screen.dart:61 'unawaited(_verifySegmentsAsync());'; :66-67 'final verifiedRide = await _activeRide.verifySegmentIntegrity(); if (!mounted) {'; _activeRide is read only at :82-96 (grep). ride_record.dart:766-770 '_verifyIntegrity(json: json, segmentPaths: declaredSegmentPaths, verifySegments: true)'; :703-710 'for (final segmentPath in segmentPaths) { ... final actualHash = await _hashFileSha256(segmentPath);'. ride_record.dart:656 'final integrity = await _buildIntegrityMetadataForPayload(json);' -> :831-832 'for (final segmentPath in segmentPaths) { final hash = await _hashFileSha256(segmentPath);'. rides_list_screen.dart:258-262 onPressed '... await ride.setLockState(false);' with no in-flight flag.
- **Verifier corrections:** Segments with no expected hash are skipped without being hashed (ride_record.dart:705-708), so only hashed segments cost time. The line numbers cited for ride_record.dart are accurate.
- **Recommendation:** In ride_record.dart, run the segment-hash loop inside Isolate.run (pass the paths and return the issues) so the UI isolate is not blocked, and let a cancellation flag be checked between segments. Also cache results keyed by path+size+mtime. In setLockState, reuse the existing integrity.segmentHashes for files whose size and mtime are unchanged and recompute only payloadHash. In rides_list_screen.dart, disable the Unlock button while a setLockState call is in flight.
- **Fix note:** hashing off UI isolate + cache; in-loop cancellation/lazy verification not done

#### F049

**Playback says it 'continues with trusted segments only' but never filters the playlist by the verification result, playing hash-mismatched or unhashed segments unmarked**

Severity: **medium** · Category: security · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:111` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:348`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:66`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:717`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:111`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:734-793`

- **What is wrong:** When verifySegmentIntegrity() reports issues, the snackbar says 'Playback continues with trusted segments only.' The playlist, however, comes from widget.ride.segmentPaths plus videoPath (:152, :336-352) and is never filtered by the verification result; _activeRide is used only to decide whether to show the message. Segments flagged segment-hash-mismatch or missing-segment-hash are probed and played seamlessly, and the seekbar does not mark them. Only quarantined paths are excluded, and RideRecord had already dropped those at load. The message also counts quarantine twice, because issues already include 'quarantined-segment-paths:N' and the quarantined paths are counted again. A merely deleted segment ('missing-segment-file') is reported as a 'Security warning'.
- **Failure scenario:** Segment 5 of a ride is replaced with an edited clip and the hashes are not updated. On open, verification adds segment-hash-mismatch:<seg5>, and the rider reads 'Security warning: this ride shows 1 integrity issue. Playback continues with trusted segments only.' The edited segment 5 then plays as part of the timeline, so the rider believes everything on screen passed verification.
- **Evidence:** ride_playback_screen.dart:110-112: 'Security warning: this ride shows $detail. Playback continues with trusted segments only.'. :152 `final declaredPaths = _orderedDeclaredSegmentPaths();`. :348-351: `for (final path in widget.ride.segmentPaths) { addPath(path); } addPath(widget.ride.videoPath);`. :66-73: `_activeRide = verifiedRide;` followed only by `_showIntegrityWarningIfNeeded()`. `grep _activeRide` shows it is used only at :57-98. ride_record.dart:716-718 adds the 'segment-hash-mismatch:' issue. ride_record.dart:369-372 appends the quarantine issue while ride_playback_screen.dart:96-99 counts quarantined paths again.
- **Verifier corrections:** The playlist is built at :336-352 (_orderedDeclaredSegmentPaths), called from :152. Quarantined paths are already excluded from segmentPaths when RideRecord loads (ride_record.dart:314-319), as the finding says. One caveat: the hashes are unkeyed SHA-256, so someone who can edit segments can also recompute the envelope. This limits the security impact but not the misleading message. | Additional mechanism: _hasShownIntegrityWarning is set by the synchronous call in initState (line 60). If the stored integrity is already 'modified', the per-segment result from verification never produces a message of its own.
- **Recommendation:** In ride_playback_screen.dart, change the SnackBar text so it states the truth, e.g. 'N segments failed verification', and count quarantine once (skip issues that start with 'quarantined-segment-paths:'). Also gather the paths flagged segment-hash-mismatch or missing-segment-hash from _activeRide.integrity.issues and draw them on the seekbar the same way missing ranges are drawn. Keep 'missing-segment-file' out of the 'Security warning' wording.
- **Fix note:** failed-check segments marked purple, honest wording

#### F050

**Opening a ride initializes a native video player for every segment sequentially just to read durations before the first frame, and keeps probing after the user leaves**

Severity: **medium** · Category: performance · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:170` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:511`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:530`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:204`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:287`

- **What is wrong:** _initializePlayback probes each existing declared segment with _probeSegmentDuration: VideoPlayerController.file(...).initialize() then dispose(), sequentially, only to read the duration. An unreadable trailing segment gets a 300 ms wait and a second probe. Only afterwards does _loadSegment(index: 0) initialise segment 0 again. On Android, each initialize() creates an ExoPlayer and a Flutter SurfaceProducer, calls prepare() and waits for STATE_READY. Each dispose() calls exoPlayer.release() on the platform thread, which on Flutter 3.41 is also the UI thread. The loop never checks mounted or _isDisposing, and a spinner covers the whole phase. The stored segmentTimeline already holds startMs and endMs for every segment.
- **Failure scenario:** A default 24-segment ride needs 25 sequential native player initialisations and 24 synchronous release() calls on the merged UI thread before anything is shown. At an estimated, not measured, 100-300 ms per probe that is ~2.5-7.5 s of spinner on every open, on top of the concurrent full-ride hashing. Backing out after 2 s does not stop the loop: the remaining probes keep creating decoders and textures while the next screen is shown. If every segment turns out to be unreadable, the method then calls setState at line 204 on a disposed State.
- **Evidence:** ride_playback_screen.dart:161-173 'for (var index = 0; index < declaredPaths.length; index++) { ... final probe = await _probeSegmentDuration(path, retryOnFailure: isTrailingSegment,'; :511-527 'final probe = VideoPlayerController.file(File(path)); try { await probe.initialize(); ... } finally { await probe.dispose(); }'; :539 300 ms retry; :287 'await _loadSegment(index: 0, autoPlay: false);'; :204 'setState(() {' with no mounted check. video_player_android-2.9.4 VideoPlayer.java:76-77 'exoPlayer.setMediaItem(mediaItem); exoPlayer.prepare();', :240 'exoPlayer.release();'; ExoPlayerEventListener.java:61-65 sends initialized on Player.STATE_READY; VideoPlayerPlugin.java:108 'createSurfaceProducer()'. Flutter engine FlutterLoader.java:432-434 'DisableMergedPlatformUIThread ... is no longer allowed.'
- **Verifier corrections:** The probes also detect unreadable segments, which feeds the 'unreadable' count and the playlist. Taking durations from segmentTimeline alone would lose that check, so unreadable files would need to be handled lazily at load time. The 'merged UI/platform thread' detail matters only for how much jank there is, not for whether the defect exists. | The setState-after-dispose at line 204 needs a ride in which every segment is unreadable and the user leaving during probing. It is logged as an async error rather than crashing the app.
- **Recommendation:** In _initializePlayback, take durations from widget.ride.segmentTimeline (endMs - startMs) when an entry exists and the file exists. Probe only segments with no timeline entry. Add 'if (!mounted || _isDisposing) return;' inside the loop and before the setState at line 204.
- **Fix note:** durations from stored timeline; probes only legacy/zero-length entries

#### F051

**Playback has no error state: when no segment is playable or segment 0 fails to load, _controller stays null and the screen spins forever while SnackBars claim playback is running**

Severity: **medium** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1231` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:287-293`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:301-334`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:682-747`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:768-824`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1231`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:198-207`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:287-298`

- **What is wrong:** The body shows a spinner whenever `_isLoading || controller == null` (1231-1232). If no declared segment is playable, `_initializePlayback` sets `_isLoading = false` and returns with `_controller` still null (198-207). If segment 0 fails to load after a good probe, `_loadSegment` returns false and `_controller` likewise stays null (287, 601-603). In both cases the screen spins indefinitely, with no error text, retry or explanation, while a 4 s SnackBar says '... Playback is running in incomplete mode.' (326-333). Other messages describe the opposite of what happened. When the final segment fails during auto-advance, the SnackBar says 'Final segment could not be opened after retry. Playback continued without it.' (742), but playback actually stops (701-706). A cross-segment seek that fails for every candidate returns silently (814-816), after the thumb has already been moved to the target (777).
- **Failure scenario:** The rider opens a ride whose segment files are unreadable, for example a truncated or unfinalised MP4. They see a spinner that never resolves and a SnackBar saying playback is running in incomplete mode, and their only option is to back out.
- **Evidence:** ride_playback_screen.dart:1231-1232 `body: _isLoading || controller == null ? const Center(child: CircularProgressIndicator()) :`; :198-207 `if (_segmentPaths.isEmpty) { _showInitializationWarning(...); setState(() { _isLoading = false; }); return; }`; :329 `'Some ride segments are unavailable (${details.join(', ')}). Playback is running in incomplete mode.$trailingHint'`; :742 `'Final segment could not be opened after retry. Playback continued without it.'`; :701-706 `if (candidate == _segmentPaths.length - 1) { _showRuntimeTrailingSegmentWarning(); } candidate++; } await _stopPlayback();`; :814-816 `if (!loaded || _isDisposing) { return; }`.
- **Verifier corrections:** The claim that a failed cross-segment seek leaves the thumb at the target is inaccurate. :777 sets _lastKnownGlobalPosition, but _globalPosition() (:749-755) reads the still-initialized previous controller and ignores _lastKnownGlobalPosition. So the thumb snaps back to the real position on the next build, and the failure is just silent. Also, the segment-0 load failing right after a successful probe is a rare path. The realistic trigger is the all-unreadable case, such as truncated MP4s after a crash. | The line-742 wording is only partly wrong. When the final segment fails, the ride really has ended and only that segment is missing, so 'continued without it' is misleading rather than the…
- **Recommendation:** In ride_playback_screen.dart, add a _loadError string. Set it when _segmentPaths is empty, and when every candidate from index 0 upward fails to load (loop the way _advanceToNextSegment does instead of ignoring _loadSegment's result). In build, render an error view with the counts and Back/Retry buttons when _loadError != null. Reword the SnackBars so they describe what happened.
- **Fix note:** explicit no-playable-video view with Retry

#### F052

**The collapsed Route Map stays built inside AnimatedCrossFade, so every ride playback fetches and caches OSM tiles around the route, sending the ride area to a third party without opt-in**

Severity: **medium** · Category: privacy · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1367` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:42`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:121`, `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:64`, `lib/features/map/map_source_config.dart:10`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1367`, `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:51`, `lib/features/map/map_source_config.dart:9`

- **What is wrong:** The route map is collapsed by default: _showRouteMap is false (:42), and the saved preference defaults to false (:121). It is drawn with AnimatedCrossFade, which keeps the hidden firstChild built and laid out. So whenever a ride with 2 or more GPS points is opened, RouteMapView builds a FlutterMap whose TileLayer points at https://tile.openstreetmap.org, with no custom caching provider. A widget harness replicating this subtree with the real RouteMapView measured 9 HTTPS requests for zoom-16 tiles (about 600 m each) around the route while the panel was collapsed, and 24 when expanded. The app never tells the rider that opening a ride sends its location to the OpenStreetMap Foundation's servers. Fetching tiles the user is not viewing also conflicts with OSM's tile usage policy; flutter_map prints its own OSM-policy warning. Fetched tiles go to flutter_map's built-in cache (app cache dir/fm_cache, default cap 1 GB). The app never configures or clears that cache, so the ride area can still be recovered from disk later.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider opens a ride to review the footage and never expands 'Route Map'. The device still requests /16/x/y.png tiles around the ride's centre from tile.openstreetmap.org, sending the device IP and User-Agent 'flutter_map (com.example.motocam)'. This tells a third party where the rider was, and the tiles are written to fm_cache.
- **Evidence:** Scratch harness: flutter test on a copy of lib/ on a Windows host. It replicates ride_playback_screen.dart:1246-1386 (outer Column -> Container -> Column(start) -> AnimatedCrossFade) with the real RouteMapView, because the screen itself cannot be pumped without video_player. HTTP was intercepted with HttpOverrides and path_provider was mocked. Output: "COLLAPSED: 9 requests, 9 distinct tiles: [/16/46892/30384.png, ..., /16/46894/30386.png]" and "EXPANDED: 24 requests, 24 distinct tiles". Counts are from the harness, not captured on a device. Code: ride_playback_screen.dart:1367-1383 `AnimatedCrossFade(... crossFadeState: _showRouteMap ? CrossFadeState.showFirst : CrossFadeState.showSecond, firstChild: Padding(... RouteMapView(telemetryData: widget.ride.samples, ...)), secondChild: const SizedBox.shrink())`. route_map_view.dart:64-69 `TileLayer(urlTemplate: MapSourceConfig.tileUrlTemplat…
- **Verifier corrections:** The privacy exposure is limited to the coarse area of the ride, plus the device IP and User-Agent, going to OSMF. Expanding the map sends the same data by design. The defect is the hidden fetch and the lack of any disclosure or cache control. The request count comes from the scanner's own harness and was not captured on a device. | The request counts come from a widget harness, not from a device. The privacy exposure is the ride's area (tile coordinates around the route centre plus the IP), not a precise track.
- **Recommendation:** In ride_playback_screen.dart, replace the AnimatedCrossFade with AnimatedSize(child: _showRouteMap ? Padding(... RouteMapView(...)) : const SizedBox.shrink()) so the map is only built when expanded. Optionally pass an explicit caching provider with a small maxCacheSize to the TileLayer in route_map_view.dart. A first-use disclosure naming the tile server can follow separately.
- **Fix note:** map built only when expanded, behind one-time consent

#### F053

**Seekbar incident and lock markers are painted by a childless CustomPaint laid out at Size.zero, so they never appear although the legend lists them and their tap targets still seek**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1457` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1451-1467`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1825-1833`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1457`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1811-1884`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:957-1022`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1537-1608`

- **What is wrong:** CustomPaint(painter: _TimelineMarkersPainter(...)) has no child and no size. It is a non-positioned child of a default (StackFit.loose) Stack inside Positioned.fill and a LayoutBuilder (1451-1467). A childless RenderCustomPaint sizes itself to constraints.constrain(Size.zero), which is 0x0 under loose constraints, so paint() returns at 'if (usableWidth <= 0) return;' (1830-1833). The sibling Listener (1468-1519) sizes to the full 336x34, so tap-to-jump still works, but on invisible targets. The markers were shipped as a feature in release-notes/v0.1.9.md:10.
- **Failure scenario:** For a ride with an incident spike or a locked segment, the legend row shows 'Incident' or 'Locked' (1537-1606), but nothing is drawn on the seekbar. The rider cannot see where incidents are or where to tap. Only the speed-graph ticks within +/-30 s of the playhead show them.
- **Evidence:** Re-ran the replica C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/layoutcheck/test/layout_test.dart: 'RESULT sliderRect=Rect.fromLTRB(12.0, 6.0, 348.0, 40.0) customPaintSize=Size(0.0, 0.0) painterSaw=Size(0.0, 0.0) listenerSize=Size(336.0, 34.0)'.
- **Recommendation:** In ride_playback_screen.dart around line 1457, give the painter the full box: wrap the IgnorePointer/CustomPaint in Positioned.fill, or set CustomPaint(size: Size.infinite, ...), or use Stack(fit: StackFit.expand). Add a widget test that checks the painter's laid-out size is non-zero.
- **Fix note:** markers painted inside the slider track shape

#### F054

**Ride History parses every sample (one DateTime.tryParse each) and re-hashes every ride's canonical JSON on the UI isolate before the list appears**

Severity: **medium** · Category: performance · Location: `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:66` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:253`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:559`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:693`, `lib/features/telemetry/models/telemetry_data.dart:107`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:117`

- **What is wrong:** _loadRides awaits RideRecord.fromTelemetryFile for each telemetry file in sequence. Each call reads and jsonDecodes the whole file and converts every sample with TelemetryData.fromJson, which runs one DateTime.tryParse per sample. It then verifies the payload hash by canonicalising the whole JSON (a deep, key-sorted copy), re-encoding it and SHA-256 hashing it. The list keeps every ride's full samples in memory, yet the cards show only aggregates, samples.length and samples.last.elapsedMs. Sample timestamps are only ever interpolated in sampleForPosition and never displayed. Each step is a synchronous block on the UI/platform thread.
- **Failure scenario:** Measured with the repo's TelemetryData and integrity_utils on an i7-1165G7 (AOT): a 2 h ride costs 334 ms (decode 23 + samples 221 + canonical hash 90) and a 10 h ride 3,432 ms (320 + 2,286 + 826). DateTime.tryParse alone takes ~58 us per sample. A history of 20 one-hour rides therefore costs ~3.3 s of UI-thread work on that CPU, more on mid-range phones, delivered as 100-500 ms freezes. This repeats on every visit, pull-to-refresh and unlock. A single very long ride puts a multi-second synchronous loop on the main thread, approaching Android's 5 s input-dispatch ANR limit on slower devices.
- **Evidence:** rides_list_screen.dart:64-66 'for (final telemetryFile in telemetryFiles) { try { final ride = await RideRecord.fromTelemetryFile(telemetryFile);'. ride_record.dart:259-260 'final raw = await telemetryFile.readAsString(); final json = jsonDecode(raw) as Map<String, dynamic>;'; :322 'final samples = _readSamples(json);'; :565-567 'raw.map((key, value) => MapEntry(key.toString(), value)); samples.add(TelemetryData.fromJson(normalized));'; telemetry_data.dart:107 'timestamp: DateTime.tryParse(json['timestamp']?.toString() ?? '') ??'; ride_record.dart:693-695 'Map<String, dynamic>.from(json)..remove('integrity'); final actualPayloadHash = _hashJsonCanonical(payloadWithoutIntegrity);'; :164 is the only use of a sample timestamp. Benchmark (bench/bin/bench.dart, bench2.dart): 'n=7200 ... total load=334ms', 'n=36000 ... jsonDecode=320ms readSamples=2286ms canonicalHash=826ms | total load=3432m…
- **Verifier corrections:** The work is broken into one synchronous block per ride (with await gaps between rides), so it is a series of per-ride freezes rather than a single block, as the finding's '100-500 ms freezes' implies. A single very long ride is the only case near multi-second stalls. The quoted benchmark timings are unverified here. | The list uses ListView with a children list (rides_list_screen.dart:121), but card building is not the main cost. The awaits between rides give the event loop a chance to run, so the result is per-ride stalls rather than one long block, unless a single ride is very long.
- **Recommendation:** Minimal: in ride_record.dart, move the parse-and-verify body of fromTelemetryFile (readAsString result to jsonDecode, _readSamples, _verifyIntegrity payload hash) into `Isolate.run(...)` with plain data across the boundary, so the UI isolate only builds the cards. Follow-up: write a small summary sidecar at stop and load samples lazily in RidePlaybackScreen.
- **Fix note:** history load in Isolate.run; summary sidecar / epoch-ms format not done

#### F055

**'Open Settings' and 'Open Location Settings' re-check as soon as startActivity returns, before the rider comes back, and nothing re-checks on resume**

Severity: **medium** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:166` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:91-113`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:184-197`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:760-826`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:230-251`

- **What is wrong:** 'Open Settings' awaits `openAppSettings()` and then re-runs the full audit immediately (165-173). 'Open Location Settings' awaits `Geolocator.openLocationSettings()` and re-checks straight away (807-812). Both plugin calls resolve as soon as `startActivity` is called (permission_handler_android 13.0.1 AppSettingsManager.java:32-34; geolocator_android 5.0.2 Utils.java:35-36), so the re-check always sees the old state. The bootstrap then shows 'Required permissions missing: Camera' (184-197) and returns without initialising the camera (108-110). The location gate always reports 'Location is still off. Enable it in system settings, then press START RECORDING again.' (817-824). The immediate re-audit also re-requests the recommended permissions and tries to launch the battery dialog while Settings is still in front. When the rider comes back, `didChangeAppLifecycleState` does nothing unless recording is active (249-251), so nothing re-checks permissions or initialises the camera.
- **Failure scenario:** On first launch the rider tapped 'Don't allow' twice for Camera. In the 'Permissions Required' dialog they tap Open Settings, grant Camera and come back. The app shows 'Required permissions missing: Camera' and an endless spinner where the preview should be. Nothing changes until they happen to press START RECORDING.
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:165-172 `case _PermissionRecoveryAction.openSettings: await openAppSettings(); if (!mounted) { return resolvedAudit; } resolvedAudit = await _permissionService.ensureRequiredPermissions(`; :807-812 `await Geolocator.openLocationSettings(); ... final enabledAfterPrompt = await Geolocator.isLocationServiceEnabled();`; :249-251 `if (state != AppLifecycleState.resumed || !camera.isRecording) { return; }`; permission_handler_android-13.0.1 AppSettingsManager.java:32-34 `context.startActivity(settingsIntent); successCallback.onSuccess(true);`; geolocator_android-5.0.2 Utils.java:35-36 `context.startActivity(settingsIntent); return true;` (both versions are the ones resolved in pubspec.lock).
- **Verifier corrections:** Pressing START RECORDING does recover the camera, because the preflight initialises it, so the 'endless spinner' lasts until the rider presses START, opens History and returns (:1021), or restarts the app. It is not permanent.
- **Recommendation:** In recording_screen.dart, add a Completer<void>? _resumeWaiter that didChangeAppLifecycleState completes on AppLifecycleState.resumed. Await it after openAppSettings() and openLocationSettings() before re-checking. Also, on resume while idle with camera not initialised, call unawaited(_bootstrapRuntimePrerequisites()) or a light permission re-check followed by _initializeCamera().
- **Fix note:** settings return waits for resume and re-inits camera

#### F056

**Stall recovery is inverted: _lastBackgroundedAt is refreshed by the synthesized hidden/inactive on return, so recovery never fires after a real stop but does after 8 s+ of PiP or the notification shade, cutting the recording**

Severity: **medium** · Category: lifecycle · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:243` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1050-1079`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:271-312`

- **What is wrong:** While recording, every inactive, hidden or paused callback overwrites _lastBackgroundedAt (237-245). Android reports onStop as 'paused' (FlutterActivityAndFragmentDelegate.java:642-647). On the way back the framework synthesizes hidden, then inactive, then resumed (services/binding.dart:312-342; enum order is detached, resumed, inactive, hidden, paused). So the timestamp is refreshed milliseconds before 'resumed', and the 8 s threshold (259-262) is never met after a real screen-off or background. Inactive-only periods keep the first timestamp: PiP (onPause becomes inactive, delegate:620-624), and the notification shade or system dialogs (focus loss while resumed becomes inactive, LifecycleChannel.java:84-90). After 8 s or more of those, recoverRecordingPipeline (camera_provider.dart:1050-1079) stops the recording and registers the segment non-deferred. That awaits the cross-mount copy, retention, and a parse of every ride's telemetry JSON (1064, 1152-1154, 1258-1298) before it restarts. The screen then claims 'Video feed may have stalled while screen was off' (294). If the restart fails, _state stays 'recording' with the zombie behaviour described in the roll-restart finding (1073…
- **Failure scenario:** The rider presses Home while recording (auto-PiP) to check Maps for a minute, then expands MotoCam. The recording is cut and restarted with a gap of several seconds (copying the partial segment plus a full telemetry prune). An extra segment counts against the loop buffer, and a false 'screen was off' SnackBar appears. Pulling down the notification shade for 10 s does the same. After a genuine 10-minute screen-off, no recovery is attempted at all.
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:237-245 `if (state == AppLifecycleState.inactive || state == AppLifecycleState.paused || state == AppLifecycleState.hidden) { ... if (camera.isRecording) { _lastBackgroundedAt = DateTime.now(); } return; }`; 259-262 `if (backgroundDuration < _stallRecoveryBackgroundThreshold) { return; }`; Flutter services/binding.dart:333-335 `} else if (previousStateIndex > stateIndex) { for (var i = stateIndex; i < previousStateIndex; ++i) { stateChanges.insert(0, AppLifecycleState.values[i]);`; camera_provider.dart:1063-1065 `final file = await _controller!.stopVideoRecording(); await _registerCompletedSegment(file.path); await _controller!.startVideoRecording();`.
- **Recommendation:** In recording_screen.dart didChangeAppLifecycleState, set _lastBackgroundedAt only on hidden or paused, and only when it is currently null (`_lastBackgroundedAt ??= DateTime.now()`). Ignore inactive. In camera_provider.dart recoverRecordingPipeline, pass deferMaintenance: true to _registerCompletedSegment so the restart is not delayed. Reword the SnackBar.
- **Fix note:** BackgroundStallTracker; recovery without maintenance

#### F057

**_stopRecordingFlow stops the foreground service (releasing the wake lock) before camera.stopRecording and the long telemetry hash/save, which then run unprotected in a background process**

Severity: **medium** · Category: lifecycle · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:863` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:55-63`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:649-670`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:863`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:345-359`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:874-880`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:50-62`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1529-1531`

- **What is wrong:** _stopRecordingFlow stops the FGS, which releases the wake lock, at 863. That happens before camera.stopRecording (865) and before stopRideSessionAndPersist (874-880). The camera stop can wait 2 s for a roll, finalize, copy the final segment and run two retention/prune passes (camera_provider.dart:1457-1515). The persist then SHA-256-hashes every segment of the ride on the UI isolate (telemetry_provider.dart:437-440; integrity_utils.dart:54-67), which is many GB for a long ride. The notification-stop path also tears down the FGS first (BackgroundRecordingService.kt:55-63). Without an FGS, a process whose activity is stopped becomes a cached background process, which Android can freeze or kill before the sidecar is written.
- **Failure scenario:** The rider taps STOP after a two-hour ride and pockets the phone. The screen turns off while the segments are still being hashed. The process is frozen or killed before the .telemetry.json is written, and the ride never appears in history (see the in-memory ride-state finding).
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:861-865 `_recordingTimerSubscription?.cancel(); _recordingTimerSubscription = null; await _backgroundRecordingService.stopForegroundRecording(); final videoPath = await camera.stopRecording();`; telemetry_provider.dart:437-440 `final integrity = await _buildIntegrityMetadata(payload: telemetryPayloadCore, segmentPaths: resolvedSegmentPaths);`, which calls integrity_utils.dart:65 `await sha256.bind(file.openRead()).first`.
- **Verifier corrections:** Freezing a cached process on its own only pauses the work, and the work resumes when the app is reopened. Data is lost only if the process is killed (low-memory killer, or the user swipes the app away) before the .telemetry.json is written. With the screen off and no wake lock, the CPU can also suspend, which stretches the window in which a kill can happen. | The process is not certain to become cached right away. Right after a screen-off it may sit in a top-sleeping or previous-app state, so the main immediate risk is the CPU sleeping once the wake lock is released (the save stalls), with kill or freeze as the data-loss path. The FGS line references overlap (50-54 is ACTION_STOP, 55-63 is…
- **Recommendation:** In recording_screen.dart _stopRecordingFlow, move `await _backgroundRecordingService.stopForegroundRecording()` into the finally block, after stopRideSessionAndPersist and cancelRideSession. In BackgroundRecordingService.kt ACTION_STOP_FROM_NOTIFICATION, only broadcast, and leave the teardown to the Dart-issued ACTION_STOP (keep a timeout fallback). Optionally move segment hashing to Isolate.run.
- **Fix note:** FGS/wake lock held until the ride is saved; hashing in isolate

#### F058

**A single transient camera-init failure marks the preset/FPS unsupported for the process and _syncSettingsWithCameraCapabilities persists the downgraded resolution and FPS over the rider's choice**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:981` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:416-460`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:582-601`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:930-968`

- **What is wrong:** Any initialize() failure removes the FPS from the preset's cached list (camera_provider.dart:439, 582-590) and marks the preset unsupported for that camera (457-459; also 943-946, 967). These marks last for the life of the process and are never re-checked. After every successful init, apply and start, _syncSettingsWithCameraCapabilities writes the negotiated resolution and FPS into SharedPreferences (970-986). So a one-off failure overwrites the rider's choice: the camera briefly busy, a thermal refusal, or the concurrent init from the re-entrancy finding. The next launch requests the downgraded value and it sticks. Also, if the requested FPS is no longer in the cached list, init picks supportedFps.last, the highest remaining rate (419-423), and the FPS cache key ignores which camera is in use (600-601).
- **Failure scenario:** The phone is hot from the sun. At launch the 2160p init fails once and 1080p succeeds. recording_resolution_p becomes 1080, and every later ride records at 1080p with no notice. Variant: 30 fps fails once, so 30 is dropped from [24, 30, 60]; the next init silently records at 60 fps, and that value is then saved.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:439 `_markFpsUnsupportedForPreset(candidatePreset, candidateFps);`, 457-459 `if (!presetInitialized) { _markResolutionUnsupported(selectedCamera.name, candidatePreset); }`, 421-422 `if (!supportedFps.contains(candidateFps)) { candidateFps = supportedFps.last; }`; recording_screen.dart:980-985 `if (settings.recordingResolution != activeResolution) { await settings.updateRecordingResolution(activeResolution); } if (settings.recordingFps != camera.recordingFps) { await settings.updateRecordingFps(camera.recordingFps); }`.
- **Verifier corrections:** The 'marked unsupported for the process' effect on resolution is UI-only (it greys options in the settings sheet and shows the fallback warning). The init fallback order (_resolutionFallbackCandidates) never consults it, and _markResolutionSupported clears it after a later success. The lasting damage comes from the SharedPreferences overwrite and the FPS cache removal, not from the resolution mark. | A camera-busy failure makes every preset fail, so nothing is saved at that moment. The downgrade is saved on the next successful init in the same process, such as the START preflight or a return from history. The resolution cache is keyed by camera name; only the FPS cache ignores the camera. T…
- **Recommendation:** In recording_screen.dart, stop _syncSettingsWithCameraCapabilities from overwriting the rider's saved resolution and FPS. Keep the requested values in AppSettingsProvider, and show the applied values from CameraProvider in the chips or a notice. In camera_provider.dart, pick the nearest FPS (prefer the requested rate, then 30, then the next lower one) instead of supportedFps.last. Include the camera name in the FPS cache key. Clear the unsupported caches when every preset fails, because that pattern means the camera is busy, not that the presets are unsupported.
- **Fix note:** negotiated values no longer overwrite saved settings; nearest FPS + cache reset

#### F059

**The blackout overlay is stacked above the in-app notice overlay and shows no recording status, hiding the exit hint, too-short-tap feedback and incident confirmations**

Severity: **medium** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1055` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:454-520`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1221-1236`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:915-926`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1100-1114`

- **What is wrong:** In the main Stack, `_buildInAppNoticeOverlay()` (1055) comes before `if (_isBlackoutMode) _buildBlackoutOverlay(...)` (1056-1057). The blackout overlay is an opaque `Positioned.fill` GestureDetector over a black Container (1100-1114). Every notice raised during blackout is therefore drawn underneath it: 'Blackout on. Hold 2s anywhere to exit.' (474, issued just after blackout turns on), 'Hold for 2 seconds to exit blackout' (519, the only feedback for a too-short tap) and 'Incident detected. Segments locked.' (925). The overlay also covers the header's 'Exit blackout' button, STOP and LOCK. It shows no REC dot or timer, so a blacked-out recording looks the same as a frozen or crashed app. The exit gesture is a 2 s tap-hold built from onTapDown, onTapUp and onTapCancel, so moving the finger beyond the tap slop cancels it with no visible feedback. That matters on a vibrating handlebar, with gloves.
- **Failure scenario:** The rider enables blackout and rides on. A pothole triggers an auto-lock, and its confirmation is drawn under the black layer. Wanting to press LOCK or STOP, the rider taps the black screen and nothing visible happens: no hint appears, and nothing shows that recording is still running. They assume the app froze and press Home or power, which ends blackout through the lifecycle handler (240-242) but also leaves the app.
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:1045-1057 `children: [ Column(...), _buildInAppNoticeOverlay(), if (_isBlackoutMode) _buildBlackoutOverlay(camera: camera), ]`; :1103-1111 `Positioned.fill(child: GestureDetector(behavior: HitTestBehavior.opaque, onTapDown: (_) => _startBlackoutExitHold(camera: camera), onTapUp: (_) => _endBlackoutExitHold(), onTapCancel: _endBlackoutExitHold, child: Container(color: Colors.black)))`; :471-474 `setState(() { _isBlackoutMode = true; }); _showInAppNotice('Blackout on. Hold 2s anywhere to exit.');`; :519 `_showInAppNotice('Hold for 2 seconds to exit blackout');`; :925 `_showInAppNotice('Incident detected. Segments locked.');`.
- **Verifier corrections:** The lifecycle handler is at about lines 231-236, not 240-242. onTapCancel also routes to _endBlackoutExitHold, so a hold cancelled by finger movement does trigger the 'Hold for 2 seconds' notice, but the rider cannot see it because it is drawn under the black layer.
- **Recommendation:** In recording_screen.dart's build Stack, move _buildInAppNoticeOverlay() after the blackout overlay, so notices draw on top. The notice is already wrapped in IgnorePointer, so the hold gesture still works. In _buildBlackoutOverlay, add a dim REC dot, the elapsed timer and a faint 'Hold 2 s to exit' label. Optionally switch to onLongPress with a 2 s duration, which tolerates more finger movement, and add haptic feedback.
- **Fix note:** blackout shows notices, REC + elapsed and exit hint

#### F060

**Camera settings saved while recording are persisted and shown but never applied after STOP, and the next START's capability sync writes the old resolution and FPS back over them**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1339` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:496-531`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:970-986`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1339`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1237-1240`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1302-1322`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1366-1372`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:496-519`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1370` …

- **What is wrong:** The settings button stays enabled while recording (1237-1240). On SAVE, _openSettingsSheet first persists every value (1302-1322), then calls applyRecordingSettings. While recording, that returns false without storing anything (camera_provider.dart:505-508), and the SnackBar says 'Stop recording first to apply camera settings' (1370-1371). But nothing applies the settings after stop. The next START uses the provider's old configuration, because the preflight only initializes when !isInitialized (569-571). _syncSettingsWithCameraCapabilities then writes the camera's old resolution and FPS back over the saved values (408-411, 970-986). Audio, bitrate, segment length, loop size and camera stay saved but unapplied until a trip to history (1021) or an app restart, while the profile chips (1386-1401) display them.
- **Failure scenario:** Mid-ride, the rider turns Audio off, raises the loop from 6 to 24 and taps SAVE. The chips show 'Audio Off' and 'x 24 loop'. The rider stops and starts a new ride: it still records audio and still trims at 6 segments. The ride JSON shows requestedAudioEnabled=false but appliedAudioEnabled=true (847, 855).
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:1315 `await settings.updateAudioEnabled(result.audioEnabled);` ... 1321 `await settings.updateLoopSegmentCount(result.loopSegmentCount);`, then 1339 `final applied = await camera.applyRecordingSettings(`; camera_provider.dart:505-508 `if (isRecording) { _log('Cannot apply recording settings while recording is active'); return false; }`; recording_screen.dart:569-571 `if (!camera.isInitialized && !camera.isRecording) { await _initializeCamera(); }`.
- **Verifier corrections:** Speed refresh and incident sensitivity do apply immediately mid-ride through telemetry (:1328-1335). Only the CameraProvider-backed settings stay pending. Restarting the app or visiting history applies the saved values. The provider's default loop count is 24, so the '6 to 24' example in the scenario is just an example.
- **Recommendation:** In _startRecordingFlow (recording_screen.dart), before camera.startRecording(), or at the end of _stopRecordingFlow, compare the saved AppSettingsProvider values with the CameraProvider's applied values. If they differ, call camera.applyRecordingSettings with the saved values, so the next ride uses them. Change the mid-ride SnackBar to 'Will apply when recording stops'. Do not disable the settings gear, because the telemetry settings legitimately apply mid-ride.
- **Fix note:** mid-ride settings apply at next START

#### F062

**START/STOP RECORDING and other primary ElevatedButton labels use the default M3 primary foreground on blue/red backgrounds, giving 1.8-2.2:1 contrast**

Severity: **medium** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1735` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/widgets/start_preflight_sheet.dart:135-144`, `lib/features/recording/widgets/recording_settings_sheet.dart:540-565`, `lib/main.dart:59-63`

- **What is wrong:** The filled buttons set only `backgroundColor` in `ElevatedButton.styleFrom`, and their label TextStyles have no colour. The text therefore takes the Material 3 ElevatedButton default foreground, `colorScheme.primary`. With the app theme (`colorSchemeSeed: Colors.blue, brightness: Brightness.dark`, main.dart:59-63) that colour is #A0CAFD, a light blue. Measured contrast: 'START RECORDING' on Colors.blue #2196F3 is 1.84:1; 'STOP RECORDING' on Colors.red #F44336 is 2.17:1; the preflight sheet's 'Start Recording' is 1.84:1; 'SAVE SETTINGS' uses the same style. The 16 dp bold label is below WCAG's large-text threshold (14 pt bold), so the 4.5:1 requirement applies, and the labels fail even the 3:1 large-text minimum. This is the app's main control, read at a glance on a handlebar mount in daylight.
- **Failure scenario:** In bright sun, the rider glances at the mount to see whether the big button says START or STOP. The pale-blue label on a saturated blue or red fill is barely legible, and a low-vision rider cannot read it at all.
- **Evidence:** Scratch widget test on Flutter 3.41.2 with the app's ThemeData and Roboto loaded: `bg=#FF2196F3 materialColor=#FF2196F3 surfaceTint=#00000000 elev=1.0 label=#FFA0CAFD primary=#FFA0CAFD contrast=1.84:1` and `bg=#FFF44336 materialColor=#FFF44336 ... label=#FFA0CAFD ... contrast=2.17:1` (line-for-line replica of recording_screen.dart:1716-1755); `preflight Start Recording label=#FFA0CAFD bg=#FF2196F3 contrast=1.84:1` (byte-identical copy of start_preflight_sheet.dart, sha256 3412abe9...). Code: recording_screen.dart:1735-1737 `style: ElevatedButton.styleFrom(backgroundColor: camera.isRecording ? Colors.red : Colors.blue,` with label style `TextStyle(fontSize: 16, fontWeight: FontWeight.bold)` (1750-1753); start_preflight_sheet.dart:139-142 `style: ElevatedButton.styleFrom(backgroundColor: Colors.blue, ...)`; recording_settings_sheet.dart:557-560 same.
- **Verifier corrections:** Contrast on red is 2.16-2.17:1 (rounding). Otherwise accurate.
- **Recommendation:** Add foregroundColor: Colors.white to the three ElevatedButton.styleFrom calls (recording_screen.dart:1735, start_preflight_sheet.dart:139, recording_settings_sheet.dart:557). Change the fills to Colors.blue.shade700 or shade800 and Colors.red.shade700, so white text reaches at least 4.5:1. Alternatively, set an elevatedButtonTheme in main.dart so every button gets this.
- **Fix note:** button colours meet 4.5:1 contrast

#### F063

**TelemetryProvider starts bestForNavigation GPS, the accelerometer and a 1 s fallback poll at app launch and never pauses them when not recording, including in the iOS background**

Severity: **medium** · Category: performance · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:104` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:137`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:268`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:967`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1006`, `lib/main.dart:54`, `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:97`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:132`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:743` …

- **What is wrong:** RecordingScreen calls telemetry.initialize() from _initializeCamera at startup (recording_screen.dart:967). initialize() starts three things. The first is a userAccelerometer stream at the sensors_plus default of 200 ms. The second is a position stream with AndroidSettings(bestForNavigation, distanceFilter 0, intervalDuration 500 ms, forceLocationManager). The third is a Timer.periodic(1 s) that issues a bestForNavigation getCurrentPosition with a 2 s timeout whenever no fix has arrived for 2 s. There is no stop or pause API; only dispose() cancels them, and the provider lives for the whole process (main.dart:54). Nothing ties them to recording or to the app lifecycle. releaseCameraIfIdle frees the camera for Ride History, but telemetry keeps running and notifying ~7-8 times per second, which also rebuilds the offstage RecordingScreen. The permission audit also asks for background location and a battery-optimisation exemption at launch. On iOS, AppleSettings defaults to allowBackgroundLocationUpdates: true, combined here with pauseLocationUpdatesAutomatically: false and UIBackgroundModes location.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider opens the app only to review a ride and watches 30 minutes of playback. For that whole time location is requested at bestForNavigation every 500 ms, the accelerometer runs at 5 Hz and the 1 s timer fires; indoors with no fix, the timer issues a new 2 s high-accuracy getCurrentPosition request every couple of seconds. None of this is needed off the recording screen. After the rider leaves the app, the timer and subscriptions stay alive for as long as the process does; on iOS, if that build runs, location continues in the background indefinitely.
- **Evidence:** telemetry_provider.dart:104-106 '_isTrackingStarted = true; _startAccelerometerTracking(); _startLocationTracking();'; :112-117 'AndroidSettings(accuracy: LocationAccuracy.bestForNavigation, distanceFilter: 0, intervalDuration: Duration(milliseconds: 500), forceLocationManager: true,'; :137-139 'Timer.periodic(const Duration(seconds: 1), (_) { _pollLocationIfStale(); });'; :155-159 getCurrentPosition(bestForNavigation, timeLimit: 2 s); :268-269 'userAccelerometerEventStream().listen(' (sensors_plus-7.0.0 default samplingPeriod = SensorInterval.normalInterval = 200 ms); :743-750 dispose is the only canceller. recording_screen.dart:967 'await telemetryProvider.initialize();'; :1006 'await camera.releaseCameraIfIdle();' leaves telemetry untouched; :230-269 lifecycle handling covers only blackout and stall recovery. app_permission_service.dart:97-110 Permission.locationAlways and requestIgn…
- **Verifier corrections:** The UI notification rate depends on the speedRefreshMs setting (_uiNotifyMinInterval clamps to 100-1000 ms), so '~7-8 times per second' is the upper bound at the 100 ms setting, not a fixed rate. | On Android, background cost is limited by the platform's background location limits (a few updates per hour), and Dart timers stop once the process is frozen. The 'stays alive as long as the process' cost applies mainly to the foreground non-recording screens (history and playback), and to iOS.
- **Recommendation:** Add pauseTracking()/resumeTracking() to TelemetryProvider, cancelling and recreating the position subscription, accelerometer subscription and poll timer. Call pause in RecordingScreen before pushing Ride History and on AppLifecycleState.paused when !camera.isRecording. Call resume on return or on resumed. On iOS, pass allowBackgroundLocationUpdates: _isRideActive, or false when idle.
- **Fix note:** sensors/GPS paused when idle in background or in history

#### F064

**The 1 s getCurrentPosition fallback goes through the Play-services Fused client, which raises 'turn on location'/Location Accuracy dialogs every few seconds**

Severity: **medium** · Category: android-platform · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:155` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:137`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:116`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:132`, `pub-cache geolocator_android-5.0.2/android/src/main/java/com/baseflow/geolocator/location/FusedLocationClient.java:257`

- **What is wrong:** The position stream uses forceLocationManager: true (line 116), but the stale-fix fallback (lines 137-167) calls Geolocator.getCurrentPosition with a plain LocationSettings. geolocator_android 5.0.2 then uses FusedLocationClient whenever Play services are present (GeolocationManager.createLocationClient). Its startPositionUpdates runs checkLocationSettings and, on RESOLUTION_REQUIRED, calls startResolutionForResult(activity) (FusedLocationClient.java:237-258). The Dart side gives up after 2 s, and the timer fires again. Location-service state is checked only once, in initialize() (line 89), and the stream subscription has no onError (lines 132-134).
- **Failure scenario:** The dialogs appear in two cases: device location is turned off during a session, or Google Location Accuracy is disabled and GPS goes stale for 2 s (app start before the first fix, tunnels, garages). In either case a system dialog covers MotoCam about every 3 s and the dialogs stack up. During recording each dialog pauses the activity, and 8 s or more of that triggers the stall recovery.
- **Evidence:** Emulator with location on but the network provider (Location Accuracy) off: right after launch `topResumedActivity=... com.google.android.gms/com.google.android.location.settings.LocationSettingsCheckerActivity` showed 'For a better experience, your device will need to use Location Accuracy' (C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/androidscan/shot2.png). A new instance appeared after 'No thanks'. With location toggled off and the app open, logcat recorded `START u0 {act=com.google.android.gms.location.settings.CHECK_SETTINGS ... LocationSettingsCheckerActivity}` 10 times in 30 s (20:31:36, :39, :41, :45, :47, :51, :54, :57, 20:32:00, :03). 21 dialog instances ended up stacked in the task, and the log also showed `Zone Error: The location service on the device is disabled.` (.../androidscan/loc-off-30s…
- **Verifier corrections:** Location-service state is checked only in initialize() (line 89): if location is off at launch, tracking and the timer never start. The location-off dialog storm therefore needs location to be turned off after tracking has started. The Location Accuracy case can occur at any stale-fix moment. | The location-off-at-launch case does not trigger dialogs, because initialize() returns before the timer starts. Dialogs need location to be disabled after tracking has started, or Google Location Accuracy to be off while GPS is stale. The 'Zone Error' in the log comes from the position stream having no onError handler, not from the fallback poll.
- **Recommendation:** In telemetry_provider.dart, pass the same platform settings to the fallback as the stream uses (AndroidSettings with forceLocationManager: true), or replace the poll with Geolocator.getLastKnownPosition(forceAndroidLocationManager: true). Add onError to the getPositionStream listen call. Subscribe to Geolocator.getServiceStatusStream() to pause polling, and show one in-app banner when location is disabled.
- **Fix note:** fallback poll uses stream settings, backs off; location-off banner

#### F065

**Ride distance integrates every accepted fix with no accuracy, stationary or timestamp-order gate, accumulating GPS jitter**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:248` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:142-167`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:197-234`

- **What is wrong:** During a ride, every accepted fix adds the haversine distance from the previous fix (239-251). The only check is 'meters > 0'. There is no accuracy gate, no stationary or zero-speed gate, no minimum displacement relative to accuracy and no timestamp-order check. The stationary heuristics (197-234) affect speed only. Fallback getCurrentPosition fixes (142-167) come from a different client than the LocationManager stream, yet they feed the same accumulator, even when they are older than the last stream fix. iOS reduced-accuracy (Precise off) fixes are integrated the same way.
- **Failure scenario:** Waiting 2 minutes at a light, with fixes every 0.5 s wandering +/-1-3 m, adds 0.24-0.75 km. A single 60 m multipath excursion in an urban canyon adds 120 m. Over a city ride with many stops, the saved distanceKm shown in the list and the overlay is inflated by kilometres.
- **Evidence:** Scratch C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/corrhist/test/telemetry_gps_test.dart T4: 'jitter=+/-1.0m fixes=240 (2 min @ 2 Hz) rideDistanceKm=0.243', '+/-2.0m ... 0.497', '+/-3.0m ... 0.751', 'single 60 m multipath excursion (acc=80 m) adds 120 m'. Code 248-250: 'if (meters > 0) { updatedDistanceKm += (meters / 1000); }'. Real jitter varies by device.
- **Verifier corrections:** The fallback poll only runs when the stream has been silent for 2 s or more (149), so mixed-source fixes are occasional rather than constant. The 0.5 s interval is only what the app requests (115). Many GNSS chipsets deliver fixes at 1 Hz, which roughly halves the jitter figures from the scratch test, though they remain material. | Two corrections. First, the 'fixes every 0.5 s' premise is only a request; GNSS hardware usually delivers about 1 Hz, so the per-stop overcount is roughly half the scratch-test numbers. Second, the larger real-world trigger is the stale-stream fallback: it uses the Fused provider and can return coarse network fixes after a GPS dropout. Out-of-order fallback fixes…
- **Recommendation:** In _updateLocation (telemetry_provider.dart), add distance only when all of these hold: the fix is later than the last ride fix; position.accuracy is at or below about 25 m; and the displacement exceeds max(3 m, the accuracy-based noise floor), or the GPS speed is above about 2 km/h. Also skip distance accumulation for fixes from _pollLocationIfStale, or pass forceLocationManager: true there, so that fallback fixes cannot add network-fix jumps.
- **Fix note:** distance accumulation gated on accuracy/plausibility

#### F066

**userAccelerometerEventStream is listened to without onError, so devices lacking a linear-acceleration sensor silently never detect incidents**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:269` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:200`, `lib/main.dart:34-40`

- **What is wrong:** userAccelerometerEventStream() is listened to without onError (268-297). sensors_plus maps it to Sensor.TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION. When getDefaultSensor returns null, it emits the error 'NO_SENSOR' and never sends data (sensors_plus-7.0.0 StreamHandlerImpl.kt:27-38). The error goes to the zone handler, which only prints in debug builds (main.dart:34-40). _smoothedLinearAccelerationG stays 0, so no incident is ever detected and isDeviceMovingByAccel is always false. There is no check, no fallback (such as TYPE_ACCELEROMETER with gravity removed) and no UI warning.
- **Failure scenario:** On a budget phone without a gyroscope, where Android does not provide the fused linear-acceleration sensor, the rider records with 'Incident MEDIUM' in the profile chips. No crash will ever be auto-detected or auto-locked.
- **Evidence:** telemetry_provider.dart:268-269: '_accelerometerSubscription = userAccelerometerEventStream().listen((UserAccelerometerEvent event) {' with no onError. StreamHandlerImpl.kt:28-37: 'sensor = sensorManager.getDefaultSensor(sensorType) if (sensor != null) {...} else { events.error(NO_SENSOR, Sensor not found, ...) }'. The Dart side caches it as a broadcast stream (method_channel_sensors.dart:117-128).
- **Verifier corrections:** The finding cites main.dart:34-40. The handler is the runZonedGuarded onError callback, at about lines 33-40. A second consequence: isDeviceMovingByAccel can never be true, so the stationary-speed heuristic loses its accelerometer input. Note also that _startAccelerometerTracking only runs after location permission is granted (initialize() 99-106), so incident detection is equally absent when location is denied. That is a related gap. | Medium confidence that some devices lack the sensor is fair. On devices without a gyroscope, AOSP allows a magnetometer-based implementation, so 'no gyroscope' does not by itself mean the sensor is missing. The failure needs the device to expose no TYPE_LINE…
- **Recommendation:** In _startAccelerometerTracking, add an onError handler. On a PlatformException with code NO_SENSOR, cancel the subscription and subscribe to accelerometerEventStream() instead, removing gravity with a low-pass filter (gravity = 0.8*gravity + 0.2*raw; linear = raw - gravity). Expose a bool such as incidentDetectionAvailable or degraded so recording_screen.dart can show a warning chip.
- **Fix note:** raw-accelerometer fallback; detection-unavailable warning

#### F067

**The incident detector thresholds an EMA of 200 ms samples and needs two consecutive hits, so impacts must far exceed the configured g (and the first sample after a ride start bypasses the EMA)**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:286` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:268-297`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:310`, `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:118-138`

- **What is wrong:** The raw value |a|/9.81 is smoothed by an EMA with alpha 0.30 (274-278). It is sampled at the sensors_plus default period of 200 ms (sensors_plus_platform_interface-2.0.1 sensor_interval.dart:5; StreamHandlerImpl samplingPeriod = 200000 us). An incident needs two consecutive smoothed samples at or above the trigger (286-291). The settings present sensitivity as plain g thresholds of 2.8, 3.5 and 4.5 (app_settings_provider.dart:118-127). In the other direction, the EMA is bypassed when the smoothed value is exactly 0.0 (275). That happens after every startRideSession (310), so a single moderate spike right after the ride starts counts at full weight.
- **Failure scenario:** At Medium (3.5 g), an 8 g impact lasting 150 ms and captured in one 200 ms sample produces no incident and no auto-lock. A single-sample impulse needs at least 16.5 g, and a 400 ms impulse at least 9.75 g. Right after START, one 5 g knock followed by quiet, for example from mounting or bumping the phone, triggers an incident and locks segments.
- **Evidence:** Scratch C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/corrhist/test/telemetry_exp_test.dart T3 (real logic via updateAccelerometerForTesting, 0.05 g baseline): 'trigger=2.8 minSingleSampleG=13.15 minTwoSampleG=7.80 samplesAt1.5x=5 ... freshRideSingleSpikeG=4.00 eightG150ms=0'; 'trigger=3.5 minSingleSampleG=16.50 minTwoSampleG=9.75 samplesAt1.5x=5 ... freshRideSingleSpikeG=5.00 eightG150ms=0'; 'trigger=4.5 minSingleSampleG=21.25 minTwoSampleG=12.55 ... freshRideSingleSpikeG=6.45 eightG150ms=0'. 200 ms is only the requested period; if Android delivers samples faster, these numbers drop.
- **Verifier corrections:** Android treats samplingPeriodUs only as a hint and often delivers events faster than 200 ms. The exact g figures are therefore a worst case for a 5 Hz delivery rate. The structural problem stands at any rate: the EMA damps short impulses and the 0.0 sentinel lets the first sample through unfiltered. A sub-200 ms impulse can also fall between samples and be missed entirely, not just attenuated. The sentinel also re-triggers mid-ride if the smoothed value ever lands at exactly 0.0, though that is practically unreachable. | The exact g figures hold only at a true 5 Hz delivery rate. Because the requested period is only a hint, real devices often deliver faster, so the effective thresholds are…
- **Recommendation:** In telemetry_provider.dart, call userAccelerometerEventStream(samplingPeriod: SensorInterval.gameInterval). Detect on the raw magnitude, or a light EMA (alpha of about 0.7), over a duration window such as 'at or above the trigger for 40 ms or more', instead of two smoothed 200 ms hits. Replace the 0.0 sentinel with an explicit bool _accelSeeded that startRideSession resets, and seed it from a short warm-up average. Keep the existing g thresholds but document them.
- **Fix note:** detection on raw magnitude at 50 Hz (threshold calibration is owner)

#### F068

**The ride save path has no error handling: hashFileSha256 throws on read errors despite its 'returns null' dartdoc, and write/encode failures escape, so a failure silently drops the ride JSON**

Severity: **medium** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:437` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:52-67`, `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:72-95`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:268-297`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:606-616`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:839-897`, `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:52`, `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:65`, `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:69` …

- **What is wrong:** Neither stopRideSessionAndPersist (362-460) nor _stopRecordingFlow (recording_screen.dart:839-897, which is try/finally only) catches errors.
