In writeTelemetryAtomically, tempFile.writeAsString(..., flush: true) runs before the try block (integrity_utils.dart:82) and throws on ENOSPC. hashFileSha256 lets read errors escape through 'sha256.bind(file.openRead()).first' (:65), although its doc says it returns null when reading fails (:52-53). jsonEncode (telemetry_provider.dart:611) throws on non-finite doubles. The exception escapes the button handler. The only visible symptom is that 'Recording + telemetry saved' never appears, and the samples and ID stay in memory. Separately, accelerometer input is not validated. One non-finite reading makes _smoothedLinearAccelerationG NaN for the rest of the ride, because the '== 0.0' reset at 275 never matches. That disables incident detection and makes this save throw.
- **Failure scenario:** The app loops recordings on a nearly full phone. At stop, writing ride_<id>.telemetry.json.tmp fails with 'No space left on device'. No JSON is written, no error is shown, and the ride never appears in Ride History. The same happens if a segment disappears between the exists() check and openRead, or after a NaN sensor sample.
- **Evidence:** Scratch C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/corrhist/test/telemetry_exp_test.dart T2 (one NaN accelerometer sample): 'path=null error=JsonUnsupportedObjectError filesInDir=[seg.mp4] samplesStillHeld=5 isRideActive=false accelAfterNaN=NaN'. integrity_utils.dart:52-53: 'Returns the SHA-256 hex digest of the file at [path], or `null` if the path is empty, the file does not exist, or reading fails.' versus :65 'final digest = await sha256.bind(file.openRead()).first;'. The ENOSPC trigger is inferred from the code.
- **Verifier corrections:** With a NaN sample, the first throw comes from _buildIntegrityMetadata -> hashJsonCanonical, which calls jsonEncode on the payload at integrity_utils.dart:21, before _writeTelemetryAtomically is reached. The outcome is the same. Two more consequences of the escape: _exitBlackoutMode (recording_screen.dart:889-891) is skipped, so the app can stay in blackout mode after a failed stop; and _rideSessionId and the samples stay in memory with no retry path, and the next startRideSession clears them. Video segments are not lost, so this is not critical. Low storage is a rubric condition for high, but the ENOSPC trigger is inferred, and the loop recorder normally frees space, so medium is reasonable…
- **Recommendation:** In integrity_utils.hashFileSha256, wrap the openRead/digest in try/catch and return null, matching the dartdoc. In TelemetryProvider, drop non-finite raw accelerometer and GPS values before the EMA and the distance and speed calculations. In stopRideSessionAndPersist, wrap the integrity and write steps in try/catch: keep the payload and samples in memory and return a failure result. _stopRecordingFlow should then catch it, show a visible 'Telemetry could not be saved (storage full?)' notice, offer a retry, and still exit blackout mode.
- **Fix note:** non-finite values rejected; failed saves queued with Retry

#### F069

**Release and profile builds swallow every framework and zone error, and the camera pipeline logs nothing outside debug**

Severity: **medium** · Category: maintainability · Location: `lib/main.dart:17` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/main.dart:22`, `lib/main.dart:34`, `lib/main.dart:14`, `lib/main.dart:53`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:171`, `release-notes/v0.1.11.md:86`, `release-notes/v0.1.12.md:110`

- **What is wrong:** main() replaces FlutterError.onError with a handler that prints only when kDebugMode is true (main.dart:17-23). The runZonedGuarded handler does the same (main.dart:34-40). In profile and release builds, Flutter's default handler would print the exception and up to 100 stack frames (FlutterError.dumpErrorToConsole, flutter/packages/flutter/lib/src/foundation/assertions.dart:1042-1047). Here both paths are silent. There is no PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError hook and no crash reporter. CameraProvider is built with `enableDebugLogging: kDebugMode` (main.dart:53). Its `_log` (camera_provider.dart:171-175) is the only diagnostic output for 79 call sites across 28 catch blocks: rollover, persistence, retention deletions and gallery export. The comments 'Errors are logged but app continues running' (main.dart:22, :39) are false outside debug. Also, WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized() runs outside the guarded zone (main.dart:14 vs :26). This causes the 'Zone mismatch' warning that release-notes/v0.1.11.md:86 and v0.1.12.md:110 list as a known limitation.
- **Failure scenario:** On a release build, a segment roll throws, the gallery export fails, or retention deletes files. A build() throw can leave a grey ErrorWidget, and an unhandled async error, such as one from the discarded updateForegroundRecording future at recording_screen.dart:419, can abort a flow. `adb logcat` shows nothing from the app in any of these cases. Field bug reports arrive with no exception text or stack trace, so recording failures cannot be diagnosed after the fact.
- **Evidence:** main.dart:17-23: `FlutterError.onError = (FlutterErrorDetails details) { if (kDebugMode) { debugPrint(...); ... } // Errors are logged but app continues running };`. main.dart:34-40 has the same pattern. main.dart:53: `CameraProvider(enableDebugLogging: kDebugMode)`. `grep -c "_log(" camera_provider.dart` returns 79. `grep -rn "PlatformDispatcher|Crashlytics|Sentry" lib` finds nothing. In the Flutter SDK, assertions.dart:1042-1047 shows the non-debug branch calling `debugPrintStack(stackTrace: details.stack, label: ..., maxFrames: 100)`.
- **Verifier corrections:** The Zone mismatch check (BindingBase.debugCheckZone) runs only inside assert, so the warning appears only in debug builds, not in release logs. Flutter's default handler in release skips errors flagged silent (assertions.dart:1029). A few other files log unconditionally via debugPrint (for example telemetry_provider.dart:600 and recording_screen.dart:464), but the camera pipeline does not. | Flutter's default dumpErrorToConsole in release prints only non-silent errors, and after the first error it prints only a one-line 'Another exception was thrown' summary. So the default would have given partial output, not complete logs.
- **Recommendation:** In lib/main.dart, move ensureInitialized() into the runZonedGuarded body. Have FlutterError.onError call FlutterError.presentError(details), set PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError, and make the zone handler debugPrint unconditionally. In camera_provider.dart, add a warning/error log path that prints in every build mode, and keep only verbose timing logs behind enableDebugLogging.
- **Fix note:** error hooks report in every build mode

#### F070

**pubspec sdk '>=3.0.0 <4.0.0' and README 'Flutter 3.0+' misstate the toolchain floor; the dependencies need Dart 3.9/Flutter 3.35 and the lockfile Dart 3.10.3/Flutter 3.38.4**

Severity: **medium** · Category: dependencies · Location: `pubspec.yaml:6` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `README.md:39`, `README.md:120`, `pubspec.lock:888`, `pubspec.yaml:16`, `README.md:40`, `README.md:44`, `pubspec.yaml:6`

- **What is wrong:** The environment declares only `sdk: '>=3.0.0 <4.0.0'` (pubspec.yaml:5-6), with no `flutter:` bound. The direct constraints cannot resolve below Dart 3.9 / Flutter 3.35: every camera 0.12.x requires sdk >=^3.9.0 and flutter >=3.35.0, and flutter_lints ^6.0.0 requires Dart 3.8. The committed lock needs dart >=3.10.3 and flutter >=3.38.4 (pubspec.lock:888-890), driven by path_provider_foundation 2.6.0 and video_player 2.11.1. README.md:39 ('Framework: Flutter 3.0+') and README.md:120 ('Flutter SDK: >=3.0.0 <4.0.0') repeat the false floor. The 3.0 lower bound also pins the package language version to 3.0, which disables Dart 3.1-3.11 language features and puts `dart format` in the pre-3.7 short style. The 15-file format drift in the baseline is measured against that style.
- **Failure scenario:** A contributor uses an older SDK that README calls supported. On Flutter <3.35, `flutter pub get` fails (camera ^0.12.0 requires SDK ^3.9.0), contradicting the docs. On Flutter 3.35.x-3.38.3, pub silently re-resolves the locked packages its SDK cannot satisfy: path_provider_foundation 2.6.0 drops to 2.5.1, and on 3.35 video_player 2.11.1 drops to 2.10.1. It rewrites pubspec.lock and prints only 'Changed N dependencies!'. The contributor then tests, and may commit, a plugin set nobody else runs.
- **Evidence:** pubspec.yaml:6 `sdk: '>=3.0.0 <4.0.0'`; pubspec.lock:888-890 `sdks: dart: ">=3.10.3 <4.0.0" flutter: ">=3.38.4"`. Pub cache pubspecs: camera-0.12.0 `sdk: ^3.9.0, flutter: ">=3.35.0"`; path_provider_foundation-2.6.0 `sdk: ^3.10.3, flutter: ">=3.38.4"`; video_player-2.11.1 `sdk: ^3.10.0, flutter: ">=3.38.0"`; flutter_lints-6.0.0 `sdk: ^3.8.0`. pub.dev API: camera 0.12.0 and 0.12.0+1 ^3.9.0, 0.12.0+2 ^3.10.0, 0.12.1 ^3.12.0; path_provider_foundation 2.5.1 ^3.9.0; video_player 2.10.1 ^3.7.0. Over all 13 direct constraints, the lowest satisfiable floor is Dart 3.9.0 / Flutter 3.35.0, set by camera. README.md:39 '- **Framework**: Flutter 3.0+', README.md:120 '- Flutter SDK: >=3.0.0 <4.0.0'. The old-SDK re-resolution is inferred from pub's documented behaviour and these SDK bounds. It was demonstrated only in the opposite direction, where Flutter 3.47.7 printed 'Changed 4 dependencies!' on thi…
- **Verifier corrections:** There is no README.md:40 or :44 defect beyond the 'Dart SDK: Compatible with Flutter version' wording. Where the SDK is too old, the main failure is a loud pub get error rather than a silent one; only the 3.35-3.38.3 window re-resolves silently. | README.md:120 labels the Dart SDK constraint as 'Flutter SDK'. Raising the lower bound changes the language version from 3.0 to 3.10+. That turns on 3.7+ language semantics (e.g. wildcard `_` variables) and switches dart format to the tall style, so the change can surface new analyzer diagnostics and reformat every file. It interacts with F025's format gate.
- **Recommendation:** In pubspec.yaml, set `sdk: ^3.10.3` and add `flutter: ">=3.38.4"`, matching the lock, or use the tested toolchain (^3.11.0 / >=3.41.2). Update README.md:39 and :120 to state both the Dart and Flutter floors. Run analyze, then land a single `dart format` commit before adding the CI format gate.
- **Fix note:** SDK floor ^3.11.0 / flutter >=3.41.0 from pubspec.lock

#### F071

**README overstates the integrity pipeline: the unkeyed hash is not tamper protection, playback never shows VERIFIED, status names differ, and the list's VERIFIED badge ignores the video files**

Severity: **medium** · Category: docs-drift · Location: `README.md:114` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed (docs)**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:204`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:111`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:625`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:693`, `lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:19`

- **What is wrong:** README.md:114 says each ride is 'protected by a SHA-256 integrity envelope' and that on playback RideRecord 'verifies both the payload hash and per-segment file hashes, surfacing "VERIFIED", "MODIFIED", or "QUARANTINED" statuses to the user.' The code differs in five ways: 1. The envelope is not protection against tampering. It is an unkeyed SHA-256 stored inside the same JSON. segmentHashes live in the integrity block, which is removed before the payload is hashed. Anyone who edits the JSON or swaps a video can recompute matching values, so it only detects accidental corruption. 2. The status values are different. The enum is {unknown, verified, modified}. There is no 'MODIFIED' label: the list badge says 'INTEGRITY WARNING'. QUARANTINED is a separate path-scope badge. Rides with no envelope get no badge at all. 3. The only 'VERIFIED' badge is in Ride History, and it ignores the video files. Ride History loads rides with verifySegments: false (payload only), so a ride whose video was replaced still shows VERIFIED. 4. Playback runs segment verification but never shows VERIFIED. It only shows a one-time SnackBar for modified or quarantined rides. That SnackBar says 'Playback contin…
- **Failure scenario:** A rider or insurer treats the green VERIFIED badge as proof that the footage is original. A segment replaced on disk still shows VERIFIED in Ride History. If the rider taps 'Unlock clip' first, setLockState recomputes segmentHashes from the replaced file, and later playback verification reports no issue at all.
- **Evidence:** README: - README.md:114: 'Each ride session is protected by a SHA-256 integrity envelope ... surfacing "VERIFIED", "MODIFIED", or "QUARANTINED" statuses to the user.' Status model and hashing: - lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:10: 'enum RideIntegrityStatus { unknown, verified, modified }'. - ride_record.dart:693-695: 'final payloadWithoutIntegrity = Map<String, dynamic>.from(json)..remove('integrity'); final actualPayloadHash = _hashJsonCanonical(payloadWithoutIntegrity);'. - lib/core/utils/integrity_utils.dart:19-23: plain 'sha256.convert(encoded)', with no key. Ride History: - lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:66: 'RideRecord.fromTelemetryFile(telemetryFile)'. verifySegments defaults to false (ride_record.dart:253). - rides_list_screen.dart:198: label 'INTEGRITY WARNING'. - rides_list_screen.dart:204: label 'VERIFIED'. - rides_list_screen.dart:212: 'QUARANTINED…
- **Verifier corrections:** Points 2 and 3 slightly understate what Ride History shows. Besides the badge, the subtitle at rides_list_screen.dart:232 shows 'Integrity: Verified/Modified/Unknown' from integrityLabel (ride_record.dart:98-106). So a title-case 'Modified' label does exist, and rides without an envelope show 'Integrity: Unknown' rather than nothing. Everything else is accurate. | The lock gate in Ride History is at rides_list_screen.dart:168, and unlocking is the only toggle offered from that list. The playback SnackBar text is at ride_playback_screen.dart:111, and the playlist builder at :335-350.
- **Recommendation:** Reword README.md:114 to call the envelope 'corruption detection (unkeyed SHA-256)'. List the real states: VERIFIED in Ride History, covering metadata only; INTEGRITY WARNING; QUARANTINED n; no badge when a ride has no envelope; and the playback warning SnackBar. In ride_playback_screen.dart, change the SnackBar text so it no longer claims trusted-only playback. In rides_list_screen.dart, run verifySegmentIntegrity() before calling setLockState.
- **Fix note:** Docs sync: README.md:46 calls it an unkeyed SHA-256 integrity check that is not tamper-proof, and lists the badges exactly as rides_list_screen.dart _buildBadges renders them (edited in this pass).

#### F072

**README 'Required Permissions' omits microphone, background location, notifications and the battery-optimization exemption, and lists a sensor permission that is never requested**

Severity: **medium** · Category: docs-drift · Location: `README.md:151` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed (docs)**

Also affects: `README.md:33`, `README.md:157`, `README.md:159`, `README.md:161`, `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:97`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:14`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:96`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:206`

- **What is wrong:** README.md:151-161 lists these permissions and says all are 'managed automatically by the permission_handler package': - Camera - Location ('for GPS tracking and navigation') - 'Sensor Access: To collect motion and orientation data' - private app storage - 'Optional Public Export' What the app actually requests at HEAD: - Camera. - Microphone, when audio is enabled. - Location when-in-use. - On Android: Notifications, Location Always (ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION), and the battery-optimization exemption through a native ACTION_REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS intent. - The manifest also declares the camera, microphone and location foreground-service types. Other gaps: - No sensor permission is requested anywhere. Only userAccelerometerEventStream is read, and no orientation data is collected. - Geolocator also requests location itself in TelemetryProvider, so not everything goes through permission_handler. - 'Optional Public Export' is not optional. Locked segments are exported automatically when recording stops, on Android only. - On Android 7-9 that export path needs WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, which is declared (maxSdkVersion 28) but never requested at runtime. The native legacy b…
- **Failure scenario:** A maintainer writes the Play Console data-safety form, the background-location declaration or a privacy policy from the README. They leave out microphone recording and background location, which is a sensitive permission that needs a Play declaration. They also describe a sensor permission and an optional export that do not exist. Riders then get microphone, notification, 'Allow all the time' location and battery-exemption prompts that no document mentions.
- **Evidence:** README: - README.md:155-161: 'Camera', 'Location: For GPS tracking and navigation', 'Sensor Access: To collect motion and orientation data', 'App Storage (private-first)', 'Optional Public Export: To export selected clips to gallery-compatible public folders', 'These permissions are managed automatically by the `permission_handler` package.' Permission code: - lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:78-115 requests Permission.camera, Permission.microphone and Permission.locationWhenInUse. On Android it also requests Permission.notification and Permission.locationAlways ('Background location (screen-off tracking)') and calls requestIgnoreBatteryOptimizations(). - android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:108: 'Intent(Settings.ACTION_REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS)'. Manifest (android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml): - :11 RECORD_AUDIO - :14 ACCESS_BACK…
- **Verifier corrections:** Export is automatic only for clips the rider has locked, and locking is itself the rider's choice. So 'Optional Public Export' is misleading rather than outright false: nothing asks the rider to export, but locking a clip triggers the export. | Auto-export covers locked segments only ('_segmentsSelectedForPublicExport'), so 'optional' is partly true: it happens only for clips the rider locks. The WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE and legacy-folder issue applies to the native legacy branch for API 28 and below; with Flutter's default minSdk that means Android 7-9. It is a functional bug rather than docs drift.
- **Recommendation:** Rewrite README.md:151-161 from AndroidManifest.xml and app_permission_service.dart. List: Camera; Microphone (when audio is on); precise location; background location (optional, Android); notifications (Android 13+); battery-optimization exemption (optional); and the camera|microphone|location foreground-service types. Drop 'Sensor Access' and 'navigation', and describe auto-export of locked clips. Separately, either request WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE on API 28 and below or skip export there, and change MainActivity.kt:164/206 to Movies/MotoCam/Recordings.
- **Fix note:** Docs sync: README.md:53-62 lists camera, microphone (only when audio is on), location while in use (no background location), notifications (Android 13+), storage (Android 9 and lower, only with gallery export) and the battery exemption offered once. It also covers the foreground-service types from granted permissions. 'precise' removed in this pass.

#### F073

**README, key.properties.template and the Gradle signing error name only MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_*, but the alias and key password are read from MOTOCAM_KEY_ALIAS and MOTOCAM_KEY_PASSWORD**

Severity: **medium** · Category: docs-drift · Location: `README.md:194` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `README.md:179`, `android/key.properties.template:12`, `android/app/build.gradle.kts:40`, `android/app/build.gradle.kts:56`

- **What is wrong:** README.md:179, README.md:194, key.properties.template:12 and the Gradle signing-gate error (build.gradle.kts:56) all name the variables only by the wildcard MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_*. Gradle actually reads these four (build.gradle.kts:38-41): - MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_FILE - MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD - MOTOCAM_KEY_ALIAS - MOTOCAM_KEY_PASSWORD The last two do not match the documented prefix, and the exact names are written down nowhere. README.md:179 also says 'environment variables take precedence'. In fact precedence is per value: each one independently prefers its environment variable and falls back to key.properties.
- **Failure scenario:** A maintainer adds CI secrets MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_FILE, MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD, MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_ALIAS and MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_KEY_PASSWORD, following the docs, with no key.properties on the runner. `flutter build appbundle` throws 'Release signing config missing. Provide android/key.properties or MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_* environment variables.' even though MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_* variables are set.
- **Evidence:** Documentation: - README.md:194: 'Use `android/key.properties` for local signing or `MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_*` in CI and automation.' - README.md:179: '...or `MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_*` environment variables. When both are present, environment variables take precedence.' - android/key.properties.template:12: 'Use CI secret storage to provide `MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_*` env vars in automation.' Gradle: - android/app/build.gradle.kts:38-41: 'resolveSigningValue("storeFile", "MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_FILE")', '("storePassword", "MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD")', '("keyAlias", "MOTOCAM_KEY_ALIAS")', '("keyPassword", "MOTOCAM_KEY_PASSWORD")'. - build.gradle.kts:56: '"Release signing config missing. Provide android/key.properties or MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_* environment variables."'
- **Verifier corrections:** There is one more inaccuracy. README.md:179 says 'The release precheck accepts signing credentials from either android/key.properties or MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_*'. But scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1 never reads credentials or environment variables; its only signing checks are regexes on build.gradle.kts (lines 45-46). It is Gradle that resolves the credentials. | README.md:179 is wrong in one more way. scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1 does not read signing credentials at all; it only greps build.gradle.kts for signingConfigs.getByName("release") (lines 45-46). The fallback is per value: resolveSigningValue checks the environment first, then key.properties, for each key separately.
- **Recommendation:** List the four exact variable names in README.md:179 and :194, in android/key.properties.template:12 and in the build.gradle.kts:56 error message, and make the error name which values are missing. Also correct README.md:179 to say that Gradle, not the precheck, reads the credentials, with a per-value fallback. Renaming the variables is unnecessary.
- **Fix note:** signing error names missing values/env vars (README part: docs workflow)

#### F074

**README says the project is MIT-licensed and points to a LICENSE file, but the public repo has no LICENSE file**

Severity: **medium** · Category: docs-drift · Location: `README.md:233` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed (docs)**

Also affects: `README.md:224`, `README.md:233`

- **What is wrong:** README.md:233 says 'This project is licensed under the MIT License - see the LICENSE file for details.' No license file exists: - There is no LICENSE or COPYING file in the working tree or in git ls-files. - The GitHub API reports license: null for the public repo Sarthak195/MotoCam. Without the MIT text and a copyright notice there is no actual MIT grant, so the README's statement contradicts what GitHub shows ('no license'). README.md:224-229 also invites pull requests with no stated inbound license.
- **Failure scenario:** Someone forks or reuses MotoCam code because the README says it is MIT. Legally they have no license grant, because the MIT text and copyright notice were never published. Contributors' pull requests also have no clear license.
- **Evidence:** - README.md:233: 'This project is licensed under the MIT License - see the LICENSE file for details.' - `test -e LICENSE` prints 'no LICENSE'. - `git ls-files | grep -i -E "licen[cs]e|copying"` prints nothing. - `gh api repos/Sarthak195/MotoCam --jq '{private, license, visibility}'` returns {"license":null,"private":false,"visibility":"public"}.
- **Verifier corrections:** Given the owner's decision, the right fix is to remove the MIT claim from the README rather than add an MIT LICENSE file. | 'Legally they have no license grant' overstates the position. A README sentence declaring MIT can arguably be read as a grant even without the full text. The defect is the ambiguity and contradiction, not a certain absence of rights. The owner's decision settles the recommendation: remove the claim, do not add an MIT LICENSE file.
- **Recommendation:** Per the owner's 2026-10-20 decision, remove the MIT sentence from the README.md License section (around line 231-233). Either delete the section or replace it with a neutral line such as 'No license has been granted yet; all rights reserved.' Do not add a LICENSE file.
- **Fix note:** Docs sync: README.md:234-236 says no license has been chosen and there is no LICENSE file. development.md:278 agrees. A grep for MIT finds nothing.

#### F075

**The v0.1.11 and v0.1.12 GitHub releases ship the same APK, and the v0.1.11 notes' hash was rewritten to a build containing v0.1.12-only code**

Severity: **medium** · Category: docs-drift · Location: `release-notes/v0.1.11.md:109` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `release-notes/v0.1.11.md:108`, `release-notes/v0.1.12.md:132`, `release-notes/v0.1.12.md:133`

- **What is wrong:** The GitHub assets for v0.1.11 and v0.1.12 are the same file (sha256 539f2bb9..., 53,979,745 bytes), published 4 s apart. How the v0.1.11 hash changed: - Commit e4cf1b6, made 7 minutes after the v0.1.11 tag, first recorded the v0.1.11 APK as 53,963,149 bytes with SHA256 F9D67BFB.... - Commit cc5899a, made after the v0.1.12 tag, replaced it with 539F2BB9... in v0.1.11.md and copied the same hash into v0.1.12.md. The shared APK contains v0.1.12-only code: - Its arm64 libapp.so includes the string 'Blackout on. Hold 2s anywhere to exit.' - recording_screen.dart has 0 'blackout' references at the v0.1.11 tag and 44 at v0.1.12. So the 'v0.1.11' download is actually a v0.1.12 build, and the APK originally documented for v0.1.11 is not published. Other problems: - v0.1.12.md:132 gives '51.48 MiB (53,963,776 bytes)'. 53,963,776 bytes is 51.46 MiB, and it matches neither the asset nor the original v0.1.11 build. - Both releases report versionCode 1 / versionName 1.0, so the binaries cannot be told apart either.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider hits a v0.1.12 regression (for example in Blackout mode or the new rollover path) and installs v0.1.11 from GitHub to roll back. They get a byte-identical APK with the same bug. A maintainer bisecting a report filed against 'v0.1.11' tests the 76974c1 tree, which is not what users ran.
- **Evidence:** Assets: - gh release view v0.1.11 and v0.1.12 --json assets both return '{"digest":"sha256:539f2bb99bf4c22e236ed933ffb7e54db7e9f6ea3d6757bedca362b782ef0889","name":"app-release.apk","size":53979745}'. - They were published 2026-04-05T16:17:45Z and 16:17:49Z. Hash history: - git show e4cf1b6 -- release-notes/v0.1.11.md: '+- APK size: 53,963,149 bytes (51.46 MiB)' and '+- APK SHA256: `F9D67BFB...CB94`'. - git show cc5899a: the line '-- APK SHA256: `F9D67BFB...`' is replaced by '+- **SHA256:** 539F2BB9...0889'. Contents of the shared APK: - In the downloaded asset (sha256 539f2bb9...), lib/arm64-v8a/libapp.so contains 'Blackout on. Hold 2s anywhere to exit.' - `git show v0.1.11:lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart | grep -ci blackout` returns 0. At v0.1.12 it returns 44. Size and version: - release-notes/v0.1.12.md:132: '**Size:** 51.48 MiB (53,963,776 bytes)'. - aapt2 on the asset…
- **Verifier corrections:** In v0.1.12.md:132, '51.48 MiB' actually matches the asset (53,979,745 B = 51.48 MiB). Only the byte count 53,963,776 is wrong. cc5899a also re-encoded v0.1.11.md and introduced mojibake ('→' became 'ΓåÆ', '–' became 'ΓÇô'). The libapp.so string check was not reproduced here; the identical digests plus the tag diff support the conclusion anyway. | I did not re-inspect the libapp.so string in the APK, because it would mean downloading the asset. The identical digests plus the git history are enough to establish the duplication. The dates are consistent: tag v0.1.11 at 17:00 +0530, the note commit e4cf1b6 at 17:06, tag v0.1.12 at 21:47 and the rewrite commit cc5899a at 21:55.
- **Recommendation:** Edit release-notes/v0.1.11.md to restore the original hash and size (F9D67BFB..., 53,963,149 bytes) and to state that this APK was not published. Correct release-notes/v0.1.12.md:132 to 53,979,745 bytes. Remove or relabel the 539F... asset on the v0.1.11 GitHub release, or upload a build from the v0.1.11 tag. Going forward, copy size and digest from `gh release view --json assets`.
- **Owner decision:** Changing published GitHub release assets (deleting or relabelling the v0.1.11 APK, or uploading a rebuilt one) means editing a published release, which is the owner's call. | Changing the published GitHub release assets (deleting or relabelling the v0.1.11 APK, or uploading a rebuilt one) is a publishing action only the repository owner can take.
- **Fix note:** release notes corrected; relabelling the GitHub asset is owner-only

#### F078

**release-security-precheck.ps1 never checks $LASTEXITCODE, so it prints 'passed' and exits 0 even when flutter analyze or flutter test fails**

Severity: **medium** · Category: build-ci · Location: `scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:67` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:72`, `scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:75`, `scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:6`, `README.md:175`, `README.md:193`, `README.md:212`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:29`

- **What is wrong:** The script sets $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop' (line 6), but that affects only cmdlet errors. The script never checks $LASTEXITCODE after the native calls `flutter analyze` (line 67) and `flutter test` (line 72). It therefore always reaches 'Release security precheck passed.' (line 75) and exits 0. README.md:175, :193 and :212 present this script, run without skip flags, as the release gate. CI escapes the problem only because it passes -SkipTests -SkipAnalyze (ci.yml:29).
- **Failure scenario:** Before a release, the maintainer runs `pwsh ./scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1` as README.md:175 instructs. A test fails or the analyzer reports an issue. The red output scrolls past, the script ends with the green 'Release security precheck passed.' and returns exit code 0, and the APK is built and published with failing tests. A wrapper script or CI job that relies on the exit code is fooled the same way.
- **Evidence:** I reproduced this with the unmodified script, copied byte-identically (verified with cmp) into C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/precheck-repro. A fake flutter.cmd that exits 1 was placed first on PATH. Output: 'Running flutter analyze... / FAKE flutter analyze FAILED / Running flutter test... / FAKE flutter test FAILED / Release security precheck passed.' followed by 'precheck exit code: 0'.
- **Recommendation:** In scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1, add `if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "flutter analyze failed ($LASTEXITCODE)" }` after line 67 and the equivalent check after line 72. Alternatively, set `$PSNativeCommandUseErrorActionPreference = $true` next to line 6 (PowerShell 7.3+). Optionally have CI run the precheck once without skip flags so the gate itself is exercised.
- **Fix note:** precheck checks native exit codes

#### F115

**The three 1.6k-2k-line files mix pure timeline/retention logic with widget and plugin code, so the riskiest logic cannot be unit-tested**

Severity: **medium (scanner said low)** · Category: maintainability · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1219` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:355`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1684`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1081`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1180`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:565`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1248`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1681`

- **What is wrong:** ride_playback_screen.dart (2035 lines): - Its build() method alone is 451 lines (1219-1669). - Timeline synthesis, missing-range merging, offsets and totals, seek location and marker building (336-509, 877-933, 1047-1071) are private State methods tangled with video_player I/O. - Three painters sit in the same file (1684-1993). camera_provider.dart (1630 lines) puts all of these behind one ChangeNotifier that needs the camera plugin: - controller setup and capability fallback (315-656); - file persistence (709-808); - session and segment bookkeeping, including the rolling-deletion rule (1081-1155); - directory-wide retention and the telemetry prune (1180-1298); - the export queue (1324-1441); - listing (1535-1589). recording_screen.dart (1878 lines) keeps, inside the widget State and next to more than 600 lines of builders (_buildControls alone is 184 lines): - a 166-line preflight evaluator (565-729); - a 138-line settings-apply pipeline (1248-1384); - the start and stop orchestration (362-430, 828-898). None of this logic can run without pumping the widget or creating a real controller. That is why coverage for the three files is 0.2%, 2.9% and 31.5%, and the 31.5% comes from UI…
- **Failure scenario:** A fix to the retention rule, which deletes user video, or to the timeline offsets can only be checked by hand on a device. Regressions such as seeks landing in the wrong segment, or locked clips removed by the sweep, ship unnoticed, and every change to these files means reviewing a 2,000-line file.
- **Evidence:** Method spans from a structural outline: ride_playback build() 1219-1670, _buildEffectiveTimelineEntries 355-436, _initializePlayback 151-301. recording_screen _buildStartPreflightChecks 565-731, _openSettingsSheet 1248-1386, _buildControls 1681-1865. camera_provider initializeCamera 315-496, _enforceRecordingRetention 1180-1258. Lines hit per lcov: 2/850, 22/747 and 234/743.
- **Verifier corrections:** There are two CustomPainters plus one SliderTrackShape, not three painters. The coverage percentages (0.2%, 2.9%, 31.5%) could not be verified from the repo.
- **Recommendation:** Extract a pure PlaybackTimeline (entries, offsets, missing ranges, locate, markers) and a RetentionPolicy/SegmentSession (rolling-limit and lock rules), and add unit tests for each extraction. Splitting the widget build sections can follow.
- **Fix note:** pure logic extracted (retention, timeline, recording UI); preflight evaluator and playback build() not split

#### F047

**Integrity verification fails open: the envelope was added without bumping or reading schemaVersion, so a ride with its 'integrity' key removed is treated as legacy 'Unknown' with no badge or warning**

Severity: **low (scanner said medium)** · Category: security · Location: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:674` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:196`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:81`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:416`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:419`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:420`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:673`

- **What is wrong:** _verifyIntegrity returns RideIntegrityReport.unknown(), with no issues, whenever 'integrity' is missing or is not an object. Every file written since cd39097 carries an envelope, but schemaVersion is still 2, so the reader cannot tell that the envelope is mandatory and treats its removal like a legacy file. Unknown gets no list badge (only VERIFIED and INTEGRITY WARNING exist), just a plain 'Integrity: Unknown' line, and playback shows no security warning. Deleting the envelope therefore silently skips every hash check, and the Unlock re-sign behaviour then upgrades the ride to VERIFIED.
- **Failure scenario:** After editing a ride's samples or swapping its segments, the editor deletes the "integrity" object instead of recomputing hashes. The ride loads as 'Unknown' with no INTEGRITY WARNING badge, and playback starts without the 'Security warning' snackbar. The same edits with the envelope left in place would have been flagged.
- **Evidence:** ride_record.dart:673-676: `final rawIntegrity = json['integrity']; if (rawIntegrity is! Map) { return const RideIntegrityReport.unknown(); }`. rides_list_screen.dart:196-208 shows badges only for modified and verified, and :232 shows 'Integrity: ${ride.integrityLabel}'. ride_playback_screen.dart:81-86 warns only on `modified || hasQuarantinedSegments`. telemetry_provider.dart:416: `'schemaVersion': 2`. Measured with flutter test on a copy of lib/ (Windows): "A3 no envelope: unknown issues=[] quarantined=0 segments=1 label=Unknown | full: unknown issues=[]".
- **Verifier corrections:** Bumping schemaVersion does not make verification fail closed against deliberate tampering, because schemaVersion sits in the same unkeyed file and can be edited back to 2. It helps only against accidental envelope loss and makes formats distinguishable. The Unlock re-sign interaction applies only to locked rides (see F046). | Severity lowered from medium to low because the marginal attack value over F032 is nil. Ship it together with F032.
- **Recommendation:** In telemetry_provider.dart, write 'schemaVersion': 3. In ride_record.dart _verifyIntegrity, return modified(['missing-integrity']) when the envelope is missing and schemaVersion is 3 or higher. In rides_list_screen.dart, add an 'UNVERIFIED' badge for unknown status and disable Unlock for unknown rides (or leave the envelope absent on unlock).
- **Fix note:** reader fails closed; UNVERIFIED badge

#### F061

**A failed camera init shows the same endless spinner as loading, with no reason and no retry**

Severity: **low (scanner said medium)** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1473` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:380-403`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:462-467`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:489-493`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:635-649`

- **What is wrong:** The preview area shows the same white CircularProgressIndicator while the camera initialises and after initialisation has failed (1473-1484). Every failure path in `initializeCamera` returns with `_isInitialized = false` and no reason: camera or microphone permission denied (camera_provider.dart:380-386), recordings directory unavailable (390-395), no camera found (398-403), no preset could be opened (462-467), or an exception (489-493). The bootstrap also skips init entirely when the rider picks 'Later' in the permission dialog (108-110). The screen never says why and offers no retry. The only hint is the preflight row 'Camera initialization failed, retry from Recording screen' (641-643), yet the Recording screen has no retry control.
- **Failure scenario:** Audio recording is on by default. A rider who denied the microphone taps 'Later' in 'Permissions Required', and the main screen then shows a spinner indefinitely. Nothing tells them the microphone is the problem, or that turning audio off in Settings would fix it. A camera held by another app produces the same permanent spinner.
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:1473-1477 `if (!camera.isInitialized || camera.controller == null) { return const Center(child: CircularProgressIndicator(color: Colors.white),); }`; camera_provider.dart:380-385 `if (!_isPermissionGranted(cameraStatus) || !_isPermissionGranted(microphoneStatus)) { _log('Camera or microphone permission denied'); _isInitialized = false; notifyListeners(); return; }`; :462-466 `_isInitialized = false; _log('Unable to initialize camera for any preset on this device.'); notifyListeners(); return;`; recording_screen.dart:108-110 `if (!mounted || resolvedAudit.hasBlockingIssues) { return; }`; app_settings_provider.dart:98 `bool _audioEnabled = true;`.
- **Verifier corrections:** Missing permissions are named, once, in the bootstrap dialog and SnackBar (recording_screen.dart:129-138, 188-189), so the rider is told which permission is missing. The message is just not persistent. START re-runs the permission audit and re-initializes the camera through the preflight (569-571), so a retry path exists even though no control is labelled as one. Severity lowered to low: this is a UX polish issue with a recovery path, not a failure of a main feature. | 'No retry' is overstated. Tapping START RECORDING re-runs _initializeCamera through the preflight, and the preflight sheet shows which permission or check failed. A transient SnackBar also names the missing permissions after…
- **Recommendation:** Add an initError field (an enum plus a message) to CameraProvider, set it on each early-return path in initializeCamera, and expose a getter. In recording_screen.dart _buildCameraPreview, when the camera is not initializing and initError is set, show the reason with Retry (calling _initializeCamera) and Open Settings buttons. On AppLifecycleState.resumed, when idle and not initialized, call _initializeCamera.
- **Fix note:** camera-unavailable panel with Retry/Open Settings; no specific failure reason

#### F077

**generate-keystore.ps1 prints both keystore passwords, puts them on keytool's command line and re-parses them with Invoke-Expression; its default output path is not git-ignored outside the repo root**

Severity: **low (scanner said medium)** · Category: security · Location: `scripts/generate-keystore.ps1:35` · Verification: two-lens (code-truth + reachability/impact) → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `scripts/generate-keystore.ps1:33`, `scripts/generate-keystore.ps1:36`, `scripts/generate-keystore.ps1:2`, `.gitignore:50`

- **What is wrong:** The script turns both SecureString prompts into plain text (lines 23-24) and never frees the BSTRs. It interpolates both passwords and the DN into one command string (line 33), prints that string with Write-Host "Running: $cmd" (line 35), and runs it with Invoke-Expression (line 36). The release keystore passwords therefore appear verbatim in the console and scrollback, in any Start-Transcript or CI log, and in keytool's process command line. Invoke-Expression re-parses the string, so a $name inside a password is expanded as a variable (usually to nothing), and ';' or spaces split the command. What keytool receives is the re-parsed value, not what the user typed. Separately, OutFile defaults to the cwd-relative 'android/release.keystore'. Run from scripts/, it creates scripts/android/release.keystore, which no .gitignore rule matches (they cover /android/*.keystore and android/**), so a later 'git add -A' would publish the release signing key in this public repo.
- **Failure scenario:** The maintainer runs 'pwsh ./scripts/generate-keystore.ps1' while screen-sharing or with a transcript on, and the console prints 'Running: keytool ... -storepass <password> -keypass <password> ...'. Anyone who also gets the keystore can sign updates as MotoCam. A password like 'Secret1$abc' reaches keytool as 'Secret1'; build-infra's keytool run showed the resulting store opens with the truncated value.
- **Evidence:** scripts/generate-keystore.ps1:23-24 `PtrToStringAuto([Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::SecureStringToBSTR($storePass))`. :33 builds the string 'keytool -genkeypair -alias $Alias ... -storepass $storePassPlain -keypass $keyPassPlain -validity $ValidityDays -dname <dn>'. :35 `Write-Host "Running: $cmd"`. :36 `Invoke-Expression $cmd`. Reproduced the re-parse in pwsh 7 with $storePassPlain='Secret1$abc' and $keyPassPlain='Pa;ss word': passing 'Write-Output -storepass $storePassPlain -keypass $keyPassPlain' through Invoke-Expression printed '-storepass', 'Secret1', '-keypass', 'Pa', then failed with "The term 'ss' is not recognized". `git check-ignore -v --no-index scripts/android/release.keystore` reports no match, while android/release.keystore matches android/.gitignore:13.
- **Verifier corrections:** The scripts/android path is only reached if the script is run from scripts/, which goes against the README. Running it from any other directory that is not ignored also leaks the keystore. The password truncation reported by build-infra comes from scanner output and was not re-run here. | The Invoke-Expression and password-reuse breakage is also claimed in F027's title; count it once. The DN prompt is interpolated the same way, so `$` in the DN is also expanded.
- **Recommendation:** In scripts/generate-keystore.ps1, drop the Write-Host of the command and call keytool directly with an argument array (`& keytool @('-genkeypair', '-alias', $Alias, ..., '-storepass', $storePassPlain, ...)`), or omit -storepass/-keypass and let keytool prompt. Free the BSTRs with ZeroFreeBSTR, and resolve $OutFile against `Join-Path $PSScriptRoot '..\android\release.keystore'`. Add root .gitignore patterns `*.jks`, `*.keystore`, `*.p12` and `key.properties`.
- **Fix note:** keystore script: no Invoke-Expression, env passwords

#### F079

**CI runs twice per tagged or same-repo PR commit, pins actions to mutable major tags and declares no permissions block**

Severity: **low** · Category: build-ci · Location: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:3` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:13`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:16`

- **What is wrong:** `on: push` and `pull_request` have no branch or tag filters (ci.yml:3-5). A tag push therefore re-runs the whole job on a commit that main has already validated, and a same-repo PR runs once for push and once for pull_request. For a33a583, run 28842091751 (main) and run 28842099241 (tag v0.1.13) both built headSha a33a58301c72. There is no concurrency group and no timeout-minutes. subosito/flutter-action@v2 and actions/checkout@v4 use movable major tags (lines 13, 16). The workflow has no `permissions:` block; the token is read-only today only because the repository default is 'read'.
- **Failure scenario:** Every release tag doubles CI minutes for an identical commit. If the repository's default workflow permission is ever switched to read/write, for example to let another workflow push, this workflow silently receives a write-scoped GITHUB_TOKEN. That token is then exposed to whatever code a moved third-party tag points at.
- **Evidence:** gh run view of the two runs: {"event":"push","headBranch":"main","headSha":"a33a58301c720c8d60e196464fb77d110afdf724"} and {"event":"push","headBranch":"v0.1.13","headSha":"a33a58301c720c8d60e196464fb77d110afdf724"}. `gh api repos/Sarthak195/MotoCam/actions/permissions/workflow` returns {"default_workflow_permissions":"read"}.
- **Recommendation:** Add top-level `permissions: contents: read`, limit push to `branches: [main]` and keep pull_request, add `concurrency: {group: ci-${{ github.ref }}, cancel-in-progress: true}` and `timeout-minutes: 20`, and optionally pin subosito/flutter-action to a commit SHA.
- **Fix note:** CI triggers, permissions, concurrency, timeouts, dependabot

#### F080

**CI's actions/checkout@v4 targets the deprecated Node.js 20 runtime and is force-run on Node 24**

Severity: **low** · Category: build-ci · Location: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:13` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:16`

- **What is wrong:** The workflow uses actions/checkout@v4 (ci.yml:13), which declares the Node.js 20 runtime. GitHub flags this on every run. The runner now forces the action onto Node.js 24, a runtime that release was not built or tested against.
- **Failure scenario:** Every push and PR run carries a deprecation annotation, and the checkout step runs on a runtime it was not built for. Any Node 24 incompatibility in that release fails the first step of CI with no repo change. Meanwhile the standing warning teaches maintainers to ignore CI annotations.
- **Evidence:** `gh run view 28842091751 -R Sarthak195/MotoCam` -> ANNOTATIONS: '! Node.js 20 is deprecated. The following actions target Node.js 20 but are being forced to run on Node.js 24: actions/checkout@v4.' ci.yml:13 `uses: actions/checkout@v4`.
- **Recommendation:** Change ci.yml:13 to `actions/checkout@v5`, which targets Node 24, and check that subosito/flutter-action's current major also runs on Node 24.
- **Fix note:** checkout@v5 / setup-java@v5 (Node 24)

#### F081

**analysis_options.yaml enables only default flutter_lints; stricter rules expose fire-and-forget futures, unchecked dynamic casts and broken doc references**

Severity: **low** · Category: maintainability · Location: `analysis_options.yaml:10` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:67`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:419`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:62`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:609`, `lib/main.dart:51`, `lib/core/services/pip_service.dart:55`, `lib/core/services/pip_service.dart:64`, `lib/core/services/location_service.dart:15` …

- **What is wrong:** analysis_options.yaml includes package:flutter_lints/flutter.yaml and adds nothing; the rules at lines 23-25 are commented out. I ran a scratch copy with strict-casts, strict-inference, strict-raw-types and 21 extra rules. `flutter analyze` reported 430 issues, 423 of them in lib/: - public_member_api_docs: 277 (camera_provider 65, app_settings_provider 55, ride_record 42, telemetry_provider 23). This contradicts cd39097's claim to 'add dartdoc to all public services, providers, and models'. - avoid_catches_without_on_clauses: 49 (27 in camera_provider). - avoid_slow_async_io: 36. - discarded_futures: 31. - unawaited_futures: 6. - directives_ordering: 17. - comment_references: 5. - prefer_final_locals: 3. - strict-casts errors (invalid_assignment): 2. The significant hits: - recording_screen.dart:67-68 starts _initializePipBridge and _initializeBackgroundRecordingBridge from initState and discards the futures; the next line wraps its own call in unawaited(). - ride_playback_screen.dart:62 discards _initializePlayback(); lines 60-61 use unawaited(). - recording_screen.dart:419 discards updateForegroundRecording every second. That method has no try/catch (background_recording_servic…
- **Failure scenario:** _initializePlayback throws, for example on a file-system error inside the probe loop. The error escapes as an unhandled zone error, which is silent in release builds (see the error-handling finding), and the playback screen spins forever. The default lint set does not flag the discarded future, so nothing in review or CI points at it. Likewise, a null reply from the PiP channel becomes a swallowed TypeError reported as 'not supported'.
- **Evidence:** The strict config is at C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/strict-lint/analysis_options.yaml. The output is at C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/strict-lint/analyze-strict.txt and reports '430 issues found.' Sample lines: "error - A value of type 'dynamic' can't be assigned to a variable of type 'bool' - lib/core/services/pip_service.dart:55:27 - invalid_assignment"; "info - 'Future'-returning calls in a non-'async' function - lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:62:5 - discarded_futures"; "info - The referenced name isn't visible in scope - lib/core/services/location_service.dart:15:44 - comment_references".
- **Verifier corrections:** Cited line numbers are approximate within a line or two. The playback 'spins forever' scenario needs file.exists() or the probe to throw, which is uncommon.
- **Recommendation:** Enable unawaited_futures, discarded_futures and comment_references, plus `analyzer: language: strict-casts: true`. Wrap the deliberate fire-and-forget calls in unawaited() or add try/catch, and change the pip_service calls to `invokeMethod<bool>(...) ?? false`.
- **Fix note:** 7 stricter lints adopted, 41 hits fixed; avoid_slow_async_io deferred

#### F082

**The Gradle signing gate matches 'release'/'bundle' substrings in task names, demanding secrets for bundleDebug/lintRelease while assemble and build bypass it**

Severity: **low** · Category: build-ci · Location: `android/app/build.gradle.kts:51` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/build.gradle.kts:54`

- **What is wrong:** releaseBuildRequested is true whenever any requested task name, lower-cased, contains 'release' or 'bundle' (lines 49-52), and in that case missing signing values throw (lines 54-58). As a result, bundleDebug (`flutter build appbundle --debug`), lintRelease and testReleaseUnitTest all demand production signing secrets, even though none of them uses the release key. Meanwhile `./gradlew assemble` and `./gradlew build` include assembleRelease but bypass the gate, and fail later inside AGP's signing validation with a less specific error.
- **Failure scenario:** A contributor without signing material runs `flutter build appbundle --debug` to test Play delivery on an internal track, or runs `./gradlew lintRelease`. Gradle aborts at configuration time with 'Release signing config missing...', although neither task needs the key.
- **Evidence:** android/app/build.gradle.kts:49-52: `gradle.startParameter.taskNames.any { task -> val normalized = task.lowercase(); normalized.contains("release") || normalized.contains("bundle") }`. The string 'bundledebug' contains 'bundle'.
- **Recommendation:** Match only signing tasks, using a regex such as `(assemble|bundle|package|sign).*release` evaluated in gradle.taskGraph.whenReady, or drop the early throw and let AGP's signing validation fail only for tasks that actually sign.
- **Fix note:** signing gate only for real release packaging tasks

#### F083

**MainActivity lost the template's android:taskAffinity="" (f0c1f08), leaving Android 7-10 devices (minSdk 24) exposed to StrandHogg-style task hijacking**

Severity: **low** · Category: security · Location: `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:37` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/build.gradle.kts:76`

- **What is wrong:** The Flutter app template sets android:taskAffinity="" on MainActivity. Commit f0c1f08 removed it, so the exported launcher activity now uses the default affinity (the package name). Android says the StrandHogg task-affinity attack is fully patched only from API 30, and that version 1 can be partly mitigated through app configuration. With minSdk 24, devices on Android 7-10 remain exposed. The impact for MotoCam is limited because it collects no credentials. A hijacked task could still place a fake MotoCam screen in front of the rider when they tap the launcher icon, for example a fake permission or 'All files access' prompt.
- **Failure scenario:** On an Android 9 phone, a malicious app declares an activity with taskAffinity="com.example.motocam" and allowTaskReparenting. When the rider next launches MotoCam from the launcher, the attacker's activity appears in MotoCam's task, imitating its UI to phish for permissions or show a fake recording state.
- **Evidence:** AndroidManifest.xml:37-46: the activity has android:exported="true" and android:launchMode="singleTop", and no taskAffinity. `git show f0c1f08 -- android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` contains the removed line '- android:taskAffinity=""'. Android docs (StrandHogg risk): "Android SDK versions 30 and newer (Android 11) contain the appropriate OS patches ... it is possible to partially mitigate version 1 of the StrandHogg attack through individual application configuration". android/app/build.gradle.kts:76 sets minSdk = flutter.minSdkVersion (24).
- **Verifier corrections:** The Android StrandHogg page names only minSdk 30 as the mitigation. It does not name taskAffinity="" as the app-level fix; that comes from the Flutter template and lint guidance. taskAffinity="" also does nothing against StrandHogg 2.0 on unpatched devices.
- **Recommendation:** Restore android:taskAffinity="" on MainActivity in AndroidManifest.xml, then re-test PiP entry and return, and the notification content-intent tap.
- **Fix note:** taskAffinity="" restored

#### F084

**The recording notification's small icon is the opaque launcher PNG, so it renders as a plain white square**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:94` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/src/main/res/mipmap-xxhdpi/ic_launcher.png`

- **What is wrong:** `setSmallIcon(R.mipmap.ic_launcher)` (94) uses the launcher icon. At every density that PNG is RGB with no alpha channel (colour type 2), and the project has no monochrome notification drawable: res/ holds only the launcher mipmaps, launch_background and two xml rule files. Android draws notification small icons from the alpha channel alone, so the recording indicator in the status bar and the notification header comes out as a solid white square rather than a recognisable MotoCam or recording glyph.
- **Failure scenario:** With the phone locked mid-ride, the rider checks the status bar or lock screen to confirm MotoCam is recording and sees only an anonymous white square.
- **Evidence:** BackgroundRecordingService.kt:94 `.setSmallIcon(R.mipmap.ic_launcher)`; PNG header check: `mipmap-hdpi/ic_launcher.png 72 72 colortype 2`, `mipmap-mdpi/ic_launcher.png 48 48 colortype 2`, `mipmap-xhdpi/ic_launcher.png 96 96 colortype 2`, `mipmap-xxhdpi/ic_launcher.png 144 144 colortype 2`, `mipmap-xxxhdpi/ic_launcher.png 192 192 colortype 2` (2 = RGB without alpha); `find android/app/src/main/res -type f` lists only those PNGs, launch_background.xml (x2), styles.xml (x2), backup_rules.xml and data_extraction_rules.xml.
- **Recommendation:** Add a white-on-transparent vector drawable (for example res/drawable/ic_stat_recording.xml) and pass it to setSmallIcon. Optionally call setColor for an accent colour.
- **Fix note:** monochrome notification icon

#### F085

**The foreground notification is rebuilt every second via startService(ACTION_UPDATE) with several binder calls on the Android main (UI) thread**

Severity: **low** · Category: performance · Location: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:82` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:418`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:45`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/BackgroundRecordingService.kt:69`

- **What is wrong:** RecordingScreen forwards every 1 s elapsed tick to updateForegroundRecording, which calls startService(ACTION_UPDATE). onStartCommand then rebuilds the notification from scratch: getNotificationChannel (a binder call), getLaunchIntentForPackage (a PackageManager query), PendingIntent.getActivity and PendingIntent.getService (two ActivityManager calls), and NotificationManager.notify, which fans the update out to SystemUI and any notification listeners. All of it runs on the Android main thread, which in Flutter 3.41 is also the Dart UI thread, only to change the 'Elapsed: hh:mm:ss' text.
- **Failure scenario:** A 3-hour ride performs ~10,800 service-start, rebuild and notify cycles of about 5-6 IPCs each. They continue through screen-off recording, when nothing displays the text, adding wake-ups in system_server and SystemUI and on the app's own main thread.
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:418-420 '_recordingTimerSubscription = camera.timerStream.listen((elapsed) { _backgroundRecordingService.updateForegroundRecording(elapsed: elapsed); });'; camera_provider.dart:893 1 s ticker. MainActivity.kt:76-83 '"updateForegroundRecording" -> { ... startService(serviceIntent)'. BackgroundRecordingService.kt:45-48 'ACTION_UPDATE -> { ... manager.notify(NOTIFICATION_ID, buildNotification(elapsedMs))'; :70-91 createNotificationChannelIfNeeded(), getLaunchIntentForPackage, PendingIntent.getActivity, PendingIntent.getService; :138 'manager.getNotificationChannel(CHANNEL_ID)'.
- **Recommendation:** In ACTION_START, build the notification once with setWhen(System.currentTimeMillis() - elapsedMs), setUsesChronometer(true) and setShowWhen(true). Drop the per-second updateForegroundRecording subscription, or throttle it to about once a minute and cache the builder and PendingIntents.
- **Fix note:** chronometer notification; no per-second service restarts

#### F086

**enterPipMode ignores enterPictureInPictureMode's boolean and isPipSupported checks only SDK_INT, so refused or unsupported PiP is reported as success and the 'Stop first to exit' notice never shows**

Severity: **low** · Category: android-platform · Location: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:134` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:127`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:305`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:988`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:134`, `lib/core/services/pip_service.dart:62-69`

- **What is wrong:** The handler ignores the boolean returned by enterPictureInPictureMode and returns true on API 26+ (lines 129-139). isPipSupported only checks SDK_INT (line 127). Activity.enterPictureInPictureMode returns false when the device lacks FEATURE_PICTURE_IN_PICTURE (for example Android Go / low-RAM devices) or when the user has disabled PiP for the app. As a result, the Dart notice at recording_screen.dart:988-995 is unreachable on API 26+. PiP is also entered from onPause/onUserLeaveHint rather than via setPictureInPictureParams(...setAutoEnterEnabled(true)) on API 31+.
- **Failure scenario:** With PiP disabled for MotoCam, pressing Back or HISTORY during a recording does nothing visible. Pressing Home stops the activity, which stops camera capture (see the screen-off finding).
- **Evidence:** `enterPictureInPictureMode(params)` followed by `result.success(true)` (MainActivity.kt:134-135); `result.success(Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= Build.VERSION_CODES.O)` (:127).
- **Verifier corrections:** isPipSupported is never called anywhere in lib/ (grep finds only its definition in pip_service.dart), so its SDK-only check is dead code with no user-visible effect. The defect that matters is the ignored return value. The 'Home stops camera capture' consequence depends on a separate finding.
- **Recommendation:** Return the boolean: `result.success(enterPictureInPictureMode(params))`, wrapped in try/catch that returns false. Optionally make isPipSupported also check packageManager.hasSystemFeature(PackageManager.FEATURE_PICTURE_IN_PICTURE).
- **Fix note:** PiP support check and real enter result

#### F087

**MediaStore export leaves the IS_PENDING item behind when the copy fails, and the Dart fallback then creates a duplicate**

Severity: **low** · Category: android-platform · Location: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:194` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:230`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1393`

- **What is wrong:** On API 29+ the item is inserted with IS_PENDING=1 (lines 182-191). If openOutputStream or copyTo throws (194-198), the catch block (230-233) returns EXPORT_FAILED without deleting the item. CameraProvider then falls back to a dart:io copy of the same clip (camera_provider.dart:1391-1399).
- **Failure scenario:** Storage runs out midway through copying an incident clip. A hidden, partial pending row keeps its bytes until MediaProvider expires it. On API 30+ the fallback then writes a second copy, wasting space exactly when space is scarce.
- **Evidence:** `val itemUri = resolver.insert(collection, values) ?: throw ...` / `resolver.openOutputStream(itemUri)?.use { ... } ?: throw ...` / `catch (e: Exception) { runOnUiThread { result.error("EXPORT_FAILED", e.message, null) } }`.
- **Verifier corrections:** In the out-of-space scenario the dart:io fallback copy would most likely also fail and be caught, returning null, so the duplicate mostly arises from other failure causes such as a transient I/O error or a provider error.
- **Recommendation:** Track itemUri in a variable outside the copy, and call resolver.delete(itemUri, null, null) in the catch block when it is non-null. Optionally skip the dart:io fallback when the native error code shows a MediaStore write failure.
- **Fix note:** failed MediaStore export deletes its pending item

#### F088

**readBatteryStatus keeps EXTRA_TEMPERATURE only when > 0, so battery temperatures at or below 0 C are reported as unknown**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:258` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:712-726`

- **What is wrong:** EXTRA_TEMPERATURE is a signed value in tenths of a degree C, but readBatteryStatus keeps it only when it is greater than 0 (257-262). Zero and negative temperatures therefore become null.
- **Failure scenario:** On a winter ride with the battery at -5 C, the Temp tile shows '--' and the preflight temperature row disappears (recording_screen.dart:712-726, 1630-1632), just when a cold-battery warning would matter.
- **Evidence:** MainActivity.kt:257-259 `val tempTenthsC = it.getIntExtra(BatteryManager.EXTRA_TEMPERATURE, Int.MIN_VALUE); if (tempTenthsC != Int.MIN_VALUE && tempTenthsC > 0) { tempTenthsC / 10.0`.
- **Verifier corrections:** The finding implies a cold-battery warning would appear if the value got through. No such warning exists; only a hot (>= 43 C) warning does.
- **Recommendation:** Change the condition to `tempTenthsC != Int.MIN_VALUE`. Optionally add a low-temperature preflight warning.
- **Fix note:** battery temperature <= 0 C reported

#### F089

**Launch and Normal themes inherit Theme.Light, so in system light mode every cold start flashes a light window before the all-black UI**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `android/app/src/main/res/values/styles.xml:4` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/src/main/res/values/styles.xml:15`, `android/app/src/main/res/drawable-v21/launch_background.xml:4`, `lib/main.dart:59-63`

- **What is wrong:** Outside system night mode, both LaunchTheme and NormalTheme inherit `@android:style/Theme.Light.NoTitleBar` (values/styles.xml:4, :15), and the launch drawable paints `?android:colorBackground` (drawable-v21/launch_background.xml:4). The Flutter app, however, defines only a dark theme (main.dart:59-63), and the recording screen is pure black (recording_screen.dart:1042). So in light mode every cold start shows a light window before the first Flutter frame appears.
- **Failure scenario:** At night, a rider whose phone is in light mode opens MotoCam on the handlebar mount and is dazzled by a bright flash before the black recording screen appears.
- **Evidence:** values/styles.xml:4 `<style name="LaunchTheme" parent="@android:style/Theme.Light.NoTitleBar">` and :15 `<style name="NormalTheme" parent="@android:style/Theme.Light.NoTitleBar">`; drawable-v21/launch_background.xml:4 `<item android:drawable="?android:colorBackground" />`; values-night/styles.xml:4,15 use `Theme.Black.NoTitleBar`; main.dart:59-63 `theme: ThemeData(colorSchemeSeed: Colors.blue, brightness: Brightness.dark, useMaterial3: true,)` (no darkTheme/themeMode).
- **Recommendation:** Set values/styles.xml parents to Theme.Black.NoTitleBar, matching values-night, or set an explicit black windowBackground, and change drawable/launch_background.xml to black. On API 31+, optionally add a values-v31 style with android:windowSplashScreenBackground set to black.
- **Fix note:** dark launch theme in light mode

#### F090

**Backup and data-extraction rules exclude only the internal 'root' domain, leaving the 'external' domain that holds every recording and GPS trace in device-to-device transfer**

Severity: **low** · Category: privacy · Location: `android/app/src/main/res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml:7` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/src/main/res/xml/backup_rules.xml:3`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:33`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:663`

- **What is wrong:** Both rule files exclude only domain="root" path=".". Android defines 'root' as the app's private internal data directory and 'external' as getExternalFilesDir(). Auto Backup includes 'external' by default, and that is where all segments and ride_*.telemetry.json files live. allowBackup="false" turns off cloud and adb backup. For apps targeting API 31+, though, Android notes that on some manufacturers' devices it does not turn off device-to-device transfer; the <device-transfer> rules govern that, and they leave 'external' in. The release precheck only asserts that the manifest references the rule files (:50-52) and that the files exist (:55-60), not what they exclude. On iOS, recordings go to the Documents directory without isExcludedFromBackup, so they would be included in iCloud and computer backups.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider moves to a new phone with a cable device-to-device transfer, on a device where allowBackup="false" does not block D2D. Gigabytes of ride footage and the full GPS history are copied to the new device, which may also stall on size. This contradicts the project's intent that nothing is backed up or transferred.
- **Evidence:** data_extraction_rules.xml:3-8: '<cloud-backup><exclude domain="root" path="." /></cloud-backup><device-transfer><exclude domain="root" path="." /></device-transfer>'. backup_rules.xml:3 has the same exclusion. AndroidManifest.xml:33-35. Android Auto Backup docs: "root: the directory on the file system where all private files belonging to this app are stored"; "external: the directory returned by getExternalFilesDir()"; default backups include "Files on external storage in the directory returned by getExternalFilesDir(String)"; and "On devices from some device manufacturers, specifying android:allowBackup=false disables cloud-based backup and restore ... but doesn't disable device-to-device transfers". camera_provider.dart:663 uses getExternalStorageDirectory on Android, and :666 uses Documents on iOS.
- **Verifier corrections:** The iOS half of the description duplicates F100 and should be tracked there.
- **Recommendation:** Add `<exclude domain="external" path="." />` (plus sharedpref, database and file as desired) to both <cloud-backup> and <device-transfer> in data_extraction_rules.xml, and add it to backup_rules.xml. Extend release-security-precheck.ps1 to assert the external exclusion.
- **Fix note:** recordings excluded from backup/device transfer

#### F091

**A Gradle problems report with absolute developer paths is tracked under android/build/, and neither android/build/ nor android/.kotlin/ is git-ignored**

Severity: **low** · Category: repo-hygiene · Location: `android/build/reports/problems/problems-report.html:1` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `.gitignore:33`, `android/.gitignore:1`

- **What is wrong:** f7e2412 (2026-03-17) committed a 142 KB Gradle problems report. It embeds absolute developer paths such as C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Pub/Cache/hosted/pub.dev/mappls_gl-2.0.3/android/build.gradle and the geolocator_android-5.0.2 equivalent, and lists deprecations from the removed mappls_gl plugin. The root .gitignore ignores only /build/ (.gitignore:33), and android/.gitignore (lines 1-14) has no build/ or .kotlin/ entry. A local android/.kotlin/sessions directory already exists.
- **Failure scenario:** A Gradle run that records problems rewrites the tracked HTML, or the Kotlin daemon writes session files. The next `git add -A` then commits a fresh report full of local paths, or Kotlin session state. Meanwhile the public repo keeps advertising a dependency that was removed months ago, along with the developer's directory layout.
- **Evidence:** git ls-files includes android/build/reports/problems/problems-report.html (142,057 bytes), and grep finds the mappls_gl-2.0.3 and geolocator_android-5.0.2 pub-cache paths inside it. `git check-ignore -v android/build/x android/.kotlin/x` exits 1, so neither path is ignored.
- **Verifier corrections:** The embedded paths use Windows backslashes (C:\\Users\\Sarthak\\...), not forward slashes.
- **Recommendation:** Run `git rm --cached -r android/build` and add `build/` and `.kotlin/` to android/.gitignore, or `/android/build/` and `/android/.kotlin/` to the root .gitignore.
- **Fix note:** android/build untracked and ignored

#### F092

**Resolution-preset converters remain duplicated in AppSettingsProvider and CameraProvider and both copies are used, despite cd39097 claiming their removal**

Severity: **low** · Category: maintainability · Location: `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:148` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:161`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:199`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:212`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:852`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:979`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1362`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1774`

- **What is wrong:** AppSettingsProvider.toResolutionPreset (app_settings_provider.dart:148-159) duplicates CameraProvider.presetForResolution (camera_provider.dart:199-210) logic for logic. AppSettingsProvider.fromResolutionPreset (:161-176) duplicates CameraProvider.resolutionForPreset (:212-225) the same way. RecordingScreen uses both reverse mappers: AppSettingsProvider.fromResolutionPreset at recording_screen.dart:852, :979 and :1362, and CameraProvider.resolutionForPreset at :1774. toResolutionPreset has no caller at all. Commit cd39097's message lists 'Refactor: remove duplicated resolution preset conversion methods', but its diff contains no preset-related lines.
- **Failure scenario:** A maintainer settles the open question of mapping 480p to ResolutionPreset.low (about 240p on Android, 352x288 on iOS) by editing only one copy. The settings write-back in _syncSettingsWithCameraCapabilities (recording_screen.dart:979) and the 'will auto-fallback to Np' warning (:1774) then disagree. The stored resolution flips on every camera init, while the UI promises a different fallback.
- **Evidence:** app_settings_provider.dart:148-159 and camera_provider.dart:199-210 use the same thresholds (480/720/1080 mapping to low/high/veryHigh, else ultraHigh). app_settings_provider.dart:161-176 and camera_provider.dart:212-225 contain the same switch. grep finds toResolutionPreset only at its definition. `git show cd39097 -- lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart | grep '^[-+].*[Pp]reset'` prints nothing.
- **Recommendation:** Delete AppSettingsProvider.toResolutionPreset and fromResolutionPreset. Switch the three recording_screen.dart call sites to CameraProvider.resolutionForPreset, and add a small unit test that pins the mapping.
- **Fix note:** single resolution/preset mapping in CameraProvider

#### F093

**hasPermanentlyDenied is also set by recommended permissions, so the required-permissions dialog shows a misleading 'permanently denied' note that steers away from Request Again**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:66` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:121-153`

- **What is wrong:** `hasPermanentlyDenied` is set when any permission is permanently denied, including recommended ones such as notifications and background location (66-68, in the shared `ensure` helper used at 78-103). The blocking dialog lists only the missing required permissions, yet appends 'Some permissions are permanently denied. Use Open Settings to enable them.' whenever the flag is set (recording_screen.dart:125-132).
- **Failure scenario:** Notifications are permanently denied after two earlier denials, and Camera has been denied once. The dialog lists 'Camera' along with the permanent-denial note, so the rider chooses Open Settings, whose re-check does not work (see the 'Open Settings' finding), even though Request Again would have shown the camera prompt.
- **Evidence:** app_permission_service.dart:66-68 `if (status.isPermanentlyDenied) { hasPermanentlyDenied = true; }`; :91-103 recommended items go through the same `ensure(...)`; recording_screen.dart:125-128 `final missing = resolvedAudit.missingRequired.join(', '); final permanentNote = resolvedAudit.hasPermanentlyDenied ? '\n\nSome permissions are permanently denied. Use Open Settings to enable them.' : '';`.
- **Recommendation:** Track a permanentlyDeniedRequired list in ensure(), adding only when required && status.isPermanentlyDenied. Show the note only when that list is non-empty, and name those permissions.
- **Fix note:** permanent-denial note only for required permissions

#### F094

**LocationService and CameraPreviewPip are dead files that the README project tree and dartdoc still describe as live components**

Severity: **low** · Category: maintainability · Location: `lib/core/services/location_service.dart:15` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/widgets/camera_preview_pip.dart:8`, `lib/core/services/location_service.dart:55`, `README.md:60`, `README.md:70`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1116`, `README.md:82`, `lib/core/services/location_service.dart:15`
