- **What is wrong:** No library imports lib/core/services/location_service.dart (107 lines) or lib/features/camera/widgets/camera_preview_pip.dart (56 lines), and neither appears in the coverage lcov. - LocationService has had no caller since e607c05 (2026-02-21). cd39097 still rewrote it ('Fix: location_service stream leak') and added a dartdoc saying it is 'used by [TelemetryProvider] for ride tracking' (:15-16). TelemetryProvider owns its own Geolocator stream (telemetry_provider.dart:109-140), and the analyzer cannot resolve that doc reference. startTracking's doc claims a second call 'is a no-op and returns the existing stream' (:55-57), but the code calls Geolocator.getPositionStream again (:59-66). - CameraPreviewPip lost its only user when 712d087 deleted lib/features/home/home_screen.dart. The current PiP layout (recording_screen.dart:1116-1203) renders no preview. README.md:60 and :70 list both files as live components.
- **Failure scenario:** A developer trusts README.md:60 and the dartdoc and wires LocationService.startTracking() into a new feature as 'the app's GPS layer'. It then runs a second position stream (high accuracy, 10 m filter) alongside TelemetryProvider's bestForNavigation/500 ms stream, against settings the rest of the app does not use. Meanwhile both files keep appearing in format and lint churn: both are in the dart format 'changed' list.
- **Evidence:** Import graph (`grep -rn '^import' lib test`): nothing imports either file. `git grep -n 'LocationService' e607c05 -- lib` finds only the file itself; the last user was 95a364b:lib/features/map/widgets/map_view.dart:17. `git show --stat 712d087` shows 'deleted: lib/features/home/home_screen.dart'. The strict lint reports 'The referenced name isn't visible in scope - lib/core/services/location_service.dart:15:44 - comment_references'.
- **Recommendation:** Delete lib/core/services/location_service.dart and lib/features/camera/widgets/camera_preview_pip.dart, and remove their two README tree entries.
- **Fix note:** dead CameraPreviewPip and LocationService deleted (README part: docs workflow)

#### F095

**The public export fallback, the 'already public' check and the history trust root hard-code /storage/emulated/0**

Severity: **low** · Category: android-platform · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:45` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:71`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1349`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1415`

- **What is wrong:** _androidPublicExportDirectory is the literal '/storage/emulated/0/Movies/MotoCam/Recordings' (lines 45-46). It is used for the dart:io fallback copy (1415) and for the 'already public' check (1349), and ride_record.dart:71-72 trusts the same literal. Secondary users and work profiles use /storage/emulated/<userId>. On Android 10, with targetSdk 36 and no requestLegacyExternalStorage, scoped storage blocks direct writes to Movies.
- **Failure scenario:** In a work profile, as a secondary user, or on Android 10, a failed MediaStore export cannot fall back, and the clip silently stays private.
- **Evidence:** `static const String _androidPublicExportDirectory = '/storage/emulated/0/Movies/MotoCam/Recordings';`
- **Verifier corrections:** Narrow the failure scope. On Android 11+ for the primary user, the dart:io fallback can write to Movies/ (the File API is allowed for app-attributed media), so the failure applies only to Android 10, to API <29 (no runtime WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE) and to secondary users or work profiles. The 1349 'already public' check is effectively dead: session segments are always persisted into the private recordings directory, and the Kotlin pre-Q export writes to Movies/MotoCam/Videos, not to .../Recordings.
- **Recommendation:** Delete _copySegmentToAndroidPublicGallery and the 1349 public-path branch, and rely on the Kotlin export: MediaStore on Q+ and getExternalStoragePublicDirectory plus scan below Q. In ride_record.dart, drop the literal trust root or get it from the platform, for example Environment.getExternalStoragePublicDirectory exposed over the media channel.
- **Fix note:** hard-coded /storage/emulated/0 paths removed

#### F096

**The '480p' option maps to ResolutionPreset.low (352x288 on iOS, 320x240 preview on Android) instead of medium**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:200` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:148-176`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:199`, `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:148`, `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:161`

- **What is wrong:** presetForResolution sends any value of 480 or less to ResolutionPreset.low (199-202), and resolutionForPreset maps low back to 480 for display (214-216). camera_platform_interface documents low as '352x288 on iOS, ~240p on Android' and medium as '~480p' (resolution_preset.dart:11-15). camera_avfoundation 0.10.1 uses the cif352x288 session preset for low (DefaultCamera.swift:314-316). CameraX caps the low preview at 320x240 4:3 (android_camera_camerax.dart:1588-1590), although its video uses SD quality.
- **Failure scenario:** An iPhone rider picks 480p to save space and gets 352x288 video, while the chips, the settings and the ride metadata all say 480p. On Android, the preview switches to 4:3 when 480p is chosen.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:199-202 `static ResolutionPreset presetForResolution(int resolution) { if (resolution <= 480) { return ResolutionPreset.low; }`; camera_platform_interface-2.12.0 resolution_preset.dart:11-15 `/// 352x288 on iOS, ~240p on Android and Web` (low) and `/// ~480p` (medium); android_camera_camerax.dart:1589-1590 `boundSize = CameraSize(width: 320, height: 240); aspectRatio = AspectRatio.ratio4To3;`.
- **Recommendation:** Map 480 to ResolutionPreset.medium in both presetForResolution (camera_provider.dart:199) and toResolutionPreset (app_settings_provider.dart:148), changing them together or making one call the other. The unsupported-preset and FPS caches are keyed by preset name, so stale 'low' entries become harmless.
- **Fix note:** 480 maps to ResolutionPreset.medium

#### F097

**initializeCamera re-requests camera, microphone and notification permissions right after the bootstrap audit, so first launch shows the notification prompt twice**

Severity: **low** · Category: android-platform · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:377` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:91`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:377`, `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:78-95`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:91-113`

- **What is wrong:** The bootstrap audit requests Permission.notification (app_permission_service.dart:91-95). Right after that, initializeCamera requests it again, as it does on every camera init (camera_provider.dart:376-378). Android 13+ allows a second prompt after one denial, so the rider sees the dialog twice back to back, and a second denial makes the permission permanently denied.
- **Failure scenario:** First launch on Android 13+: the rider taps 'Don't allow' on the notification prompt and immediately gets the same prompt again from camera initialization.
- **Evidence:** `if (Platform.isAndroid) { await Permission.notification.request(); }` (camera_provider.dart:376-378) runs after `await ensure(Permission.notification, ...)` (app_permission_service.dart:91-95).
- **Verifier corrections:** The deny-twice rule is general Android runtime-permission behaviour, not something specific to Android 13. Only POST_NOTIFICATIONS itself is new in Android 13. initializeCamera is also reached from settings-apply (camera_provider.dart:529, recording_screen.dart:570) and from returning from History (recording_screen.dart:1021), where the audit does not run again.
- **Recommendation:** Remove only the Permission.notification.request() call from initializeCamera, or check .status there without requesting. Keep the camera and microphone requests, because the re-init paths do not run the audit again.
- **Fix note:** no duplicate notification prompt at init

#### F098

**With 'Audio Off', camera_android_camerax still enables muted audio, opening the microphone and muxing a silent track whenever RECORD_AUDIO is granted**

Severity: **low** · Category: privacy · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:641` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **owner decision**

Also affects: `pub-cache camera_android_camerax-0.7.1/lib/src/android_camera_camerax.dart:1199`, `pub-cache camera_android_camerax-0.7.1/android/src/main/java/io/flutter/plugins/camerax/PendingRecordingProxyApi.java:40`

- **What is wrong:** With enableAudio: false, camera_android_camerax still calls pendingRecording.withAudioEnabled(initialMuted: true) (pub-cache android_camera_camerax.dart:1199-1202). The native side enables audio whenever RECORD_AUDIO is granted (PendingRecordingProxyApi.java:40-48). So with the setting off, CameraX still opens an AudioRecord session for every segment and muxes a muted track.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider granted the microphone once and later switched 'Record audio' off. Android still shows the microphone-in-use indicator during every ride, and the app holds the mic, competing with calls and voice assistants. This undermines the rider's trust that audio is not captured.
- **Evidence:** Recording made with the log line `[MotoCam] Audio enabled: false` and RECORD_AUDIO granted: ffprobe shows `codec_name=amr_nb codec_type=audio ... duration=262.44`, and volumedetect reports `mean_volume: -91.0 dB`. audioserver logged `App op 27 missing, silencing record AttributionSourceState{... packageName: com.example.motocam ...}` (C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/androidscan/pulled/REC8872897093834849105.mp4).
- **Recommendation:** Patch or fork the plugin, or file an upstream fix, so that enableAudio=false skips withAudioEnabled. In the meantime, drop the microphone FGS type when audio is off and tell the user they can revoke the microphone permission, or call revokeSelfPermissionOnKill(RECORD_AUDIO) on API 33+.
- **Owner decision:** Choosing between maintaining a forked or patched camera plugin and revoking RECORD_AUDIO on the user's behalf, which would re-prompt if audio is turned back on, is a product and maintenance decision.
- **Fix note:** owner-only (decision 7)

#### F099

**_setupRecordingsDirectory re-resolves the recordings root on every init and silently falls back to app documents or the evictable cache, hiding earlier rides**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:663` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:389-395`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1535-1548`

- **What is wrong:** _setupRecordingsDirectory runs on every initializeCamera (389). On Android, if getExternalStorageDirectory() returns null even once, the root becomes app documents (663-664). If creating the directory throws, it becomes getApplicationCacheDirectory()/MotoCam Recordings (684-696), which the OS may clear. If both fail, the old value is silently kept (697-699). History and retention only look at the current root (1535-1548), and the rider is never told.
- **Failure scenario:** Shared storage is briefly unavailable when the camera initializes, so the next rides go to app documents or the cache. Earlier rides vanish from history, and rides stored in the cache can be removed by the system when storage runs low.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:663-664 `baseDirectory = (await getExternalStorageDirectory()) ?? await getApplicationDocumentsDirectory();`; 686-695 `// Fallback to app cache ... final cacheDir = await getApplicationCacheDirectory(); ... _recordingsDirectory = fallbackDir.path;`.
- **Recommendation:** Resolve and save the root once. If it is unavailable later, fail init with a visible error instead of switching roots, never use the cache directory for recordings, and let history scan every known root.
- **Fix note:** recordings root resolved once; no cache/documents fallback

#### F100

**On iOS all recordings and GPS JSON live in Documents and are backed up to iCloud with no exclusion, while Android disables backup**

Severity: **low** · Category: privacy · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:665` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **owner decision**

Also affects: `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:33`, `android/app/src/main/res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml:3`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:678`

- **What is wrong:** Android blocks all backup and device-to-device transfer of app data: allowBackup="false" (AndroidManifest.xml:33-35), backup_rules.xml:3, and data_extraction_rules.xml:3-8 `<exclude domain="root" path="." />`. On iOS, segments and the ride JSON go to getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()/MotoCam Recordings (camera_provider.dart:665-666). The JSON, which holds the full GPS track, sits next to the segments (telemetry_provider.dart:678-697). Apple documents that Documents is backed up by iTunes and iCloud. Nothing sets NSURLIsExcludedFromBackupKey, and there is no native iOS code that could. The volume is large. The defaults are 1080p at 8 Mbps with 300 s segments and 24 rolling segments per session (app_settings_provider.dart:94-101). That is about 300 MB per segment and up to about 7.2 GB per ride. The rolling cap applies per session only (camera_provider.dart:1117), so successive rides accumulate.
- **Failure scenario:** On an iPhone with iCloud Backup turned on, every ride's video and its GPS trace go into the rider's iCloud backup. The traces can reveal home and work locations. On the 5 GB tier, a single default ride exceeds the quota, and the whole device backup starts failing. The Android build deliberately keeps the same data out of backups.
- **Evidence:** - camera_provider.dart:665-666: `} else if (Platform.isIOS) { baseDirectory = await getApplicationDocumentsDirectory();` - AndroidManifest.xml:33: `android:allowBackup="false"` - data_extraction_rules.xml:3-8: `<cloud-backup><exclude domain="root" path="." /></cloud-backup><device-transfer><exclude domain="root" path="." /></device-transfer>` - Apple File System Programming Guide, entry for Documents/: 'The contents of this directory are backed up by iTunes and iCloud.' - app_settings_provider.dart:96 `int _videoBitrateMbps = 8;`, :100 `int _segmentDurationSeconds = 300;`, :101 `int _loopSegmentCount = 24;`
- **Verifier corrections:** The file path is lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart, not app_settings_provider.dart at an unspecified path. Whether the 8 Mbps bitrate is actually applied by the iOS camera plugin was not verified, so per-ride sizes are approximate.
- **Recommendation:** Add a small iOS method channel, or a plugin, that sets URLResourceValues.isExcludedFromBackup = true on the MotoCam Recordings directory at setup and on each new segment and telemetry JSON. Alternatively, store recordings under Application Support with the flag set.
- **Owner decision:** Whether iOS ride footage and GPS traces should be backed up to iCloud is a product and privacy choice, since Android deliberately disables backup. The owner should decide whether to exclude it always or make it a user setting.
- **Fix note:** owner-only (decision 7)

#### F101

**Pending gallery exports of locked clips live only in memory, are cleared by the next START and lost if the process dies**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:865` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1505-1513`

- **What is wrong:** At stop, locked segments are queued in memory and exported 2 s later, one MediaStore copy at a time (1338-1402). startRecording clears _pendingGalleryExports (865), which also ends a running export loop after its current item (1375). The queue is never saved or retried.
- **Failure scenario:** The rider stops with three locked incident clips and restarts within a few seconds, or the app is killed. Clips not yet copied never reach the gallery; only the private copies remain, and the retention sweep can delete those.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:865 `_pendingGalleryExports.clear();`; 1365-1367 `Future<void>.delayed(const Duration(seconds: 2), () async { try { await _drainGalleryExportQueue();`; 1375-1376 `while (_pendingGalleryExports.isNotEmpty) { final sourcePath = _pendingGalleryExports.removeAt(0);`.
- **Recommendation:** Don't clear _pendingGalleryExports in startRecording. Save pending export paths, for example in the ride's telemetry sidecar or SharedPreferences, and retry them at launch or after the next stop.
- **Fix note:** export queue no longer cleared/duplicated; persistence across process death not done

#### F102

**Every private loop segment is handed to MediaScanner (with no .nomedia), so app-private footage can be indexed into MediaStore on older Android**

Severity: **low** · Category: privacy · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1140` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1433`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1398`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:150`

- **What is wrong:** On Android, after each segment is moved into the app-specific recordings directory, _registerCompletedSegment calls _scanMediaFile(persistedVideoPath) for every segment, locked or not. The helper's comment states the intent: 'Scan media file to make it visible in gallery'. The export fallback also scans the private source path when copying fails (:1396-1399), which is the expected outcome on API < 29, where WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE is declared but never requested. No .nomedia file is created. Android 10+ treats Android/data as hidden from the media scanner, so there the calls are wasted IPC. On Android 7-9, the legacy scanner's explicit scanFile is expected to insert these files into MediaStore. That would expose private loop footage to gallery apps and leave stale rows once the loop deletes the files. The legacy behaviour was not verified on a device; only an API 36 emulator was available.
- **Failure scenario:** On an Android 8/9 dashcam phone, every 5-minute loop segment appears in Google Photos or Gallery under 'MotoCam Recordings', although the rider expects only locked clips to leave the app. Segments later deleted by the loop leave broken thumbnails behind.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1140-1148: `if (Platform.isAndroid) { if (deferMaintenance) { ... unawaited(_scanMediaFile(persistedVideoPath)); ... } else { unawaited(_scanMediaFile(persistedVideoPath)); } }`. :1433-1436: '// Scan media file to make it visible in gallery ... await platform.invokeMethod('scanMediaFile', {'path': filePath});'. :1396-1399 falls back to `await _scanMediaFile(sourcePath);`. MainActivity.kt:150-156 calls `MediaScannerConnection.scanFile(this, arrayOf(path), null)`. AndroidManifest.xml:23-24 declares WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE with maxSdkVersion 28, and nothing in lib/ requests it at runtime. `grep -rn nomedia lib android/app/src` finds nothing.
- **Verifier corrections:** Not verified on a physical API ≤28 device. The verdict rests on the AOSP pie MediaScanner source. The comment on the helper at :1433 says 'Scan media file to make it visible in gallery'.
- **Recommendation:** Remove the _scanMediaFile call for private segments in _registerCompletedSegment and the private-source scan in the export failure path. Scan only files actually written to shared storage, and create an empty .nomedia file in the recordings directory.
- **Fix note:** private segments not media-scanned; .nomedia

#### F103

**_pruneOrphanTelemetryFiles fully jsonDecodes every ride JSON on the UI isolate, twice per stop and once per resume recovery**

Severity: **low** · Category: performance · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1515` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1153`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1258`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1064`

- **What is wrong:** stopRecording registers the final segment without deferral, which runs _enforceRecordingRetention(includeTelemetryPrune: true), and then calls it again at line 1515. recoverRecordingPipeline runs it once more. _pruneOrphanTelemetryFiles lists the directory recursively and, for every *.telemetry.json, reads the whole file and jsonDecodes it, including all samples, only to read segmentPaths. Measured jsonDecode time is 23 ms for a 2 h ride's 1.66 MB JSON and 320 ms for a 10 h ride's 8.33 MB (i7-1165G7, AOT). Each decode is a single synchronous block, and the number of files grows with every ride (see the storage finding).
- **Failure scenario:** With 40 stored rides of 1-2 h, each stop decodes ~33-66 MB of JSON twice, roughly 1-2 s of UI-thread work on the laptop CPU and more on most phones, before stopRecording even returns. That delays the save and stutters the preview. With a 10 h ride in history, each pass also contains a ~0.3 s or longer freeze.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1494 'await _registerCompletedSegment(cacheVideoPath);' -> :1152-1153 'await _enforceRecordingRetention(includeTelemetryPrune: true);' -> :1194-1195 'await _pruneOrphanTelemetryFiles(recordingsDir);'; :1515 'await _enforceRecordingRetention(includeTelemetryPrune: true);'; :1064 'await _registerCompletedSegment(file.path);' (recovery); :1267-1268 'final raw = await entity.readAsString(); final json = jsonDecode(raw) as Map<String, dynamic>;'. Benchmark: 'n=7200 samples: json=1.66 MB ... jsonDecode=23ms', 'n=36000 samples: json=8.33 MB ... jsonDecode=320ms'.
- **Recommendation:** Drop one of the two prune calls on the stop path (for example pass deferMaintenance or includeTelemetryPrune=false when registering the final segment), and run the prune as deferred maintenance. Avoid full decodes by reading segmentPaths from a small index, or decode inside Isolate.run.
- **Fix note:** orphan-sidecar prune once per stop, in an isolate

#### F104

**getRecordings sorts every file with two statSync calls per comparison, an ordering its only caller discards**

Severity: **low** · Category: performance · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1550` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:75`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1210`

- **What is wrong:** getRecordings lists every file under the recordings directory, all videos plus the JSON files. It sorts them with a comparator that calls File(...).statSync() twice per comparison: about 2 x n x log2(n) blocking stat syscalls in one synchronous sort on the UI/platform thread. RidesListScreen, the only caller, then keeps only *.telemetry.json files and re-sorts rides by createdAt, so the mtime ordering is never used. Commit cd39097 fixed the same pattern in the retention sweep (lines 1210-1218, 'Pre-fetch modification times asynchronously') but not here.
- **Failure scenario:** With 20 rides of up to 24 segments (~500 files), the sort performs ~9,000 synchronous stats. On Android external storage each stat goes through the storage stack (FUSE or sdcardfs), so opening Ride History blocks the thread for a fraction of a second or more before any JSON is parsed. The cost grows with every ride, because storage is never trimmed across rides. Per-stat latency was not measured on a device.
- **Evidence:** camera_provider.dart:1550-1553 'files.sort((a, b) => File(b.path).statSync().modified.compareTo(File(a.path).statSync().modified));'; :1576-1584 every File is appended; rides_list_screen.dart:58-61 '.where((file) => file.path.toLowerCase().endsWith('.telemetry.json'))', :75 'rides.sort((a, b) => b.createdAt.compareTo(a.createdAt));'; camera_provider.dart:1210-1215 the asynchronous pre-fetch used in the sweep.
- **Verifier corrections:** Additional effect: a statSync exception inside the sort, for example from a file deleted during listing, makes getRecordings return [] and Ride History shows empty.
- **Recommendation:** Delete the sort in getRecordings, or add a method that returns only *.telemetry.json files. If an order is ever needed, filter first and pre-fetch stats asynchronously.
- **Fix note:** no statSync sort in getRecordings

#### F105

**RideRecord.fromVideoFile has had no callers since v0.1.11, so video-only ride recovery and its 'VIDEO ONLY (RECOVERED)' badge are dead**

Severity: **low** · Category: maintainability · Location: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:170` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:864`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:162`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:177`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:238`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:339`

- **What is wrong:** RideRecord.fromVideoFile (ride_record.dart:170-251) and _telemetryPathForVideo (:864-870) have no callers. The last call, at rides_list_screen.dart:68, was removed in 76974c1 (v0.1.11). The list now loads rides only through fromTelemetryFile (rides_list_screen.dart:66), which always sets `telemetryPath: telemetryFile.path` (ride_record.dart:339). So `hasTelemetry` (rides_list_screen.dart:162) is always true, and neither the 'VIDEO ONLY (RECOVERED)' badge (:177-195) nor 'Telemetry: Not available' (:238) can render. release-notes/v0.1.10.md still advertises orphan-video recovery and the badge. Related model state is unread as well: isSessionComplete, rideSessionId and RideIntegrityReport.hasMetadata are never read outside the model, and isVerified is read only by tests.
- **Failure scenario:** A ride is interrupted before STOP, for example by an app kill or a dead battery. Its segments have no ride_*.telemetry.json, so they never appear in Ride History. The code suggests a recovery path that would list them as 'VIDEO ONLY (RECOVERED)', so a maintainer investigating 'missing rides' spends time on code that cannot run.
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn fromVideoFile lib test` finds only ride_record.dart:170. `git grep -n fromVideoFile 76974c1^ -- lib` finds 'lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:68: final ride = await RideRecord.fromVideoFile(videoFile);', and at 76974c1 there is no match. ride_record.dart:339 reads `telemetryPath: telemetryFile.path,`. In the lcov, ride_record.dart lines 170-251 have 0 hits.
- **Recommendation:** Either delete fromVideoFile, _telemetryPathForVideo, the !hasTelemetry badge and the 'Not available' branch and add a note to the release notes, or have _loadRides also list video files that no telemetry JSON references and build them with fromVideoFile, plus a test.
- **Owner decision:** Whether orphan-video recovery is a product feature to restore or one to remove is a product decision.
- **Fix note:** orphan videos surfaced via fromVideoFile

#### F106

**Segment quarantine treats the storageDirectory and videoPath parent declared by the very JSON being validated as trusted roots**

Severity: **low** · Category: security · Location: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:489` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:494`, `lib/core/utils/path_utils.dart:74`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:348`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:489`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:481-512`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:514-543`

- **What is wrong:** _trustedRootsForTelemetry adds the JSON's own storageDirectory value and the parent directory of its videoPath as trusted roots. They sit next to the telemetry file's directory and, on Android, the public export directory. The scope check behind the QUARANTINED badge is therefore judged against values supplied by the file being checked. Pointing storageDirectory at another folder or at the volume root, or pointing videoPath at a foreign file, makes arbitrary paths trusted. Those paths are then hashed, opened with VideoPlayerController.file, and re-hashed into the envelope by setLockState. The quarantine that README.md:114 advertises only catches inconsistent files and '..' traversal, not crafted ones.
- **Failure scenario:** A telemetry file is placed in the recordings folder (via USB/adb, or by another app on Android 9 and earlier). It lists segments from another directory, sets "storageDirectory" to that directory or to the volume root (or points videoPath there), and has recomputed hashes. Ride History shows VERIFIED with no QUARANTINED badge, and playback presents the foreign videos as a genuine MotoCam ride.
- **Evidence:** ride_record.dart:487-497: `roots.add(telemetryFile.parent.path); final storageDirectory = _readOptionalString(json, 'storageDirectory'); if (storageDirectory != null) { roots.add(storageDirectory); } ... roots.add(File(fallback).parent.path);`. fallback is the JSON's videoPath (:261-266). path_utils.dart:74-95 checks containment lexically. Measured with flutter test on a copy of lib/ (Windows, so Platform.isAndroid was false and the public export root was not involved): "C1 honest: modified issues=[orphan-integrity-hash:<tmp>\Elsewhere\foreign.mp4, quarantined-segment-paths:1] quarantined=1"; "C2 storageDirectory=foreign: verified issues=[] quarantined=0 segments=2"; "C3 storageDirectory=C:\ (volume root): verified issues=[] quarantined=0 segments=2"; "C4 videoPath=foreign file: verified issues=[] quarantined=0 segments=2".
- **Verifier corrections:** Practical impact is bounded because the integrity envelope is unkeyed: anyone who can plant the JSON can also forge a fully 'consistent' ride inside the trusted directory, so the quarantine never resisted crafted files in the first place.
- **Recommendation:** Pass the app-controlled roots (CameraProvider's recordings directory and the export directory) into fromTelemetryFile/verifySegmentIntegrity, and stop deriving roots from the JSON's storageDirectory or videoPath. Optionally resolve symlinks before checking containment.
- **Fix note:** trusted roots only from app-controlled locations

#### F107

**A missing segment file is classified as 'modified' and surfaced as a tampering 'Security warning'**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:711` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:76-117`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1209-1234`

- **What is wrong:** With verifySegments, a declared segment whose file no longer exists adds 'missing-segment-file:<path>', and the ride becomes 'modified' (710-713, 728-731). Playback then shows 'Security warning: this ride shows 1 integrity issue' (ride_playback_screen.dart:76-115). Segments can disappear for benign reasons. One is the app's own directory-wide sweep: when a session overflows with locked segments, it deletes the oldest video files of other rides without updating their JSON (camera_provider.dart:1209-1234). _pruneOrphanTelemetryFiles keeps a JSON while any of its segments still exists (camera_provider.dart:1281-1293).
- **Failure scenario:** After the overflow sweep deletes the oldest segment of an earlier ride, opening that ride shows a security warning as if it had been tampered with.
- **Evidence:** Re-ran .../scratchpad/exp/motocam/test/exp_history_test.dart H (one of two segments deleted): 'H segs=2 lazy=RideIntegrityStatus.verified full=RideIntegrityStatus.modified [missing-segment-file]'.
- **Verifier corrections:** The sweep is reached only when `_sessionSegmentPaths.length > _maxRollingSegments` (camera_provider.dart:1190), which in practice means every segment of the current session is locked. A manual deletion is a more common trigger.
- **Recommendation:** In _verifyIntegrity, record missing files under a separate category (for example an 'incomplete' status, or skip them and rely on playback's missing-range UI). Mark the ride 'modified' only for hash mismatches, missing hashes or a payload mismatch.
- **Fix note:** missing segments reported as INCOMPLETE, not tampering

#### F108

**The writer, reader and playback screen each re-implement telemetry-file rules (segment list, timeline clamping, integrity envelope) with diverging semantics**

Severity: **low** · Category: maintainability · Location: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:815` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:649`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:497`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:457`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:336`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:552`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:593`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:389`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:45` …

- **What is wrong:** There is no shared codec for the ride JSON, so each layer re-derives the same structures under different rules: - Segment list: the writer always appends videoPath when it is missing (telemetry_provider.dart:497-517). The reader appends it only when the list is empty (ride_record.dart:457-479). Playback always appends it (ride_playback_screen.dart:336-353). - Timeline: the writer clamps a negative startMs to 0 (telemetry_provider.dart:552-557). The reader keeps negative starts (ride_record.dart:593-595). Playback clamps start to at least 0, forces end to at least start+1, and re-packs entries monotonically (ride_playback_screen.dart:389-390, 424-425). - Integrity envelope: the writer's _buildIntegrityMetadata (telemetry_provider.dart:649-670) sets segmentCount to the number of persisted paths. RideRecord._buildIntegrityMetadataForPayload (ride_record.dart:815-847), used by setLockState, re-derives the list without trust filtering and counts only files that still exist. - File-name extraction exists four times: ride_record.dart:90-93, telemetry_provider.dart:480-486, camera_provider.dart:1383 and path_utils.dart:100-106. camera_provider uses two different helpers for the same expor…
- **Failure scenario:** A developer adds an envelope field, or changes how videoPath joins segmentPaths, in TelemetryProvider. setLockState then re-signs files in the old shape, and playback builds a different segment list from the one the reader verified. On a secondary Android user or a work profile (/storage/emulated/10), the hard-coded root is wrong. No test fails, because ride_record_test builds its own envelope (test lines 171-235) and never calls stopRideSessionAndPersist.
- **Evidence:** Line references above. In the lcov at C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/coverage/lcov.info, telemetry_provider.dart 362-460 and 497-698 have 0 hits, and ride_record.dart 815-847 has 0 hits.
- **Verifier corrections:** The writer's segmentCount counts paths that are already filtered by directory and existence (telemetry_provider.dart:391-401). The reader's _buildIntegrityMetadataForPayload counts existing paths, so the two differ only in the directory/trust filter, not in what is counted.
- **Recommendation:** Move segment-list normalization, timeline normalization and envelope construction into one shared helper in lib/core used by TelemetryProvider, RideRecord.setLockState and RidePlaybackScreen. Use path_utils.fileNameFromPath everywhere, and add one writer-to-reader round-trip test.
- **Fix note:** shared envelope/path helpers; remaining duplication outside history noted

#### F109

**An in-flight auto-advance can load and play the next segment after the app was backgrounded, playing audio in the background**

Severity: **low** · Category: lifecycle · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:143` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:682-710`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:637-639`

- **What is wrong:** didChangeAppLifecycleState pauses only the current _controller on inactive, paused or detached (143-149). An in-flight _advanceToNextSegment, which calls _loadSegment(autoPlay: true) (697), completes later and calls controller.play() (637-639). The new controller registers video_player's lifecycle observer during initialize(), after 'paused' was already delivered (video_player.dart:540-545, 1043-1052), so nothing pauses it. When it finishes, auto-advance moves on to the next segment.
- **Failure scenario:** The rider presses Home just as a segment ends. The old controller is paused, then the next segment finishes initializing and plays with audio while the app is in the background, and playback continues through the following segments.
- **Evidence:** Scratch .../scratchpad/corrhist/test/playback_race_test.dart P6 (completed event, then inactive, hidden and paused during the 300 ms init): 'P6 app paused: lifecycle=AppLifecycleState.paused ... notDisposed=[4:seg1.mp4] playing=[4:seg1.mp4]'.
- **Verifier corrections:** The stated mechanism ('registers its observer after paused was already delivered') is only one ordering. Registration timing does not matter: even when the observer is registered before 'paused', the controller is not playing yet, so the observer does nothing, and _loadSegment calls play() afterwards anyway.
- **Recommendation:** Keep the latest AppLifecycleState in a field (set in didChangeAppLifecycleState). In _loadSegment, skip play() when the state is not resumed, and bail out of _advanceToNextSegment when it is not resumed.
- **Fix note:** playback pauses when backgrounded

#### F110

**_initializePlayback calls setState without a mounted check when probing finds no playable segment, and the probe loop ignores disposal**

Severity: **low** · Category: lifecycle · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:204` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:161-189`

- **What is wrong:** After the awaited probe loop (161-189), the empty-playlist branch calls setState(() { _isLoading = false; }) (204-206) without a mounted check. The loop never checks _isDisposing either, so probing continues after the screen is closed.
- **Failure scenario:** The rider opens a ride whose segments are unreadable and presses back during probing. When probing ends, setState runs on a defunct State. In debug this is a FlutterError; in release it is a null-check error on the State's element. Either way it surfaces as an uncaught async error.
- **Evidence:** Scratch .../scratchpad/corrhist/test/playback_race_test.dart P5: 'setState() called after dispose(): _RidePlaybackScreenState#f6833(lifecycle state: defunct, not mounted)' ... '#2 _RidePlaybackScreenState._initializePlayback (package:motocam/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:204:7)'.
- **Recommendation:** Add `if (!mounted || _isDisposing) return;` at the top of each probe-loop iteration and right before the setState at line 204.
- **Fix note:** probe loop guarded by mounted/disposing

#### F111

**Footage removed by loop recording becomes an unmarked dead zone on the playback seekbar**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:272` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:355-434`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:459-509`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1117-1138`, `release-notes/v0.1.11.md:8`

- **What is wrong:** The seekbar's length is the telemetry duration (`_totalDuration = telemetryDuration`, 272-278). Loop recording deletes the oldest segments from both the session paths and the timeline (camera_provider.dart:1126-1128), so neither the effective timeline (355-434) nor `_buildMissingSegmentRanges` (459-509) has anything to mark for the overwritten span. The thumb opens at the first retained segment's offset (631-634), and any seek into the earlier span snaps forward to it (1051-1056). No red range, legend entry or message explains this. release-notes/v0.1.11.md:8 lists 'Playback seekbar showing video jumps over missing segments with no visual indication' as fixed.
- **Failure scenario:** With 'Segment length 30 sec' and 'Loop buffer size 6' (3 min of video kept), a 20-minute ride opens with the thumb at about 17:00 of 20:00. Tapping anywhere in the first 85% of the bar jumps back to 17:00 without explanation, while the ride card says 'Duration: 0:20:00' and 'Segments: 6'.
- **Evidence:** ride_playback_screen.dart:272-278 `_totalDuration = telemetryDuration; if (maxSegmentEnd > _totalDuration) { _totalDuration = maxSegmentEnd; }`; :631-634 `_lastKnownGlobalPosition = _clampDuration(_segmentOffsets[index] + clampedPosition, _totalDuration,);`; :1051-1055 `if (position <= start) { return _SegmentSeekTarget(segmentIndex: index, localPosition: Duration.zero,); }`; camera_provider.dart:1126-1128 `final oldestPath = _sessionSegmentPaths.removeAt(removableIndex); _lockedSegmentPaths.remove(oldestPath); _sessionSegmentTimeline.removeWhere((entry) => entry.path == oldestPath);`.
- **Recommendation:** In _buildMissingSegmentRanges, add a range from 0 to the first timeline entry's startMs (and any internal gaps between entries) when it is non-zero, with a distinct 'Overwritten (loop)' style and legend entry. Optionally show the kept-video span on the ride card.
- **Fix note:** uncovered spans shown on seekbar and ride card

#### F112

**Runtime VideoPlayerValue errors are never read, so playback silently stalls and auto-advance never fires**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:660` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1253`

- **What is wrong:** A platform error after initialization sets VideoPlayerValue.erroneous, whose isInitialized is false (video_player.dart:183-188, 661-667). The screen never reads value.hasError or errorDescription. _onControllerTick returns early when !isInitialized (660-662), so auto-advance never fires. play and seekTo do nothing on a controller that is not initialized (video_player.dart:731-734, 797-800). The aspect ratio falls back to 1.0 (1253).
- **Failure scenario:** A segment probes fine but hits a decode or source error partway through the file. The picture freezes, the play button toggles without effect, no message appears and playback does not skip to the next segment.
- **Evidence:** ride_playback_screen.dart:660-662: 'if (controller == null || !controller.value.isInitialized) { return; }'. video_player.dart:663: 'value = VideoPlayerValue.erroneous(e.message!);' with isInitialized: false at :186. No reference to hasError or errorDescription in ride_playback_screen.dart (grep). The mid-file trigger is not reproduced.
- **Recommendation:** In _onControllerTick, check controller.value.hasError before the isInitialized early return. Show a SnackBar with errorDescription and call _advanceToNextSegment(), or offer a retry.
- **Fix note:** runtime controller errors skip to next segment

#### F113

**Incidents are not persisted; playback re-derives markers from 1 Hz samples with a hard-coded 3.2 g / 5 s rule unrelated to the rider's detection sensitivity**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:913` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:415-435`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:700-711`, `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:118-138`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:913`, `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:118`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:286`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:700`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:849`

- **What is wrong:** The saved payload (telemetry_provider.dart:415-435) contains no incident events. _buildIncidentMarkers flags 1 Hz samples whose smoothed accelerationG is >= 3.2, at least 5 s apart (905-933). Live detection uses the rider's sensitivity (2.8, 3.5 or 4.5 g with a 3, 6 or 10 s debounce; app_settings_provider.dart:118-138) on samples at about 5 Hz, with a two-hit rule (telemetry_provider.dart:286-291, 700-711). recordingSettings.requestedIncidentSensitivity is saved (recording_screen.dart:849) but never read.
- **Failure scenario:** At High sensitivity, a crash detected live at 2.9 g, with its segments locked, gets no incident marker. At Low, a 3.3 g pothole that was not detected live appears as 'Incident'. A spike that falls between 1 Hz samples is never marked.
- **Evidence:** ride_playback_screen.dart:913-914: 'const incidentThresholdG = 3.2; const debounceMs = 5000;'. The payload keys at telemetry_provider.dart:415-435 include no incident field. app_settings_provider.dart:118-127 maps low/medium/high to 4.5/3.5/2.8.
- **Verifier corrections:** The claim that live samples arrive at about 5 Hz was not verified (it depends on the sensor stream rate). It does not change the conclusion.
- **Recommendation:** Append each _detectCrash event (elapsedMs, gForce, trigger threshold) to an incidents list in TelemetryProvider and save it in the ride JSON. Draw playback markers from that list, and fall back to the current heuristic only for older rides without it.
- **Fix note:** incidents persisted and drawn from the ride JSON

#### F114

**Speed-graph drag re-adds the finger's offset from centre on every update, acting as a rate-dependent jog where a leftward drag can still move forward**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1168` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1024-1045`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1145-1189`

- **What is wrong:** Each `onHorizontalDragUpdate` computes `target = position + ((dx - w/2)/w) * window` (1168-1175, 1028-1045). Here `position` is the value captured at build time, which during a scrub is the last preview (1236-1238). The finger's offset from the centre is therefore added again on every move event that causes a rebuild. With the finger right of centre, any movement, even to the left, moves playback further forward, and the step size depends on the event and frame rate. A press held longer than kPressTimeout before dragging also fires onTapDown, which seeks immediately (1147-1159). This is inferred from the code, not observed on a device.
- **Failure scenario:** To step back about 2 s, the rider touches the graph right of centre and drags slightly left. Playback jumps forward several seconds instead.
- **Evidence:** ride_playback_screen.dart:1168-1174 `onHorizontalDragUpdate: (details) { final target = _graphTargetForOffset(dx: details.localPosition.dx, width: constraints.maxWidth, currentPosition: position,); _updateScrubPreview(target); }`; :1040-1043 `final deltaMs = ((dx - centerX) / width) * windowMs; return _clampDuration(currentPosition + Duration(milliseconds: deltaMs.round()), _totalDuration,);`; :1236-1238 `final position = _isScrubbing ? (_scrubPreviewPosition ?? _globalPosition()) : _globalPosition();`.
- **Recommendation:** In onHorizontalDragStart, record startPosition and startDx. In the update handler compute startPosition + (dx - startDx)/w * window, or the inverse if the graph should feel like dragging content. Move tap-to-seek to onTapUp so a press-then-drag does not seek first.
- **Fix note:** speed-graph drag anchored; tap on tap-up

#### F116

**A single ValueListenableBuilder rebuilds the whole playback body every 100 ms, and RouteMapView redoes all route work on each rebuild**

Severity: **low** · Category: performance · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1233` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:23`, `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:64`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1700`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:842`

- **What is wrong:** A single ValueListenableBuilder<VideoPlayerValue> wraps the entire body. video_player 2.11.1 updates the position from a 100 ms Timer.periodic while playing, so about 10 times per second the overlay, speed graph, slider, legend and RouteMapView are rebuilt. On each rebuild, RouteMapView does the following: it re-extracts a LatLng for every sample, downsamples, recomputes bounds and constructs a new TileLayer. Each TileLayer constructor creates a new NetworkTileProvider and RetryClient(Client()); this is allocation churn, not a socket leak. It also builds a new PolylineLayer, whose didUpdateWidget unconditionally clears the projection and simplification caches, so up to 1,801 points are re-projected and re-simplified every tick. _SpeedGraphPainter.shouldRepaint returns true on every tick, and paint walks all samples twice. All of this also happens while the map panel is collapsed, because AnimatedCrossFade keeps the hidden child built and laid out.
- **Failure scenario:** Measured on an i7-1165G7 (AOT), route extraction, downsampling and projection alone cost 0.42 ms per rebuild for a 2 h ride (7,200 samples) and 4.5 ms for a 10 h ride (36,000 samples). That is 4-45 ms of UI-thread CPU per second of playback, before flutter_map's simplification and element rebuilds. On a mid-range phone, a long ride's per-tick work can approach the 16 ms frame budget, dropping frames while playing or scrubbing (scrub drags also call setState on every move).
- **Evidence:** ride_playback_screen.dart:1233-1235 'ValueListenableBuilder<VideoPlayerValue>(valueListenable: controller, builder: (context, value, _) {' wrapping the whole Column; :1376-1380 'RouteMapView(telemetryData: widget.ride.samples, height: mapHeight, currentSample: sample,'; :1706-1763 two loops over samples; :1803 'return oldDelegate.currentPositionMs != currentPositionMs ||'. video_player-2.11.1 video_player.dart:739 '_timer = Timer.periodic(const Duration(milliseconds: 100), ('. route_map_view.dart:23 'final routePoints = _downsample(_extractPoints(telemetryData));', :32 'LatLngBounds.fromPoints(routePoints)', :64-78 new TileLayer and PolylineLayer. flutter_map-8.2.2 tile_layer.dart:278 'tileProvider = tileProvider ?? NetworkTileProvider(),'; network/tile_provider.dart:42 '_httpClient = httpClient ?? RetryClient(Client());'; layer_projection_simplification/state.dart:52-58 didUpdateWidget…
- **Verifier corrections:** The '1,801 points' cap is actually about 1800: _downsample keeps every step-th point. Map tiles are not refetched per tick, only objects are churned.
- **Recommendation:** Make RouteMapView stateful and compute routePoints, bounds, zoom and the TileLayer/PolylineLayer once (or memoise on telemetryData identity), feeding only the current marker per tick. Narrow the controller listener to the position-dependent widgets, and skip building the map child when _showRouteMap is false.
- **Fix note:** map layers cached, graph windowed; body still under one ValueListenableBuilder

#### F117

**A translucent marker Listener over the Slider makes one tap trigger both the marker jump and the Slider's own seek; the tap position wins while the SnackBar reports the marker**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1493` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:996-1022`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1439-1448`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:957-994`

- **What is wrong:** The marker Listener (1468-1519) uses HitTestBehavior.translucent over the Slider, so one tap reaches both. Measured order: Listener.down, onChangeStart, onChanged, Listener.up, onChangeEnd. Listener.up calls _jumpToTimelineMarker, which calls _seekGlobal(marker) (996-1005). The Slider's onChangeEnd then calls _commitScrub(tapValue), which calls _seekGlobal(tapValue) (1439-1448, 853-866). The marker x uses a 14 px inset (1812, 972-982), the Slider maps the tap with its own track padding, and the tap tolerance is 18 px (971), so the two targets differ.
- **Failure scenario:** On a 1-hour ride, the rider taps an incident marker at 30:00. The marker seek is issued, then the Slider's seek to the tapped x lands last, so playback resumes at the tapped position, which can be minutes away. The SnackBar still says 'Jumped to incident marker at 0:30:00'. If the marker and the tap fall in different segments, the two seeks start concurrent loads (see the orphaned-controller finding).
- **Evidence:** Re-ran replica C:/Users/Sarthak/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Sarthak-Documents-MotoCam/f2235d59-4dc6-48c0-bff9-49e27bc65ea5/scratchpad/layoutcheck/test/order_test.dart: 'RESULT hold=0 order=Listener.down -> onChangeStart(100.0) -> onChanged(500.0) -> Listener.up -> onChangeEnd(500.0)'. layout_test.dart: 'RESULT tapAtMarker90 pointerUps=1 changeStarts=1 changeEnds=[927.7777777777777] (marker value would be 900.0)'.
- **Recommendation:** Drop the separate Listener seek. In the Slider's onChangeEnd, snap the value to the nearest marker within the tolerance (computed from the same track rect), then show the SnackBar. Alternatively make the marker hit zones opaque so the Slider does not also receive the tap.
- **Fix note:** marker taps snap within onChangeEnd

#### F118

**The ride list shows VERIFIED after a payload-only check, so replaced or missing segment files still show VERIFIED**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:66` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:702-720`, `README.md:114`

- **What is wrong:** The list loads rides with RideRecord.fromTelemetryFile(telemetryFile) and no verifySegments (66), so _verifyIntegrity skips segment hashing (ride_record.dart:702-720). The green VERIFIED badge and the text 'Integrity: Verified' (202-207, 232) therefore say nothing about the video files. 'Segments: n' (230) counts declared files that may no longer exist. README.md:114 says per-segment hashes are verified and shown as VERIFIED.
- **Failure scenario:** After one of two segment files is deleted or replaced, the card still shows VERIFIED and 'Segments: 2'. Only opening playback reveals the integrity issue.
- **Evidence:** Re-ran .../scratchpad/exp/motocam/test/exp_history_test.dart: with a tampered segment, 'B lazy status=RideIntegrityStatus.verified'; with one segment deleted, 'H segs=2 lazy=RideIntegrityStatus.verified full=RideIntegrityStatus.modified [missing-segment-file]'.
- **Verifier corrections:** README.md:114 qualifies the claim with 'On playback', which is accurate for the playback screen. The mismatch is the list badge using the same 'VERIFIED' wording after a payload-only check, not an outright README contradiction.
- **Recommendation:** Show a distinct label on the list for a payload-only check (for example 'METADATA OK'), or run verifySegmentIntegrity in the background per card and update the badge, and count only existing segments in 'Segments: n'.
- **Fix note:** "DATA UNMODIFIED" wording; segment counts honest

#### F119

**The ride card title is the raw CameraX temp file name, and fixed-width badges squeeze it to an unreadable column or make the row overflow**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:176` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:174-217`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:241-279`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:90-93`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:755-758`

- **What is wrong:** The card title is `ride.fileName`, the segment file name carried over from CameraX's temp file: 'REC', a random long number, then '.mp4' (camera_android_camerax 0.7.1 android_camera_camerax.dart:131 `videoPrefix = 'REC'`, :1186-1189 `getTempFilePath(videoPrefix, '.mp4')`). It means nothing to a rider. The title sits in an Expanded next to up to three fixed-width badges with no wrapping or truncation (174-217), inside a ListTile whose trailing box is 84 px wide when the ride is locked (242). Measured with Roboto at 360 dp: for a VERIFIED+LOCKED ride the name gets 72 px and wraps to 4 lines at 1.0x text; at 1.3x it gets 46 px and is 279 px tall; at 2.0x the row overflows by 15 px. At 320 dp and 1.0x it gets 32 px and wraps to 9 lines. INTEGRITY WARNING+QUARANTINED 1+LOCKED overflows by 101 px at 360 dp and by 141 px at 320 dp, leaving the name 0 px wide and 624 px tall. INTEGRITY WARNING+QUARANTINED 1 overflows by 29 px at 320 dp and by 47 px at 1.3x on 360 dp.
- **Failure scenario:** In Ride History, every locked incident ride shows 'REC8094724897381057316.mp4' as a narrow column of digits, and at larger text sizes the cards grow hundreds of pixels tall. Tampered or quarantined rides overflow, so their badges are clipped (and show yellow and black stripes in debug builds).
- **Evidence:** Scratch replica of rides_list_screen.dart:161-297 (Flutter 3.41.2, app theme, Roboto): `w=360.0 scale=1.0 VERIFIED+LOCKED: overflow=false rowW=204 badgesW(no gaps)=126 fileNameBox=72.1x96`; `w=360.0 scale=1.3 VERIFIED+LOCKED: ... fileNameBox=46.0x279`; `w=320.0 scale=1.0 VERIFIED+LOCKED: ... fileNameBox=32.1x216`; `w=360.0 scale=1.0 INTEGRITY WARNING+QUARANTINED 1+LOCKED: overflow=true rowW=204 badgesW(no gaps)=299 fileNameBox=0.0x624 ... A RenderFlex overflowed by 101 pixels on the right.`; `w=360.0 scale=2.0 VERIFIED+LOCKED: overflow=true ... overflowed by 15 pixels on the right.` Code: :176 `Expanded(child: Text(ride.fileName)),`; ride_record.dart:90-93 `String get fileName { final parts = videoPath.split(Platform.pathSeparator); return parts.isEmpty ? videoPath : parts.last; }`.
- **Recommendation:** Use the ride start date/time as the title with maxLines: 1 and TextOverflow.ellipsis, move the badges into a Wrap in the subtitle, and show the file name only as secondary text.
- **Fix note:** ride card title/badges wrap

#### F120

**Unlock runs instantly with no confirmation or progress, always reports success, and promises a 'loop policy' that does not apply to past rides**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:269` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:625-666`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1180-1256`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:112-139`

- **What is wrong:** Tapping the unlock icon calls `ride.setLockState(false)` immediately, with no confirmation and no undo (258-273). `setLockState` re-reads the ride and re-hashes it before rewriting the JSON (ride_record.dart:641-662), and no progress is shown meanwhile. It returns void, and on a missing file, non-map JSON, or a hashing or write error it either returns early or swallows the exception (ride_record.dart:637-646, 663-665). The list nonetheless always shows 'Clip unlocked and moved to normal loop policy'. No such policy applies to past rides. Per-session rolling only touches the in-memory session list (camera_provider.dart:1117-1138), and the directory sweep deletes the oldest files without reading any persisted lock (camera_provider.dart:1200-1234). So the LOCKED badge and the 'Locked Clips' section do not actually protect a ride, and unlocking does not expose it to deletion.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider taps the unlock icon by accident while scrolling past an incident clip, and it is unlocked at once with no undo. Alternatively, the write fails: the SnackBar still says 'Clip unlocked…', and after the reload the card still shows LOCKED.
- **Evidence:** rides_list_screen.dart:260-271 `: () async { final messenger = ScaffoldMessenger.of(context); await ride.setLockState(false); if (!mounted) return; setState(() { _ridesFuture = _loadRides(); }); messenger.showSnackBar(const SnackBar(content: Text('Clip unlocked and moved to normal loop policy'),`; ride_record.dart:625 `Future<void> setLockState(bool locked) async {`, :663-665 `} catch (_) { // Ignore lock-state updates for malformed telemetry files. }`; camera_provider.dart:1220-1227 `final removeCount = videoFiles.length - _maxRollingSegments; ... for (final file in videoFiles.take(removeCount)) { ... await file.delete();` (no lock check).
- **Recommendation:** Have setLockState return a bool (or throw), show a confirmation dialog and a busy state, and choose the SnackBar text from the result. Separately, either make _enforceRecordingRetention skip segments listed in any persisted lockedSegmentPaths, or reword the LOCKED badge and message so they do not promise retention protection.
- **Owner decision:** Whether persisted locks should actually protect past rides from the retention sweep, or the UI should stop promising it, is a product decision about retention semantics.
- **Fix note:** unlock confirmed and accurately reported; locks protect past rides

#### F121

**No tileProvider is passed, so every TileLayer rebuild creates a new NetworkTileProvider and HTTP client that is never closed**

Severity: **low** · Category: lifecycle · Location: `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:64` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1233`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1367-1383`

- **What is wrong:** No tileProvider is passed, so every TileLayer construction runs 'tileProvider ?? NetworkTileProvider()' (flutter_map-8.2.2 tile_layer.dart:278), and every provider creates RetryClient(Client()) (network/tile_provider.dart:41-42). TileLayer's State disposes only the provider that is current when it is disposed (tile_layer.dart:536); didUpdateWidget (465-530) never disposes the old one. RouteMapView is rebuilt on every VideoPlayerValue update: the ValueListenableBuilder is at ride_playback_screen.dart:1233, and the position timer fires every 100 ms (video_player.dart:739). This happens even while the map is collapsed, because AnimatedCrossFade keeps firstChild built (ride_playback_screen.dart:1367-1383).
- **Failure scenario:** During 1 hour of playback, about 36,000 providers and HTTP clients are created and only the last one is closed. Tiles fetched after a rebuild use a new client, so keep-alive connections to tile.openstreetmap.org are not reused, and idle sockets stay open until the dart:io idle timeout.
- **Evidence:** route_map_view.dart:64-69 builds TileLayer(urlTemplate: ..., userAgentPackageName: ..., maxNativeZoom: ..., maxZoom: ...) with no tileProvider. flutter_map tile_layer.dart:278 'tileProvider = tileProvider ?? NetworkTileProvider(),'; :536 'widget.tileProvider.dispose();' only in State.dispose; network/tile_provider.dart:42 '_httpClient = httpClient ?? RetryClient(Client());', closed only in dispose (:129-131).
- **Verifier corrections:** The impact is allocation and GC churn plus lost connection reuse (about 36k short-lived provider/client objects per hour of playback). It is not a lasting leak: unclosed clients without sockets are collected, and used sockets close at the idle timeout.
- **Recommendation:** Hold one NetworkTileProvider per map, for example by making RouteMapView a StatefulWidget that creates the provider in initState and disposes it in dispose, or by using a static final, and pass it as tileProvider:. Also build RouteMapView outside the per-frame ValueListenableBuilder, or only while the panel is expanded.
- **Fix note:** one tile provider per map, disposed

#### F122

**TileLayer has no error image or callback and the map background stays light grey, so failed tile loads show a blank grey box with no message**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:64` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:52-62`

- **What is wrong:** TileLayer sets no errorImage, errorTileCallback or fallbackUrl (64-69), and MapOptions leaves backgroundColor at flutter_map's default of 0xFFE0E0E0 (flutter_map-8.2.2 options.dart:153). When tiles cannot load, for example offline, the dark playback panel shows a light-grey rectangle with only the route line and markers on it, and nothing says that the map failed to load.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider reviews a ride in airplane mode or an area with no signal and sees a bright grey box behind the blue route line, with no hint that the map is unavailable offline.
- **Evidence:** route_map_view.dart:64-69 `TileLayer(urlTemplate: MapSourceConfig.tileUrlTemplate, userAgentPackageName: MapSourceConfig.userAgentPackageName, maxNativeZoom: MapSourceConfig.maxNativeZoom, maxZoom: MapSourceConfig.maxZoom,),`; :52-62 MapOptions with no backgroundColor; flutter_map-8.2.2/lib/src/map/options/options.dart:153 `this.backgroundColor = const Color(0xFFE0E0E0),`.
- **Verifier corrections:** Only uncached tiles go blank. flutter_map's built-in tile cache, on by default on native, shows tiles viewed earlier while offline.
- **Recommendation:** Set MapOptions(backgroundColor: Colors.grey[900]!) so the map matches the panel. Add an errorTileCallback that sets a flag and shows a small 'Map tiles unavailable offline' overlay, or supply an errorImage.
- **Fix note:** dark map background + offline badge

#### F123

**TileLayer.maxZoom 19 with unbounded MapOptions zoom blanks every tile at zoom 19.5 and above**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:68` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/map/map_source_config.dart:13-14`, `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:52-62`

- **What is wrong:** TileLayer gets maxZoom: MapSourceConfig.maxZoom, which is 19 (68; map_source_config.dart:13). MapOptions sets no maxZoom (52-62), so the camera zoom is unbounded (flutter_map-8.2.2 camera.dart:391-394). TileLayer.build returns SizedBox.shrink() when zoom.round() > maxZoom (tile_layer.dart:544, 791-792). flutter_map documents that TileLayer.maxZoom 'should usually be infinite', and that maxNativeZoom, already 19 here, sets the tile source's limit (tile_layer.dart:87-96).
- **Failure scenario:** A short route opens at zoom 16. Four double-taps, each adding 1 (map_interactive_viewer.dart:864), or a pinch to inspect an incident spot, take the zoom to 19.5 or more. All tiles vanish, leaving only the grey background, the route line and the markers.
- **Evidence:** route_map_view.dart:67-68: 'maxNativeZoom: MapSourceConfig.maxNativeZoom, maxZoom: MapSourceConfig.maxZoom,'. flutter_map tile_layer.dart:544: 'if (_outsideZoomLimits(map.zoom.round())) return const SizedBox.shrink();'; :792 'zoom < widget.minZoom || zoom > widget.maxZoom;'.
- **Recommendation:** Remove maxZoom from the TileLayer and keep maxNativeZoom: 19 so tiles are upscaled past 19. Alternatively, also set MapOptions(maxZoom: 19) or a small cap above it such as 20 to bound the camera.
- **Fix note:** zoom capped via MapOptions; tiles upscale

#### F124

**The OpenStreetMap attribution is plain non-tappable text and the app has no licence screen**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:125` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/map/map_source_config.dart:11`

- **What is wrong:** The map attribution is a static Text, '© OpenStreetMap contributors' (route_map_view.dart:125-138; map_source_config.dart:11), with no tap target. The app has no About or licences screen (lib/ has no LicenseRegistry, showAboutDialog or showLicensePage) and no url_launcher dependency. The OSMF Attribution Guidelines ask for a way to reach licence and origin information when the attribution text itself does not contain it. flutter_map ships RichAttributionWidget for exactly this purpose.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider, or an OSMF reviewer, taps the attribution and nothing happens; nowhere in the app leads to openstreetmap.org/copyright or the ODbL.
- **Evidence:** route_map_view.dart:134-137 `child: const Text(MapSourceConfig.attribution, style: TextStyle(color: Colors.white70, fontSize: 10),),`; map_source_config.dart:11 `static const String attribution = '© OpenStreetMap contributors';`; grep -i `LicenseRegistry|showAboutDialog|showLicensePage|AboutListTile|copyright|odbl|url_launcher` in lib/ and pubspec.yaml -> no matches; flutter_map-8.2.2/lib/src/layer/attribution_layer/rich/widget.dart defines RichAttributionWidget.
- **Recommendation:** Replace the overlay with flutter_map's RichAttributionWidget, using a TextSourceAttribution('OpenStreetMap contributors') whose onTap opens https://www.openstreetmap.org/copyright (this adds url_launcher). Alternatively, add an About/licences entry that shows that URL and calls showLicensePage.
- **Fix note:** `OsmAttribution` (flutter_map `RichAttributionWidget`, tap opens the copyright page with url_launcher, Android https `<queries>` entry) on the route map, and an About and licences entry in the recording settings sheet

#### F125

**The route map's initial zoom ignores its 90-130 px height and the Mercator latitude stretch, so north-south routes open clipped**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:191` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1241-1243`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1367-1383`

- **What is wrong:** `_initialZoomForBounds` maps the larger degree span to zoom 16, 14, 12, 10, 8 or 6 without considering the widget's size or the latitude (191-202). The playback screen makes the map only clamp(0.14 × screen height, 90, 130) px tall (ride_playback_screen.dart:1241-1243), and Web Mercator stretches north-south spans by sec(latitude). Near the top of each zoom bracket, north-south routes therefore do not fit vertically. For example, a 0.045° (about 5 km) north-south ride at 28.6°N opens at zoom 12, where 256·2^12/360 × sec 28.6° ≈ 3,318 px per degree. The route is then about 149 px tall in a map of at most 128 px, and both endpoint markers sit about 10 px outside the frame. flutter_map 8.2.2 provides MapOptions.initialCameraFit with CameraFit.bounds (options.dart:67).
- **Failure scenario:** The rider expands Route Map for a short north-south commute, finds the start and end markers cut off, and has to pinch out to see the whole route.
- **Evidence:** route_map_view.dart:192-200 `final span = latSpan > lonSpan ? latSpan : lonSpan; if (span <= 0.002) return 16; if (span <= 0.01) return 14; if (span <= 0.05) return 12; if (span <= 0.2) return 10; if (span <= 1.0) return 8;`; :54 `initialZoom: _initialZoomForBounds(bounds),`; ride_playback_screen.dart:1242-1243 `final mapHeight = (viewportHeight * 0.14).clamp(90.0, 130.0).toDouble();`; flutter_map-8.2.2/lib/src/map/options/options.dart:67 `final CameraFit? initialCameraFit;`, camera_fit.dart:22 `const factory CameraFit.bounds({`.
- **Recommendation:** Use MapOptions(initialCameraFit: CameraFit.bounds(bounds: bounds, padding: const EdgeInsets.all(16))) in place of initialCenter/initialZoom, so the fit uses the real widget size and the Mercator projection.
- **Fix note:** CameraFit.bounds initial fit

#### F126

**The OSM tile User-Agent uses the template placeholder 'com.example.motocam' with no contact, and the tile URL is a constant with no fallback**

Severity: **low** · Category: dependencies · Location: `lib/features/map/map_source_config.dart:12` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/map/map_source_config.dart:9`, `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:64`, `android/app/build.gradle.kts:75`

- **What is wrong:** All route-map tiles come from tile.openstreetmap.org (map_source_config.dart:9-10, consumed at route_map_view.dart:64-69). flutter_map 8.2.2 builds the User-Agent as `flutter_map ($userAgentPackageName)` (tile_layer.dart:284-287), so every install sends 'flutter_map (com.example.motocam)'. `com.example` is the `flutter create` placeholder namespace, so the string is neither unique to this app nor contactable, and it duplicates the template applicationId by hand (android/app/build.gradle.kts:61,75). The OSM tile policy requires 'a clear, unique User-Agent string that names your app' and warns that traffic with generic or spoofed identities may be blocked without notice. It also recommends 'Avoid hard-coding the tile URL; allow switching without needing a software update'. Here the URL is a static const with no fallbackUrl and no remote config.
- **Failure scenario:** OSMF throttles or blocks this identity, or the applicationId is changed for Play (com.example is not publishable) and this constant is forgotten. Every installed copy's playback route map then shows only the grey placeholder, and restoring maps requires shipping a new release.
- **Evidence:** map_source_config.dart:9-12 `tileUrlTemplate = 'https://tile.openstreetmap.org/{z}/{x}/{y}.png'` ... `userAgentPackageName = 'com.example.motocam'`; [pub cache] flutter_map-8.2.2/lib/src/layer/tile_layer/tile_layer.dart:284-287 `this.tileProvider.headers.putIfAbsent('User-Agent', () => 'flutter_map ($userAgentPackageName)');`. OSM Tile Usage Policy (operations.osmfoundation.org/policies/tiles, fetched 2026-10-20). Section 3.1: 'Send a clear, unique User-Agent string that names your app and optionally includes a contact URL or email.' Section 3.4: 'Apps must configure a distinct, stable User-Agent naming your app ...' and 'Traffic using generic defaults, referer-stripping, or spoofed identities may be blocked without notice.' Quick summary: 'You should (recommended): Avoid hard-coding the tile URL; allow switching without needing a software update.'
- **Verifier corrections:** The policy's 'defaults will be blocked' wording targets library and HTTP-client default UAs. 'flutter_map (com.example.motocam)' is app-specific, though it is a placeholder with no contact, so blocking is a risk rather than a certainty. flutter_map 8.2.2's own OSM block (_blockOpenStreetMapUrl) is hard-coded to false.
- **Recommendation:** Pass a NetworkTileProvider with an explicit header such as 'User-Agent': 'MotoCam/<version> (+https://github.com/Sarthak195/MotoCam)', or derive the UA from the real applicationId once one is chosen. Read the tile URL from configuration, with a fallbackUrl, so the source can change without a release.
- **Owner decision:** A real reverse-DNS application id and a public contact URL or email for the UA are identity choices for the owner. Changing applicationId also affects Play publishing and upgrade paths.
- **Fix note:** User-Agent with repo contact; fallback tile source needs owner choice

#### F127

**_pipBlinkStream's 700 ms Stream.periodic timer is never cancelled once the PiP view has been shown**

Severity: **low** · Category: performance · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:57` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1132`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:305`

- **What is wrong:** The PiP REC dot listens to Stream<int>.periodic(700 ms).asBroadcastStream(). _AsBroadcastStream subscribes to its source on the first listener. Without an onCancel handler, it cancels that subscription only when the source closes, and Stream.periodic never closes. The State has no handle to cancel it, and dispose() does not try. MainActivity enters PiP automatically on onPause or onUserLeaveHint while recording. After the first such PiP view, the 700 ms timer fires for the rest of the process lifetime, including after the screen is disposed and while the app is in the background.
- **Failure scenario:** After one PiP session, the isolate wakes ~1.4 times per second for as long as the process lives, pushing a tick into a broadcast controller with no listeners. The cost per wake-up is tiny, but the timer leak is unbounded and keeps the main thread waking periodically even when nothing else runs.
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:57-60 'final Stream<int> _pipBlinkStream = Stream<int>.periodic( const Duration(milliseconds: 700), (tick) => tick, ).asBroadcastStream();'; :1132-1133 'StreamBuilder<int>( stream: _pipBlinkStream,'; dispose at :1864-1876 does not touch it. sky_engine/lib/async/stream_impl.dart:872-886 '_onCancel() { ... bool shutdown = (controller == null) || controller.isClosed; ... if (shutdown) { ... subscription.cancel();'. MainActivity.kt:300-308 onUserLeaveHint and onPause -> maybeEnterRecordingPip().
- **Verifier corrections:** The failure scenario overstates the impact. This is one leaked 700 ms timer for the lifetime of the State (in practice the whole process, because RecordingScreen is the root screen), not unbounded growth. The finding's suggested minimum fix, `onCancel: (s) => s.cancel()`, is unsafe. Stream.periodic is single-subscription (_SyncStreamController), so once the source subscription is cancelled, the next PiP entry re-listens and throws 'Stream has already been listened to'.
- **Recommendation:** Either use `.asBroadcastStream(onListen: (s) => s.resume(), onCancel: (s) => s.pause())` (a paused Stream.periodic cancels its timer), or replace the stream with a State-owned Timer.periodic/AnimationController that starts with the PiP view and is cancelled in dispose().
- **Fix note:** blinking dot owns and cancels its timer

#### F128

**Icon-only controls have no tooltips or semantics labels, custom gesture areas have no Semantics, and the Route Map toggle is an 18 dp tap target**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1237` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1613-1649`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1337-1366`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1100-1114`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1145-1189`
