- **What is wrong:** The settings gear (recording_screen.dart:1237-1240) and the playback transport IconButtons (back 10 s, play/pause and forward 10 s, ride_playback_screen.dart:1613-1649) have no tooltip, so TalkBack and VoiceOver announce them as unlabelled buttons. lib/ contains no Semantics widget at all, so the blackout overlay (recording_screen.dart:1104-1112) and the speed-graph scrubber (ride_playback_screen.dart:1145-1189) expose no label or hint. The Route Map expand/collapse header is an InkWell around a bare Row: a 16 px icon, 12 px text and an 18 px chevron (1337-1366). It measures 18 dp tall (22 dp at 1.3x), far below the 48 dp minimum, and does not announce whether it is expanded.
- **Failure scenario:** A screen-reader user hears 'Button, button, button' for the playback transport row and just 'Button' for the gear on the main screen. A rider wearing gloves misses the 18 dp Route Map header.
- **Evidence:** grep `tooltip:` in lib -> only rides_list_screen.dart:249, ride_playback_screen.dart:1287, recording_screen.dart:1226; grep `Semantics|semanticLabel|semanticsLabel` in lib -> no matches; recording_screen.dart:1237-1240 `IconButton(icon: const Icon(Icons.settings, color: Colors.white), onPressed: _isApplyingSettings ? null : _openSettingsSheet,)`; ride_playback_screen.dart:1613-1622 `IconButton(onPressed: () async {...}, icon: const Icon(Icons.replay_10),)`; scratch replica of :1337-1366 -> `textScale=1.0 Route Map header InkWell size=756x18.0`, `textScale=1.3 Route Map header InkWell size=756x22.0`.
- **Verifier corrections:** The blackout overlay does expose a semantic tap action, synthesized by GestureDetector's default semantics delegate. It lacks a label/hint, and a synthesized tap cannot perform the long-hold exit. The 18/22 dp heights come from the finding's own replica measurement and were not re-run here, under the resource rule.
- **Recommendation:** Add tooltips ('Settings', 'Back 10 seconds', 'Play'/'Pause', 'Forward 10 seconds'). Wrap the blackout overlay and speed graph in Semantics with label/hint. Give the Route Map header ConstrainedBox(minHeight: 48) plus Semantics(button: true, expanded: _showRouteMap), or use ListTile/ExpansionTile.
- **Fix note:** Tooltips on the settings, blackout, fullscreen and transport buttons; Semantics on the blackout overlay and the speed graph; the Route Map header has a 48 dp minimum height and Semantics(expanded). (The fix summary listed the header as open; the code has it.)

#### F129

**Telemetry strip values (Speed, Duration, Distance) are ellipsized at larger system font sizes**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1663` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1551-1639`

- **What is wrong:** The strip packs five equal Expanded columns, each holding a 12 dp bold value with maxLines: 1 and an ellipsis (1563-1638, 1641-1679), into the screen width minus 32 dp. lib/ has no textScaler override, so these explicit font sizes still scale with the system setting, and at larger settings the values no longer fit. Measured with Roboto and linear scaling: at 360 dp, Distance ('12.34 km') is cut off from 1.3x, and Speed ('88 km/h') and Duration ('01:23:45') from 1.5x. At 393 dp, Duration and Distance are cut off from 1.5x. At 411 dp, Distance is cut off from 1.5x, and Speed and Duration at 2.0x. Nothing is cut off at 1.0x.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider who uses Android's larger font sizes sees the recording timer as '01:23:…' and the distance as '12.34 k…' on the main recording screen.
- **Evidence:** Scratch replica of recording_screen.dart:1551-1679 (Roboto loaded): `w=360.0 scale=1.3 truncated values: Distance`, `w=360.0 scale=1.5 truncated values: Speed, Duration, Distance`, `w=393.0 scale=1.5 truncated values: Duration, Distance`, `w=411.0 scale=2.0 truncated values: Speed, Duration, Distance`, `w=360.0 scale=1.0 truncated values: none`. Code :1663-1668 `Text(value, maxLines: 1, overflow: TextOverflow.ellipsis, textAlign: TextAlign.center, style: valueStyle,)` with `fontSize: compact ? 12 : 18` (1650).
- **Recommendation:** Wrap the value Text in FittedBox(fit: BoxFit.scaleDown), or switch to a two-row layout or drop Temp/Battery when MediaQuery.textScalerOf(context).scale(12) exceeds a threshold.
- **Fix note:** telemetry values scale down instead of ellipsising

#### F130

**Telemetry notifications (~7-8 per second) rebuild the record controls and the PiP view, neither of which displays telemetry**

Severity: **low** · Category: performance · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1687` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1123`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:727`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:192`

- **What is wrong:** _buildControls wraps the START/STOP button and the resolution warnings in Consumer3<CameraProvider, TelemetryProvider, AppSettingsProvider>, but only forwards telemetry to the button callbacks. The PiP view's Consumer2<CameraProvider, TelemetryProvider> never reads telemetry at all. TelemetryProvider notifies on every accelerometer event (5 Hz), every GPS fix (requested every 500 ms) and every sample (1 Hz), throttled to one notification per 100 ms. Both subtrees therefore rebuild ~7-8 times per second. Each rebuild also copies knownUnsupportedResolutionPresets up to five times, since the getter returns a new Set.unmodifiable on every call. Because telemetry never stops (see the sensor finding), the rebuilds continue while RecordingScreen sits offstage under Ride History and playback. The speed and distance tiles already use Selector correctly. The one whole-screen rebuild per segment roll (camera_provider.dart:192) is negligible.
- **Failure scenario:** A 3-hour ride with the screen on causes ~80,000 rebuilds of the button subtree with no visible change, and reviewing rides keeps the offstage recording screen rebuilding at the same rate. Each rebuild is small, but together they are steady frame work and wake-ups on the shared UI/platform thread for nothing.
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:1687-1688 'Consumer3<CameraProvider, TelemetryProvider, AppSettingsProvider>( builder: (context, camera, telemetry, settings, _) {'; telemetry is used only at :1722-1732 (passed to _stopRecordingFlow and _startRecordingFlow); :1123-1124 'Consumer2<CameraProvider, TelemetryProvider>( builder: (context, camera, telemetry, _) {' with no telemetry read; :1696 and :1704 camera.knownUnsupportedResolutionPresets (camera_provider.dart:160-167 returns 'Set.unmodifiable('). telemetry_provider.dart:43 100 ms throttle, :264 and :296 _notifyUiIfNeeded(), :359 _forceNotifyUi().
- **Verifier corrections:** 'Throttled to one notification per 100 ms' is loose. The 1 Hz sample path calls _forceNotifyUi, which bypasses the throttle, and the throttle interval comes from the user's speedRefreshMs setting (default 100 ms, adjustable to 1000 ms). The ~7-8/s rate holds only at the default setting.
- **Recommendation:** Change _buildControls to Consumer2<CameraProvider, AppSettingsProvider> and call context.read<TelemetryProvider>() inside the button callbacks. Change the PiP view to Consumer<CameraProvider>. Optionally read knownUnsupportedResolutionPresets once into a local per build.
- **Fix note:** narrower Consumer/Selector rebuilds

#### F131

**While recording, the HISTORY button next to LOCK minimises the app into PiP without changing its label**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1799` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:988-995`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1116-1203`, `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/example/motocam/MainActivity.kt:129-139`

- **What is wrong:** While recording, HISTORY keeps its label and icon but calls `_handleBackPressWhileRecording` (1799-1802). On Android 8.0 and later that calls `PipService.enterPipMode()`, which shrinks the activity into a PiP window with no controls, showing only REC, the timer and 'Return to app for controls' (1116-1203). On iOS and older Android it instead shows 'Recording active. Stop first to exit.' (988-995). The button sits 10 dp from LOCK in the same row (1793-1856).
- **Failure scenario:** Mid-ride, the rider reaches for LOCK with gloves on and hits HISTORY instead. The app collapses into a small PiP window, and LOCK and STOP are out of reach until the rider expands it again.
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:1797-1805 `onPressed: _isApplyingSettings ? null : (camera.isRecording ? () { unawaited(_handleBackPressWhileRecording()); } : () async { await _openRideHistory(camera: camera); }),`; :1808-1809 `label: const Text('HISTORY',`; :988-995 `final enteredPip = await PipService.enterPipMode(); if (!mounted || enteredPip) { return; } _showInAppNotice('Recording active. Stop first to exit.');`; MainActivity.kt:130-135 `enterPictureInPictureMode(params) result.success(true)`.
- **Recommendation:** When camera.isRecording, either disable HISTORY or relabel it 'MINIMISE' with a picture_in_picture icon. Consider giving LOCK a larger target or more spacing from navigation actions.
- **Owner decision:** Using HISTORY as a minimise-to-PiP action while recording may be deliberate. The owner must choose between disabling the button, relabelling it, or keeping the current behaviour.
- **Fix note:** button reads MINIMISE while recording

#### F132

**LOCK feedback is a 2 s notice counting only completed locked segments, so it shows '(0)' during the first segment and running totals afterwards**

Severity: **low** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1832` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **partially fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1300-1322`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1111-1115`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:432-452`

- **What is wrong:** The only feedback from LOCK is the default 2 s, 12 pt, single-line notice 'Emergency lock enabled (${camera.lockedSegmentCount})' (1831-1833, 432-452). `lockedSegmentCount` counts locked segments that have already been completed (camera_provider.dart:131). The lock on the current segment is only pending (camera_provider.dart:1315-1317) and is applied when that segment is registered at the next roll (1111-1115). So any press during the first segment, which lasts 5 minutes by default, shows '(0)', and later presses show running totals rather than what that press protected. There is no persistent LOCKED indicator and no haptic feedback, and repeated presses silently queue locks on future segments.
- **Failure scenario:** Two minutes into a ride, the rider witnesses a crash and taps LOCK. The screen briefly shows 'Emergency lock enabled (0)', which reads as 'nothing was locked', so they press again, which also locks the next segment.
- **Evidence:** recording_screen.dart:1826-1833 `camera.markIncident(protectPastSegments: 2, protectCurrentSegment: true, reason: 'manual-emergency',); _showInAppNotice('Emergency lock enabled (${camera.lockedSegmentCount})',);`; camera_provider.dart:131 `int get lockedSegmentCount => _lockedSegmentPaths.length;`; :1315-1317 `if (protectCurrentSegment && isRecording) { _pendingIncidentSegmentLocks++; }`; app_settings_provider.dart:100 `int _segmentDurationSeconds = 300;`; recording_screen.dart:434 default `Duration duration = const Duration(seconds: 2)`.
- **Recommendation:** Change the notice to describe what was protected (e.g. 'Locked: current clip + N previous'), counting pending locks. Show a persistent LOCKED badge while _pendingIncidentSegmentLocks > 0, and add HapticFeedback.heavyImpact(). Optionally cap pending locks so repeated presses do not queue more than one.
- **Fix note:** lock feedback names what was locked + LOCKED badge; badge not tied to the pending segment

#### F133

**The settings sheet FPS dropdown initializes to the saved FPS without checking it against the (possibly shrunken) supported-FPS list**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/recording/widgets/recording_settings_sheet.dart:70` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1263-1268`

- **What is wrong:** initState sets selectedFps = settings.recordingFps (70) and availableFps = widget.initialFpsOptions (81). That list is the per-preset cache, which init failures shrink (camera_provider.dart:582-590). The FPS DropdownButtonFormField uses both values directly (387-391). They are only reconciled when the rider changes resolution or profile (212-217, 276-280, 365-367). After an init that failed completely, _syncSettingsWithCameraCapabilities is skipped (recording_screen.dart:974-976), so the saved FPS can be missing from the list.
- **Failure scenario:** 60 fps is saved, and a failed init removed 60 from the cache. Opening Settings trips DropdownButton's 'exactly one item' assertion in debug builds and shows an empty FPS field in release builds. SAVE then submits 60 again.
- **Evidence:** recording_settings_sheet.dart:70 `selectedFps = settings.recordingFps;`, 81 `availableFps = List<int>.from(widget.initialFpsOptions);`, 387-391 `key: ValueKey(selectedFps), initialValue: selectedFps, ... items: availableFps`.
- **Verifier corrections:** The reachable path is a total init failure, where every preset's cache loses the saved FPS. A partial failure that succeeds at a fallback FPS calls _syncSettingsWithCameraCapabilities, which rewrites settings.recordingFps. The list is never empty, because _markFpsUnsupportedForPreset re-adds 30.
- **Recommendation:** In initState, after building availableFps, add `if (!availableFps.contains(selectedFps)) selectedFps = availableFps.contains(30) ? 30 : availableFps.last;`. This mirrors the existing handlers at :365-367.
- **Fix note:** resolveInitialFps keeps dropdown valid

#### F134

**TelemetryData dartdoc overstates fromJson's fallback (non-numeric values throw) and the bearing range (iOS reports -1 when invalid)**

Severity: **low** · Category: docs-drift · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/models/telemetry_data.dart:94` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/models/telemetry_data.dart:17`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:564`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:160`

- **What is wrong:** fromJson fallback: - The dartdoc (:94-97) says it 'Falls back to sensible defaults ... when fields are missing or malformed'. - Each field is read with `as num?` (:100-106), which throws a TypeError when a value is present but not numeric, for example a string. Only missing or null fields fall back. - RideRecord._readSamples catches that error and drops the whole sample (ride_record.dart:564-570). Bearing range: - bearing is documented as 'degrees (0–360)' (:17). - It is copied from Position.heading (telemetry_provider.dart:258). On iOS that is CLLocation.course passed through unfiltered, so it is −1 when invalid (geolocator_apple LocationMapper.m:54,60). - RideRecord interpolates it linearly (ride_record.dart:160), so values between 359° and 1° interpolate to about 180°.
- **Failure scenario:** A telemetry file whose samples contain "speed": "12.5" (a string, for example from an external tool or a future schema) loses those samples entirely instead of defaulting the bad field. Playback then shows gaps in the speed graph and the route. Consumers of exported telemetry who trust the documented 0–360 range mishandle −1 bearings from iOS and wrap-around interpolation.
- **Evidence:** fromJson: - telemetry_data.dart:96-97: 'Falls back to sensible defaults (`0.0`, `DateTime.now()`) when fields are missing or malformed.' - :100: "latitude: (json['latitude'] as num?)?.toDouble() ?? 0.0,". - ride_record.dart:564-570: 'try { ... samples.add(TelemetryData.fromJson(normalized)); } catch (_) { // Keep loading the ride even if a single sample is malformed. }'. Bearing: - telemetry_data.dart:17: '/// GPS bearing / heading in degrees (0–360).' - telemetry_provider.dart:258: 'bearing: position.heading,'. - <pubcache>/geolocator_apple-2.3.13/darwin/geolocator_apple/Sources/geolocator_apple/Utils/LocationMapper.m:54: '// A negative course value indicates that the course information is invalid.' - LocationMapper.m:60: 'double heading = location.course;'. - ride_record.dart:160: 'bearing: _lerp(lower.bearing, upper.bearing, t),'.
- **Verifier corrections:** Bearing has no visible consumer in lib besides interpolation, so the wrap-around and -1 issues affect only exported/consumer data, not the current UI.
- **Recommendation:** Narrow the fromJson doc to 'missing or null', or parse each field with a tolerant helper (value is num ? value.toDouble() : double.tryParse('$value') ?? 0.0). Document bearing as '0–360, negative when unavailable (iOS)' and clamp or normalise it at capture.
- **Fix note:** lenient fromJson; shortest-arc bearing interpolation

#### F135

**The position stream has no onError/onDone and is never re-subscribed, so it stays dead after location is switched off and on**

Severity: **low** · Category: lifecycle · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:134` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:84-107`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:142-167`

- **What is wrong:** getPositionStream(...).listen(_updateLocation) has no onError or onDone (132-134). With forceLocationManager, geolocator_android's LocationManagerClient.onProviderDisabled removes updates and reports locationServicesDisabled, while onProviderEnabled does nothing (geolocator_android-5.0.2 LocationManagerClient.java:237-249). The plugin keeps returning the cached stream (geolocator_android.dart:166-212). initialize() will not restart tracking because _isTrackingStarted stays true (85-87, 104).
- **Failure scenario:** The rider briefly turns location off and on mid-ride. The 500 ms stream never resumes. For the rest of the process, telemetry depends on one fallback getCurrentPosition every 1-3 s from a different provider, which gives laggy speed and coarser distance.
- **Evidence:** telemetry_provider.dart:132-134: '_positionSubscription = Geolocator.getPositionStream(locationSettings: locationSettings).listen(_updateLocation);'. LocationManagerClient.java:237 'public void onProviderEnabled(@NonNull String provider) {}'; :241-248 onProviderDisabled -> 'this.locationManager.removeUpdates(this);' ... 'errorCallback.onError(ErrorCodes.locationServicesDisabled);'. The device behaviour is inferred from plugin source.
- **Verifier corrections:** Without onError, the stream error goes to the zone's uncaught-error handler and is logged. It does not crash the app. The finding should also say this.
- **Recommendation:** Add onError to the listen call. Subscribe to Geolocator.getServiceStatusStream(), and when it reports ServiceStatus.enabled (or after a locationServicesDisabled error), cancel _positionSubscription and call _startLocationTracking() again.
- **Fix note:** position stream re-subscribes after location toggles

#### F136

**Mock GPS fixes are accepted with no isMocked check or provenance flag and are later sealed as VERIFIED**

Severity: **low** · Category: security · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:169` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/models/telemetry_data.dart:81`

- **What is wrong:** _updateLocation uses every Position without checking Position.isMocked, which geolocator 4.2.6 exposes. Neither TelemetryData nor the telemetry JSON records whether any fix came from a mock provider. A ride fed by a mock-location app is saved, and later shown as VERIFIED, exactly like a real one. That undercuts the evidentiary purpose the integrity UI implies.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider enables a mock-location app in Developer options and records a ride with a fabricated route and speeds. The telemetry is written with a valid envelope, Ride History shows VERIFIED, and nothing indicates the GPS was simulated.
- **Evidence:** telemetry_provider.dart:169-264 reads position latitude, longitude, speed, heading, accuracy and speedAccuracy, but never isMocked. `grep -rn isMocked lib test` finds nothing. The pub-cache geolocator_platform_interface-4.2.6 position.dart:91 defines 'final bool isMocked;'. telemetry_data.dart:81-92 toJson has no mock or provider field. ride_record.dart:728-729 returns verified whenever the hashes match.
- **Recommendation:** In _updateLocation, set a ride-level flag such as _mockLocationDetected |= position.isMocked (or store it per sample), and write it into telemetryPayloadCore so it is covered by payloadHash. Show the flag in Ride History and playback.
- **Fix note:** mock-location flag saved and shown

#### F137

**Telemetry samples carry no fix validity or age, so (0,0) before the first fix and stale last-known values are saved as real data**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:341` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:319-325`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:155-165`, `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:23-29`, `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:157-171`, `lib/features/map/widgets/static_map_viewer.dart:89-145`

- **What is wrong:** _currentData starts as TelemetryData.empty() with latitude and longitude 0.0 (23; telemetry_data.dart:44-55). startRideSession keeps whatever position it already had (319-325). _captureSample copies _currentData every second regardless of fix age (336-345), and only _updateLocation changes speed and position (169-265), so when fixes stop the last values are repeated. Readers cannot tell the difference. sampleForPosition interpolates linearly between a (0,0) sample and the first fix (ride_record.dart:155-158), and the overlay prints those coordinates (ride_playback_screen.dart:1107-1108). RouteMapView rejects only exact (0,0) (route_map_view.dart:164-166). When there are fewer than 2 valid points it passes the unfiltered list to StaticMapViewer (route_map_view.dart:23-29), which prints 'Start: 0.0000, 0.0000' and computes its bounds over all samples (static_map_viewer.dart:97-109, 135-145).
- **Failure scenario:** On a cold GPS start, the first 20 samples are (0,0). In the first seconds of playback, the GPS stat and the map marker show points between (0,0) and the real start, out in the Arabian Sea for a rider in Delhi. In a 2-minute tunnel at 80 km/h, about 120 samples record 80 km/h at the tunnel entrance, skewing the average and the overlay. A ride with no fix shows a 'route' at 0.0000, 0.0000. A stale position from before the ride becomes the route's start point.
- **Evidence:** telemetry_provider.dart:23 'TelemetryData _currentData = TelemetryData.empty();'; :341-345 'final TelemetryData sample = _currentData.copyWith(elapsedMs: ..., distanceKm: _rideDistanceKm, timestamp: DateTime.now());'; ride_record.dart:157-158 'latitude: _lerp(lower.latitude, upper.latitude, t), longitude: _lerp(lower.longitude, upper.longitude, t),'; route_map_view.dart:25-26 'return StaticMapViewer(telemetryData: telemetryData,'.
- **Verifier corrections:** The 'stale position from before the ride' case is mostly harmless, because it is normally the rider's real current location. The main defects are the (0,0) samples before the first fix and the frozen speed during outages.
- **Recommendation:** Record a hasFix flag (or a fix timestamp) in _updateLocation, and in _captureSample write null or omit lat/lon when there has been no fix or the fix is older than a few seconds. Decay or mark speed unknown after N seconds without a fix. In sampleForPosition and the map widgets, skip samples without a fix.
- **Fix note:** hasFix/fixAgeMs; no-fix samples skipped in stats/maps/interpolation

#### F138

**Ride timestamps are saved via toIso8601String on local DateTime values, with no UTC offset**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:429` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/models/telemetry_data.dart:90`, `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:231`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:75`

- **What is wrong:** startedAt and endedAt (429-430), generatedAt (668) and the sample timestamps (telemetry_data.dart:90) use toIso8601String() on local DateTime.now() values, which leaves out the offset. RideRecord parses them back as local time (ride_record.dart:231, 337), and the list sorts and displays rides by createdAt (rides_list_screen.dart:75, 224).
- **Failure scenario:** A ride recorded in IST and viewed after travelling to another time zone, or across a DST change, shows a different clock time and can sort out of order. As dashcam evidence, the absolute time of an incident is ambiguous.
- **Evidence:** telemetry_provider.dart:429-430: ''startedAt': rideStart.toIso8601String(), 'endedAt': rideEnd.toIso8601String(),' (local DateTime values from :302 and :380). ride_record.dart:337: 'createdAt: DateTime.tryParse(json['startedAt']?.toString() ?? '')'.
- **Verifier corrections:** After travelling to another time zone, the list still shows the original recording-local wall-clock time, because a string with no offset parses to the same wall-clock fields. It does not show a different clock time. The real problems are the ambiguous absolute instant (bad for evidence), ordering errors across a DST fall-back or across time zones, and inconsistency with the UTC-based rideSessionId.
- **Recommendation:** Write timestamps with toUtc().toIso8601String() (or include the offset), and call toLocal() only for display. Existing offset-less files still parse as local time, so they stay backward compatible.
- **Fix note:** UTC timestamps written, shown in local time

#### F139

**updateAccelerometerForTesting duplicates the production accelerometer listener, so the incident tests exercise the copy**

Severity: **low** · Category: testing · Location: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:758` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:269`, `test/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider_test.dart:131`

- **What is wrong:** The @visibleForTesting hook (telemetry_provider.dart:757-783) duplicates the body of the userAccelerometerEventStream listener (:269-297) line for line instead of calling shared code. Both compute magnitude/9.81, apply the 0.30 EMA, call copyWith, apply the two-consecutive-hit rule and call _notifyUiIfNeeded. The three incident tests (telemetry_provider_test.dart:131-208) therefore exercise the copy, and the real listener body has 0 hits. By contrast, updateLocationForTesting delegates to _updateLocation (:752-755).
- **Failure scenario:** Someone tunes the live listener: changes smoothingAlpha, switches to accelerometerEventStream or adds a sampling period. In the process they introduce a bug, for example never resetting _consecutiveIncidentHits. Incident auto-lock breaks on devices while all three incident tests stay green, because they run the untouched copy.
- **Evidence:** telemetry_provider.dart:271-278 vs :759-765 (identical smoothing) and :286-294 vs :772-780 (identical hit logic). In the lcov, telemetry_provider.dart lines 267-269, 272, 275, 277-278, 280-282, 286-290, 293 and 296 have 0 hits.
- **Verifier corrections:** The lcov hit counts were not checked independently. The duplication and the tests' sole use of the copy are confirmed from source.
- **Recommendation:** Extract a private _onLinearAcceleration(double x, double y, double z) from the listener body. Have the listener call it with event.x/y/z and the test hook delegate to it.
- **Fix note:** tests drive the production accelerometer path

#### F140

**WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized runs outside runZonedGuarded, causing a 'Zone mismatch' error on every debug launch and letting gesture-handler errors bypass the zone handler**

Severity: **low** · Category: correctness · Location: `lib/main.dart:14` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/main.dart:17-23`, `lib/main.dart:26-41`

- **What is wrong:** WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized() runs in the root zone (14), while runApp runs inside runZonedGuarded (26-33). runApp asserts binding.debugCheckZone (C:/Users/Sarthak/flutter/packages/flutter/lib/src/widgets/binding.dart:1680), which reports 'Zone mismatch.' through FlutterError.reportError (foundation/binding.dart:493-523). It is not fatal only because debugZoneErrorsAreFatal defaults to false (:452). Callbacks that the binding registered in the root zone, such as pointer dispatch, also run outside the guarded zone, so async errors raised from gesture handlers bypass the zone handler. The release notes list the warning as a known limitation (release-notes/v0.1.11.md:86, v0.1.12.md:110).
- **Failure scenario:** Every debug run prints 'Flutter Error: Zone mismatch...' through the custom handler (main.dart:19), which teaches developers to ignore error output. The guarded handler misses most real async errors, such as an exception thrown during the save from the START/STOP button; those go to the default root handler instead.
- **Evidence:** main.dart:14 'WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();'; :26-32 'runZonedGuarded(() async { await SystemChrome.setPreferredOrientations(...); runApp(const MotoCamApp()); },'. widgets/binding.dart:1680 'assert(binding.debugCheckZone(debugEntryPoint));'; foundation/binding.dart:501 'Zone mismatch.'
- **Verifier corrections:** 'Misses most real async errors' is overstated as an impact. The errors are not lost: they go to the root zone / PlatformDispatcher path and the engine still logs them.
- **Recommendation:** Move WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized() and the FlutterError.onError assignment inside the runZonedGuarded body. Better, drop the zone and set PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError for uncaught async errors.
- **Fix note:** runZonedGuarded removed (no zone mismatch)

#### F141

**The portrait lock via setPreferredOrientations is ignored on Android 16 large screens (sw >= 600dp) because targetSdk is 36**

Severity: **low** · Category: android-platform · Location: `lib/main.dart:28` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/app/build.gradle.kts:77`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:37`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:616`

- **What is wrong:** Orientation is restricted only by SystemChrome.setPreferredOrientations([portraitUp]) (main.dart:28-30), which calls Activity.setRequestedOrientation. For apps targeting API 36, Android 16 ignores setRequestedOrientation, screenOrientation and resizeableActivity on displays whose smallest width is 600dp or more. Capture orientation is locked to portraitUp (camera_provider.dart:616), and the UI is designed for portrait only.
- **Failure scenario:** On a tablet or unfolded foldable running Android 16, the recording UI rotates to landscape while capture stays locked to portrait, so the preview and the layout no longer match. This was not verified on a large-screen device.
- **Evidence:** developer.android.com/about/versions/16/behavior-changes-16: 'For apps targeting Android 16 (API level 36), orientation, resizability, and aspect ratio restrictions no longer apply on displays with smallest width >= 600dp'. setRequestedOrientation is listed as ignored. The temporary opt-out PROPERTY_COMPAT_ALLOW_RESTRICTED_RESIZABILITY stops working at API 37. targetSdk = 36 (build.gradle.kts:77).
- **Recommendation:** Short term, add <property android:name="android.window.PROPERTY_COMPAT_ALLOW_RESTRICTED_RESIZABILITY" android:value="true"/> to the MainActivity element. Before targeting API 37, make the recording and playback layouts work in landscape and handle rotation in capture.
- **Fix note:** Android 16 resizability opt-out property

#### F142

**The lockfile is stale (51 upgrades within current constraints), pulls the discontinued 'lists' package, and no update automation exists**

Severity: **low** · Category: dependencies · Location: `pubspec.lock:379` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `pubspec.lock:603`, `pubspec.lock:235`, `pubspec.lock:28`, `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:155`

- **What is wrong:** Baseline `flutter pub outdated` shows 51 dependencies locked below versions the current pubspec already allows, and the repo has no Dependabot or Renovate config (.github contains only workflows/ci.yml). The stale graph keeps flutter_map 8.2.2 -> proj4dart 2.1.0 -> mgrs_dart 2.0.0 -> unicode 0.3.1 -> `lists` 1.0.1 (pubspec.lock:379-386). pub.dev marks `lists` discontinued; its last release was 2021-07-01. flutter_map 8.3.x accepts proj4dart <4.0.0, and proj4dart 3.0.0 -> mgrs_dart 3.0.0 -> unicode 1.1.9 drops it. A plain `flutter pub upgrade` on Flutter 3.41.2 would also bring in fixes relevant to this app. camera 0.12.0+2 stops a disposed CameraController from updating its value after initialize, onCameraError or orientation events; MotoCam disposes and recreates controllers often (camera_provider.dart:362,624,955,1615). geolocator_android 5.2.1 registers the NMEA listener and GNSS status callback only when useMSLAltitude is requested; upstream fixed this because the per-request synchronous Binder registration on the main thread caused ANRs. flutter_map 8.3.0-8.3.2 fixes TileLayer rebuild churn and tile visibility.
- **Failure scenario:** The rider enters a tunnel or parking garage and GPS goes stale. MotoCam's fallback poller (telemetry_provider.dart:137-158: 1 s timer, getCurrentPosition with a 2 s timeLimit) issues a new position request every ~2-3 s for as long as there is no fix. With the locked geolocator_android 5.0.2, each request creates a new client whose NmeaClient.start() makes one synchronous addNmeaListener + registerGnssStatusCallback pair on the main thread (MethodCallHandlerImpl.java:238-241, NmeaClient.java:70-84). Upstream fixed exactly that pattern as an ANR in 5.2.1; whether it causes an ANR depends on the device. Separately, `lists` will never receive fixes or SDK updates, and it stays in the graph until the lock is refreshed.
- **Evidence:** Baseline pub outdated: '51 upgradable dependencies are locked (in pubspec.lock) to older versions'; rows camera *0.12.0 -> 0.12.0+2, flutter_map *8.2.2 -> 8.3.2, geolocator_android *5.0.2 -> 5.2.1, proj4dart *2.1.0 -> 3.0.0, unicode *0.3.1 -> 1.1.9. pub.dev /api/packages/lists/options -> isDiscontinued: true. [pub cache] unicode-0.3.1/pubspec.yaml `lists: ^1.0.0`; pub.dev: flutter_map 8.3.1 depends on `proj4dart: >=2.1.0 <4.0.0`, mgrs_dart 3.0.0 on `unicode: ^1.1.8`, unicode 1.1.9 on archive and simple_sparse_list. Changelogs: camera 0.12.0+2 'Fixes a crash where a `CameraController` could update its value after being disposed'; geolocator_android 5.2.1 'Fixes an ANR caused by `NmeaClient` registering the NMEA listener and the GNSS status callback (synchronous Binder calls) on the main thread for every position request'. [pub cache] geolocator_android-5.0.2 NmeaClient.java:80-81 `locati…
- **Verifier corrections:** I did not re-run `flutter pub outdated` under the resource rule, so the count of 51 upgradable packages is unverified.
- **Recommendation:** Run `flutter pub upgrade` within the existing constraints and commit the refreshed pubspec.lock after analyze, test and a device smoke test. Add .github/dependabot.yml for the `pub` and `github-actions` ecosystems on a weekly schedule.
- **Fix note:** flutter pub upgrade within constraints; dependabot

#### F143

**README advertises unimplemented features: intl multi-language support (no l10n delegates, ARB files or locale-aware formatting), navigation and thermal throttling**

Severity: **low** · Category: docs-drift · Location: `README.md:35` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed (docs)**

Also affects: `README.md:3`, `README.md:156`, `pubspec.yaml:2`, `README.md:35`, `README.md:109`, `README.md:114`, `README.md:179`, `README.md:187` …

- **What is wrong:** Localization: - README.md:35 says 'Localization: Multi-language support with intl package'. - There is no l10n.yaml, no .arb file, no flutter_localizations, and no AppLocalizations, localizationsDelegates or supportedLocales. - intl is imported only by rides_list_screen.dart, for DateFormat, and all UI strings are hard-coded English. Navigation: - README.md:3 says 'A navigation-enabled dashcam app', pubspec.yaml:2 says 'Navigation-enabled dashcam app for riders', and README.md:156 says 'For GPS tracking and navigation'. - There is no navigation feature: no routing, directions or destination search. The only map is the playback route viewer. - 'Navigation' appears in code only as LocationAccuracy.bestForNavigation and ActivityType.automotiveNavigation.

Code: - `grep -rln "AppLocalizations\|supportedLocales\|localizationsDelegates\|flutter_localizations" lib pubspec.yaml` finds nothing, and git ls-files has no .arb or l10n.yaml. - `grep -rn "package:intl" lib` finds only lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:4. - `grep -rni navigat lib` finds only bestForNavigation / automotiveNavigation at telemetry_provider.dart:113, :120, :122, :157 and the history-route flag _isNavigatingHistory.

The real gap is that no UI or logic reads thermalStatus. The 'Navigation' grep should also mention the Navigator.of(...) routing calls, which are screen navigation rather than a navigation feature.
- **Recommendation:** Delete the Localization bullet at README.md:35. Replace 'navigation-enabled' at README.md:3 and :156 and at pubspec.yaml:2 with 'GPS route-logging'. Optionally note that thermalStatus is collected but not yet used.

#### F144

**README misdescribes the release precheck: it reads no signing credentials and is not Android-only (it also checks the iOS Info.plist)**

Severity: **low** · Category: docs-drift · Location: `README.md:179` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed (docs)**

Also affects: `README.md:187`, `scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:62`

- **What is wrong:** README.md:179 says 'The release precheck accepts signing credentials from either android/key.properties or MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_* environment variables', and README.md:187 says 'The release precheck is Android-specific for now.' Neither is true: - scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1 never reads key.properties or any MOTOCAM_* variable. - It regex-checks build.gradle.kts, the manifest, two XML resource files and ios/Runner/Info.plist. For the plist it asserts that NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription and NSBonjourServiceTypes are absent. - It then optionally runs flutter analyze and flutter test. - Signing credentials are read by Gradle (build.gradle.kts:28-41). So a passing precheck says nothing about whether signing material is present or valid.
- **Failure scenario:** A maintainer adds the precheck to a release pipeline expecting it to validate the signing credentials, as README.md:179 implies. It prints 'Release security precheck passed.' on a runner with no keystore at all. The missing credentials only surface when `flutter build apk --release` hits the Gradle gate.
- **Evidence:** - README.md:179: 'The release precheck accepts signing credentials from either `android/key.properties` or `MOTOCAM_KEYSTORE_*` environment variables.' - README.md:187: 'The release precheck is Android-specific for now.' - scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:37-41 resolves only the gradle file, the manifest, ios/Runner/Info.plist and two XML paths. - scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:62-63: 'Assert-NotContains -FilePath $iosPlistFile -Pattern 'NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription' ...'. - `grep -c 'MOTOCAM\|key.properties' scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1` returns 0. - android/app/build.gradle.kts:28-41 contains resolveSigningValue and its four calls.
- **Verifier corrections:** 'Not Android-only' is slightly overstated: apart from the two iOS plist absence checks, every assertion is Android.
- **Recommendation:** Reword README.md:179 so that Gradle (android/app/build.gradle.kts), not the precheck, resolves the signing credentials, with env vars taking precedence. List the precheck's actual assertions, and change :187 to 'mostly Android checks plus two iOS Info.plist checks'.
- **Fix note:** Docs sync: README.md:207 says the precheck reads no signing credentials and also checks the iOS Info.plist and Podfile. This matches release.md:183-214 (18 checks).

#### F146

**The release precheck only regex-matches the raw source manifest, missing merged-manifest permissions, commented-out matches and protectionLevel**

Severity: **low** · Category: build-ci · Location: `scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:15` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:28`, `scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:48`, `scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:53`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:3`

- **What is wrong:** Assert-Contains and Assert-NotContains run regexes over `Get-Content -Raw` text (lines 15-17, 28-30), and only against android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml (lines 48-53). The shipped manifest is the merged one, and it already differs from the source. The HEAD debug build's merger report shows `uses-permission#android.permission.READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE IMPLIED ... reason: io.flutter.plugins.camerax requested WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, with no maxSdk. A match inside an XML comment satisfies Assert-Contains. The INTERNAL_BROADCAST assertion (line 53) matches the name in the <uses-permission> element (manifest :22) as well as in the <permission> element (:3-5), and never checks protectionLevel="signature". The Kotlin broadcasts rely on that protection (MainActivity.kt:31, BackgroundRecordingService.kt:34).
- **Failure scenario:** A dependency update brings MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE in through its library manifest. The precheck still passes because it never reads the merged manifest, and Play review then rejects the build. Or a refactor drops the <permission> element but keeps the <uses-permission>. The INTERNAL_BROADCAST check still passes, while the broadcast permission is now undefined and could be claimed by another app.
- **Evidence:** scripts/release-security-precheck.ps1:15: `$content = Get-Content -Raw -Path $FilePath`. :16: `if ($content -notmatch $Pattern)`. :53 uses the pattern 'com\.example\.motocam\.permission\.INTERNAL_BROADCAST'. The local build output build/app/outputs/logs/manifest-merger-debug-report.txt contains the IMPLIED READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE entry quoted above.
- **Verifier corrections:** The implied READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE also appears in the release merger report, not only the debug one.
- **Recommendation:** Parse the manifest as [xml] and assert that a <permission> element named INTERNAL_BROADCAST has protectionLevel=signature. Add a post-build step that runs the forbidden-permission checks against the merged release manifest, or against `aapt2 dump permissions`.
- **Fix note:** precheck parses the manifest as XML

#### F147

**Existing tests assert little: a tautological widget smoke test, a misnamed telemetry test and a quarantine fixture that is never referenced**

Severity: **low** · Category: testing · Location: `test/widget_test.dart:18` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `test/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider_test.dart:177`, `test/features/history/models/ride_record_test.dart:103`, `test/features/history/models/ride_record_test.dart:196`

- **What is wrong:** widget_test.dart: - It pumps `const MotoCamApp()` and asserts `find.byType(MotoCamApp)` (lines 15-18), which is always true after pumpWidget. Its only real check is that the first build() does not throw. - It never calls main(), so main.dart:13-41 has 0 hits. - It mocks neither SharedPreferences nor any channel, so bootstrap stops at the first platform await: AppSettingsProvider.load gets no further than app_settings_provider.dart:245. Other tests: - telemetry_provider_test.dart:177 is named 'clearHistory resets all variables', but it asserts that incidentCount is not reset (:205-206). - In ride_record_test.dart:103-119, the traversal path '<storage>/../outside/Elsewhere/segment_bad.mp4' resolves to '<tmp>/storage/MotoCam/outside/...'. The file the test actually creates is at '<tmp>/outside/Elsewhere/segment_bad.mp4', so the case of an existing out-of-scope file is never exercised. - ride_record_test also re-implements canonical hashing (:196-235) instead of producing its fixture with the real writer.
- **Failure scenario:** The smoke test catches only exceptions thrown during the first frame. A regression in load(), the settings migration, the START flow or the error handlers still passes CI. A reader who trusts the telemetry test's name assumes clearHistory resets incident state, which it does not.
- **Evidence:** test/widget_test.dart:15-18. Per the lcov, main.dart lines 13-14, 17, 19-20, 26-28, 32, 34 and 36-37 have 0 hits, and app_settings_provider.dart lines 246-275 have 0 hits. telemetry_provider_test.dart:205-206: '// Note: incidentCount is not reset by clearHistory() ...' followed by `expect(provider.incidentCount, 1);`. Baseline log line: 'quarantined out-of-scope segment path ... -> ...\Recordings\..\outside\Elsewhere\segment_bad.mp4'.
- **Verifier corrections:** I did not re-check the lcov per-line hit counts; they come from the finding.
- **Recommendation:** Mock SharedPreferences and the platform channels, and assert real UI such as the START and HISTORY controls in the widget test. Rename the telemetry test to say incidentCount is preserved. Build the traversal path with enough '..' segments to reach '<tmp>/outside'.
- **Fix note:** misleading tests fixed; widget test asserts real state

#### F151

**Ungated debugPrint calls and flutter_map tile-error logs write ride file paths and location-encoding tile URLs to logcat in release builds**

Severity: **low (scanner said info)** · Category: privacy · Location: `lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:533` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/telemetry/providers/telemetry_provider.dart:600`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:186`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:561`

- **What is wrong:** Most app logging is gated: CameraProvider._log checks kDebugMode, and the error handlers in main.dart print only in debug. Several debugPrint calls are not gated, and debugPrint also prints in release builds: quarantined segment paths (ride_record.dart:533), dropped segment paths (telemetry_provider.dart:600), playback probe and load failures with paths (ride_playback_screen.dart:186, 561), and blackout errors (recording_screen.dart:464, 490). More sensitive, flutter_map's TileLayer debugPrints every tile load error before calling any errorTileCallback. Offline errors include the full tile URL (zoom/x/y), which pins the ride area to about 600 m. All of this goes to logcat. Other apps cannot read logcat, but bug reports, adb and log-collecting tools can.
- **Failure scenario:** A rider opens a ride while offline. logcat receives lines like 'ClientException with SocketException: ..., uri=https://tile.openstreetmap.org/16/46894/30384.png', which reveal where the ride happened to anyone who later collects a bug report from the device.
- **Evidence:** ride_record.dart:533-535 `debugPrint('RideRecord: quarantined out-of-scope segment path from $sourceLabel -> $path')`. telemetry_provider.dart:600 `debugPrint('Telemetry: dropping missing segment path $path')`. ride_playback_screen.dart:186-188 and :561-563. In flutter_map-8.2.2, tile_layer.dart:751-752: 'void _onTileLoadError(...) { debugPrint(error.toString()); widget.errorTileCallback?.call(...) }'. The map harness printed "ClientException with SocketException: offline (test), uri=https://tile.openstreetmap.org/16/46892/30386.png".
- **Verifier corrections:** Severity raised from info to low: release builds really do log ride file paths and location-revealing tile URLs, which is a small defect and not only a recommendation.
- **Recommendation:** In main() before runApp, add `if (kReleaseMode) { debugPrint = (String? message, {int? wrapWidth}) {}; }`. This also silences flutter_map's internal tile-error print. Optionally gate the app's own calls with kDebugMode.
- **Fix note:** release-mode debugPrint silenced; debug-gated logs

#### F152

**The fallback StaticMapViewer draws a fixed decorative 'Start' dot and prints raw (0,0) coordinates instead of saying there was no GPS fix**

Severity: **low (scanner said info)** · Category: ux-accessibility · Location: `lib/features/map/widgets/static_map_viewer.dart:49` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:23-30`, `lib/features/history/widgets/route_map_view.dart:120`

- **What is wrong:** When a ride has fewer than two valid route points (route_map_view.dart:23-30), for example because GPS never got a fix, StaticMapViewer is shown instead of the map. It draws a fixed green dot labelled 'Start' in the top-left corner whatever the coordinates (49-74), and prints 'Points: N', 'Start: …' and 'End: …' from the raw samples (88-110), which read 0.0000, 0.0000 before a fix. 'Points' here counts raw samples, whereas in RouteMapView it counts the downsampled plotted points, at most 1,801 (route_map_view.dart:120).
- **Failure scenario:** A ride recorded without a GPS fix shows a green 'Start' marker and 'Start: 0.0000, 0.0000 / End: 0.0000, 0.0000' instead of 'No GPS fix recorded'.
- **Evidence:** static_map_viewer.dart:49-71 `// Start marker Positioned(left: 12, top: 12, child: Column(children: [Container(width: 12, height: 12, decoration: const BoxDecoration(color: Colors.green, shape: BoxShape.circle),), ... const Text('Start',`; :97-99 `'Start: ${telemetryData.first.latitude.toStringAsFixed(4)}, ${telemetryData.first.longitude.toStringAsFixed(4)}'`; route_map_view.dart:24-29 `if (routePoints.length < 2) { return StaticMapViewer(telemetryData: telemetryData, ...`; :120 `'Points: ${routePoints.length}'`.
- **Verifier corrections:** Severity raised from info to low because this is a concrete, minor display defect.
- **Recommendation:** In RouteMapView, when routePoints is empty, show a 'No GPS fix recorded for this ride' placeholder instead of StaticMapViewer, or have StaticMapViewer filter out (0,0) samples, show that message, and drop the decorative 'Start' legend. Label the counts consistently, for example 'GPS samples: N'.
- **Fix note:** shared GPS-fix filter; honest "No GPS fix" state

#### F154

**README lists iOS as supported, but every Android-native feature (background recording, notification Stop, PiP, device status, gallery export) silently degrades on iOS**

Severity: **low (scanner said info)** · Category: ios-platform · Location: `README.md:10` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed (docs)**

Also affects: `lib/core/services/device_status_service.dart:60`, `lib/core/services/pip_service.dart:62`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:1339`, `README.md:107`, `README.md:159`

- **What is wrong:** README.md:5-11 lists iOS as a supported platform, but no iOS native code exists: ios/Runner has only the template AppDelegate and SceneDelegate. State at HEAD, feature by feature: 1. Launch and build: blocked (Info.plist finding). 2. Runtime permissions: compiled out (Podfile macros finding). 3. Screen-off and background recording: - Android: a camera|microphone|location foreground service plus a partial wake lock. - iOS: nothing. start, update and stop return early (background_recording_service.dart:32-34, 53-55, 109-111, 118-120). - iOS forbids background camera use, and the app does not disable the idle timer (Auto-Lock finding). 4. Recording notification with a Stop action: Android only. The notification permission is requested only on Android (app_permission_service.dart:90-96; camera_provider.dart:376-378). 5. Picture-in-picture: - iOS has no handler. enterPipMode's MissingPluginException is caught and returns false (pip_service.dart:62-69). - Back or HISTORY while recording then shows 'Recording active. Stop first to exit.' (recording_screen.dart:988-995). 6. Battery, temperature and thermal: - DeviceStatusSnapshot.empty is returned off Android (device_status_service.dart:6…
- **Failure scenario:** Even after the blocking defects are fixed, an iOS rider still gets: - no screen-off recording; - no PiP; - no battery or temperature readouts; - no recording notification or Stop action; - no export of incident clips. None of this is disclosed in the README or in the in-app preflight, which simply omits the Android rows on iOS.
- **Evidence:** - background_recording_service.dart:53-55: `if (!Platform.isAndroid) { return; }` - device_status_service.dart:60-62: `if (defaultTargetPlatform != TargetPlatform.android) { return DeviceStatusSnapshot.empty; }` - pip_service.dart:62-68: `try { final bool result = await _platform.invokeMethod('enterPipMode'); return result; } catch (_) { return false; }` - camera_provider.dart:1339-1341: `if (!Platform.isAndroid) { return; }` - ios/Runner/AppDelegate.swift:13-15 contains only `GeneratedPluginRegistrant.register(with: engineBridge.pluginRegistry)`. - README.md:10: `- iOS`
- **Verifier corrections:** The README Native Bridges table already labels background_recording as an 'Android foreground service', so the docs do not omit platform scope entirely. The pip, device_status and media rows are unlabelled. Several enumerated sub-items (Info.plist launch blocker, Podfile macros, 480p) depend on other findings and were not re-verified here.
- **Recommendation:** Add an 'iOS limitations' note to README.md and label the pip, device_status and media channels as Android-only. Optionally show an iOS preflight notice that recording requires the app in the foreground with the screen on.
- **Owner decision:** The owner must decide whether to keep iOS as a supported platform with documented limitations, implement iOS counterparts, or drop iOS from the claimed platforms.
- **Fix note:** Docs sync: README.md:25 (iOS lacks background service, notification Stop, PiP, device status and gallery export, and needs foreground with the screen on) and README.md:28-32 (idle timer). Also platform-integration.md:280-312 (iOS current state), features.md:317-323 and release.md:326-336.

### Info (4)

#### F148

**android/build.gradle uses the deprecated Project.buildDir API and silently adds concurrent-futures to every plugin module**

Severity: **info** · Category: build-ci · Location: `android/build.gradle:8` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `android/build.gradle:16`, `android/build.gradle:29`, `android/app/build.gradle.kts:106`

- **What is wrong:** Lines 8, 10 and 29 use rootProject.buildDir and project.buildDir. Gradle 8.14.4 marks both @Deprecated ('Use getLayout().getBuildDirectory() instead'), and the Flutter 3.41.2 template uses layout.buildDirectory. Lines 16-26 add `implementation 'androidx.concurrent:concurrent-futures:1.1.0'` to every subproject that applies com.android.application or com.android.library, which means every Flutter plugin module. The app also declares the same dependency (android/app/build.gradle.kts:106-108). No comment explains why.
- **Failure scenario:** A future Gradle or AGP upgrade removes the deprecated buildDir setter, and the whole Android build fails at configuration time. Separately, if a plugin later needs a newer concurrent-futures, the resulting version conflict is hard to trace, because the injection is invisible from the plugin's own build file.
- **Evidence:** android/build.gradle:8: `rootProject.buildDir = '../build'`. :10: `project.buildDir = "${rootProject.buildDir}/${project.name}"`. :16-26: the withPlugin injection. In the Gradle 8.14.4 wrapper sources, src/core-api/org/gradle/api/Project.java annotates getBuildDir and setBuildDir with `@deprecated Use {@code getLayout().getBuildDirectory()} instead`.
- **Recommendation:** Port to the current Flutter template form (`rootProject.layout.buildDirectory.value(rootProject.layout.buildDirectory.dir("../../build").get())` and the per-subproject equivalent). Either add a comment naming the plugin and error the concurrent-futures injection works around, or remove it after a debug and release build check.
- **Fix note:** layout.buildDirectory

#### F149

**Commit cd39097 claims dartdoc was added to all public services, providers and models, but the core providers and models document only their class headers**

Severity: **info** · Category: docs-drift · Location: `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:33` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed (docs)**

Also affects: `lib/core/providers/app_settings_provider.dart:148`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:199`

- **What is wrong:** Commit cd39097 claims 'Docs: add dartdoc to all public services, providers, and models' and 'Refactor: remove duplicated resolution preset conversion methods'. Dartdoc in the tree: | File | `///` lines | Total lines | |---|---|---| | CameraProvider | 6, all in the class header | 1630 | | TelemetryProvider | 5 | 784 | | RideRecord | 6 | 892 | | AppSettingsProvider | 6 | 476 | | recording_screen.dart | 0 | 1878 | | ride_playback_screen.dart | 0 | 2035 | The four services document their classes and almost no members. The preset-conversion duplicates also remain: AppSettingsProvider.toResolutionPreset/fromResolutionPreset (app_settings_provider.dart:148-176) versus CameraProvider.presetForResolution/resolutionForPreset (camera_provider.dart:199-225). They are identical today.
- **Failure scenario:** Contributors who trust the commit message expect documented public APIs, for example the contracts of CameraProvider start/stop/markIncident and their error behaviour. Instead they must reverse-engineer files of 1,600 lines or more. The two preset mappings can drift apart, because the claimed deduplication never happened.
- **Evidence:** - `git log -1 --format=%B cd39097`: '- Docs: add dartdoc to all public services, providers, and models' and '- Refactor: remove duplicated resolution preset conversion methods'. - `grep -c '^\s*///'` returns: camera_provider.dart 6 of 1630 lines; telemetry_provider.dart 5 of 784; ride_record.dart 6 of 892; app_settings_provider.dart 6 of 476; recording_screen.dart 0 of 1878; ride_playback_screen.dart 0 of 2035. - app_settings_provider.dart:148 'static ResolutionPreset toResolutionPreset(int resolution) {' and camera_provider.dart:199 'static ResolutionPreset presetForResolution(int resolution) {' have the same body.
- **Verifier corrections:** The preset-duplication part overlaps F092 and should be deduplicated with it.
- **Recommendation:** Add dartdoc to the public members of CameraProvider, TelemetryProvider, RideRecord and AppSettingsProvider, and enable public_member_api_docs for those paths. Handle the preset duplication under F092.
- **Fix note:** Dartdoc added to the public members of CameraProvider, TelemetryProvider, RideRecord (and the other public types in ride_record.dart); AppSettingsProvider was already complete. Checked by reading lib, not edited. A heuristic scan found no undocumented public member or type in camera_provider.dart, telemetry_provider.dart, ride_record.dart or app_settings_provider.dart (the only hits were false positives on continuation lines). Spot check: every RideRecord field at ride_record.dart:122-168 has ///.

- **Failure scenario:** Maintainers and documentation treat the public API as the contract, and describe roll metrics, a 'paused' state or a samples-based duration fallback that nothing uses. One concrete hazard: a future screen calls PipService.instance.dispose() in its own dispose(). The next onPipModeChanged then throws 'Cannot add new events after calling close' inside the channel handler, and the PiP UI never updates again.

#### F153

**Permission-granted checks (5 copies) and elapsed-time formatting (4 copies, 2 formats) are duplicated across screens**

Severity: **info** · Category: maintainability · Location: `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:577` · Verification: single → confirmed · Status: **fixed**

Also affects: `lib/core/services/app_permission_service.dart:36`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:195`, `lib/features/camera/providers/camera_provider.dart:372`, `lib/features/history/rides_list_screen.dart:79`, `lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:1083`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1170`, `lib/features/recording/recording_screen.dart:1586`

- **What is wrong:** The `status.isGranted || status.isLimited` check exists in five places: app_permission_service.dart:36-38, camera_provider.dart:195-197, and inline at recording_screen.dart:577, 592 and 606-607. Requesting permissions is also split: AppPermissionService runs the audit, and CameraProvider.initializeCamera requests camera, microphone and notification again (camera_provider.dart:372-378). Elapsed-time formatting is duplicated four times in two formats. H:MM:SS appears in rides_list_screen.dart:79-84 and ride_playback_screen.dart:1083-1088, with identical bodies. HH:MM:SS appears inline at recording_screen.dart:1170-1175 and 1586-1591.
- **Failure scenario:** A permission rule change, such as treating provisional as granted on iOS or adding a permission, lands in one copy only. The preflight then shows 'Granted' while CameraProvider refuses to initialize, or the reverse. Riders already see the same ride as '00:05:00' while recording and '0:05:00' in history and playback.
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn "isGranted ||" lib` hits app_permission_service.dart:37, camera_provider.dart:196 and recording_screen.dart:577, 592 and 606. The _formatDuration bodies at rides_list_screen.dart:79-84 and ride_playback_screen.dart:1083-1088 are identical.
- **Recommendation:** Expose a single static isPermissionGranted(PermissionStatus) predicate from AppPermissionService and use it in all five places. Add one formatElapsed(Duration) helper under lib/core/utils and use it in all four duration-format sites.
- **Fix note:** shared isPermissionGranted and formatElapsed helpers

- Probe controllers are always disposed in a finally block (lib/features/history/ride_playback_screen.dart:511-528), and a single cross-segment seek leaves no controller behind (scratch P2: notDisposed=[] after leaving the screen).
- sampleForPosition's binary search and clamping are correct for sorted samples (lib/features/history/models/ride_record.dart:116-166). <details><summary>Performance & battery (16)</summary>

- Repository untouched: 'git status --porcelain' is empty at a33a583 after the scan. Benchmarks ran only in the scratchpad (bench/bin/bench.dart, bench2.dart and bench3.dart import the repo's integrity_utils.dart and telemetry_data.dart by file URI; dart compile exe; i7-1165G7).
- Startup: main.dart:49-65 creates the providers lazily, and RecordingScreen.initState (recording_screen.dart:64-71) does no directory scan, JSON parsing or hashing. The first heavy step is camera init (camera_provider.dart:315-494) after the permission audit. The only startup cost in scope is the se…
- Segment roll critical path: _rollSegmentIfNeeded restarts recording before registration (camera_provider.dart:1011-1029). Deferred maintenance returns right after an exists() check while the session is within the limit (1157-1170, 1193-1198), so a normal roll does no directory listing or JSON parsi…
- The retention sweep pre-fetches mtimes asynchronously before sorting (camera_provider.dart:1210-1218); its statSync problem is fixed. Only getRecordings still has it.
- Telemetry memory is bounded per ride: 1 sample/s in _activeRideSamples (telemetry_provider.dart:329-347), cleared at start, successful stop and cancel (318, 456, 467). The old _telemetryHistory leak is gone (a33a583).
- _captureSample recomputes the average with an O(n) fold (telemetry_provider.dart:353-357): ~36,000 additions per second at hour 10 of a ride, tens of microseconds, negligible.
- Telemetry UI notifications are throttled to one per 100 ms (telemetry_provider.dart:727-736). The speed and distance tiles use Selector and rebuild only when the displayed string changes (recording_screen.dart:1566-1617). The elapsed timer emits 1/s only while recording (camera_provider.dart:891-89…
- Roll metrics history is capped at 10 entries (camera_provider.dart:188-191). The per-roll notifyListeners (192) causes one whole-screen rebuild per segment (default every 300 s), which is negligible.
- Preview is not rendered when not needed in the main cases: blackout mode calls pausePreview (recording_screen.dart:454-475), and opening Ride History releases the camera (recording_screen.dart:1006; camera_provider.dart:1591-1619).
- Playback controllers are disposed correctly: the previous segment controller after handoff (ride_playback_screen.dart:641-645), the active one on dispose (1669-1681), and probe controllers in finally (525-527).
- Auto-advance retry after failed segments is bounded: after a completion only the plugin's pause/seekTo(duration) notifications (video_player.dart:630-636) can re-enter _advanceToNextSegment once _isSwitchingSegment is cleared, and an unchanged VideoPlayerValue does not re-notify, so at most a coupl…
- Device status polling every 15 s reads the sticky ACTION_BATTERY_CHANGED intent (recording_screen.dart:202-208; MainActivity.kt:242-277). Cheap.
- Wake lock: PARTIAL_WAKE_LOCK is acquired at ACTION_START and released at ACTION_STOP/onDestroy, with a 12 h timeout (BackgroundRecordingService.kt:106-130). This statement covers acquisition and release only, not whether the stop lifecycle is correct.
- Gallery export copies run on a background Thread with runOnUiThread replies (MainActivity.kt:171-235), not on the main thread.
- StaticMapViewer (used when a ride has fewer than two valid points) is a pure CustomPaint with no network access (static_map_viewer.dart:45, 123).
- Stop-path JSON work besides hashing is moderate: building the sample maps took 15 ms / 69 ms and jsonEncode 30 ms / 316 ms for 2 h / 10 h rides on the benchmark CPU. The canonical hash (87 / 639 ms) is noted in the stop-hashing finding.

</details>
